base64.ww

Base64

object Base64#

Bytes written as text in 64 characters (RFC 4648), A-Z, a-z, 0-9, + and /, with = to pad the text to a whole number of fours: how pictures are put inside a data: address, an e-mail or an SVG file.

val text = Base64.encode("hello".toBytes())    // "aGVsbG8="
val back = Base64.decode(text).decodeToString()  // "hello"
fun encode(data : Bytes) : string#

data as Base64 text, padded with = to a whole number of fours, on one line.

fun decode(text : string) : Bytes#

The bytes Base64 text stands for. Whitespace in it is passed over, so the lines MIME breaks it into every 76 characters are read as one; the padding = at the end may be left out. An IllegalArgumentException for a character Base64 has not, for = before the end, or for a length no bytes give (one character over a whole number of fours).

Hex (networking plan, part 2)

fun Bytes.toHex() : string#

The bytes as hex text, two lowercase digits each: Bytes([1, 171]).toHex() is "01ab", as a digest or key is written.

fun string.hexToBytes() : Bytes#

The bytes hex text stands for, two digits each, either case: "01AB".hexToBytes() is Bytes([1, 171]). An IllegalArgumentException for an odd count or a character that is no hex digit.

bigint.ww

Whole numbers of any size (2026-11-19)

A BigInt is a whole number with no limit to its size: what an int holds and past it, its arithmetic exact however long the numbers grow. It never changes; each operation gives a new one. Written in WinterWolf, its digits held as a list of ints of 30 bits each, lowest first, so that a digit times a digit, and what is carried, always fits in an int.

val big = BigInt.of(2).pow(100)            // 1267650600228229401496703205376
val n = "123456789012345678901234567890".toBigInt()
print(big * n + 7, big / 3, big % 1000, -big < n)

It mixes with ints on either side (5 + big, big * 2, 5 == BigInt.of(5)), and with floats, which make a float (a division by or of a float rounded once, from the exact quotient). divideToFloat divides two as a float, rounded once. / and % of two cut toward zero, as an int's do (-7 / 2 is -3, -7 % 2 is -1); floorDiv and floorMod round down, as Python's // and % do. The bitwise operators work as if the number were in two's complement with as many bits as it needs, a negative one's ones going on forever (as Python's and Java's do).

class BigInt#

A whole number of any size, exact; each operation gives a new one.

fun of(n : int) : BigInt#

The number n.

fun of(x : float) : BigInt#

The whole number x is, cut toward zero (2.7 makes 2, -2.7 makes -2), exactly however large; an ArithmeticException for an infinity or NaN.

fun parse(text : string, radix : int = 10) : BigInt#

The number text writes in base radix (2 to 36), a sign first if it has one, and underscores between digits allowed (1_000_000); a NumberFormatException for anything else.

fun parseOrNull(text : string, radix : int = 10) : BigInt?#

The same, null for text that is not one.

val zero : BigInt#

0.

val one : BigInt#

1.

val two : BigInt#

2.

val ten : BigInt#

10.

val signum : int#

-1, 0 or 1, as the number is below, at or above zero.

val isZero : bool#

Whether it is 0.

val bitLength : int#

The bits it needs, not counting a sign: 0 for 0, 1 for 1 and -1, 8 for 255 and -256 (as Java's bitLength: a negative number's in two's complement, its ones not counted).

op +(other : BigInt) : BigInt#

The sum.

op +(other : int) : BigInt#

The sum with an int.

op +(other : float) : float#

The sum with a float, as a float.

op -(other : BigInt) : BigInt#

The difference.

op -(other : int) : BigInt#

The difference with an int.

op -(other : float) : float#

The difference with a float, as a float.

op -() : BigInt#

The number with its sign turned round.

op *(other : BigInt) : BigInt#

The product.

op *(other : int) : BigInt#

The product with an int.

op *(other : float) : float#

The product with a float, as a float.

op /(other : BigInt) : BigInt#

Cut toward zero, as an int's; an ArithmeticException for 0.

op /(other : int) : BigInt#

Divided by an int, cut toward zero.

op /(other : float) : float#

Divided by a float, as the float nearest the exact quotient (rounded once).

op %(other : BigInt) : BigInt#

The rest, with the sign of the number divided, as an int's; an ArithmeticException for 0.

op %(other : int) : BigInt#

The rest after dividing by an int, with the sign of this number.

fun divRem(by : BigInt) : Pair<BigInt, BigInt>#

The quotient cut toward zero and the rest, in one division.

fun floorDiv(by : BigInt) : BigInt#

Divided and rounded down (toward minus infinity), as Python's //.

fun floorDiv(by : int) : BigInt#

floorDiv by an int.

fun floorMod(by : BigInt) : BigInt#

The rest after floorDiv, with the sign of by, as Python's %.

fun floorMod(by : int) : BigInt#

floorMod by an int.

fun abs() : BigInt#

Its size, the sign dropped.

fun pow(exponent : int) : BigInt#

To the power exponent, 0 or more (anything to the 0 is 1).

fun gcd(other : BigInt) : BigInt#

The greatest whole number dividing both, never negative; gcd(0, 0) is 0.

fun gcd(other : int) : BigInt#

gcd with an int.

fun modPow(exponent : BigInt, modulus : BigInt) : BigInt#

To the power exponent (0 or more), all modulo modulus (above 0): the result 0 or more and below it, worked out without the whole power.

fun modPow(exponent : int, modulus : BigInt) : BigInt#

modPow with an int exponent.

fun modInverse(modulus : BigInt) : BigInt#

The number that, times this, is 1 modulo modulus; an ArithmeticException where there is none (they share a divisor).

fun sqrt() : BigInt#

The whole part of its square root: the largest whole number whose square is not above it; an ArithmeticException for a negative number.

op <<(by : int) : BigInt#

Moved up by bits (times 2 to that power); a negative by moves it down.

op >>(by : int) : BigInt#

Moved down by bits, rounded down (toward minus infinity), as an int's >> is.

op &(other : BigInt) : BigInt#

The bits set in both, in two's complement.

op &(other : int) : BigInt#

& with an int.

op |(other : BigInt) : BigInt#

The bits set in either, in two's complement.

op |(other : int) : BigInt#

| with an int.

op ^(other : BigInt) : BigInt#

The bits set in one but not both, in two's complement.

op ^(other : int) : BigInt#

^ with an int.

op ~() : BigInt#

Every bit turned round: -this - 1.

fun testBit(n : int) : bool#

Whether bit n (0 the lowest) is set, in two's complement.

fun compareTo(other : BigInt) : int#

-1, 0 or 1, as it is below, equal to or above other.

fun compareTo(other : int) : int#

compareTo an int.

op <(other : BigInt) : bool#

Whether it is below other.

op <(other : int) : bool#

Whether it is below the int.

op <=(other : BigInt) : bool#

Whether it is below other or equal.

op <=(other : int) : bool#

Whether it is below the int or equal.

op >(other : BigInt) : bool#

Whether it is above other.

op >(other : int) : bool#

Whether it is above the int.

op >=(other : BigInt) : bool#

Whether it is above other or equal.

op >=(other : int) : bool#

Whether it is above the int or equal.

op ==(other : any?) : bool#

Equal to a BigInt, an int or a float of the same whole value (exactly: no float rounding).

fun hashCode() : int#

Equal to an int's where it is one, so that BigInt.of(5) and 5 find each other as keys.

fun toInt() : int#

As an int; an ArithmeticException where it does not fit.

fun toIntOrNull() : int?#

As an int, null where it does not fit.

val fitsInt : bool#

Whether it fits in an int.

fun toFloat() : float#

The nearest float, rounded as the processor rounds (to even where halfway); an infinity past the largest.

fun divideToFloat(divisor : BigInt) : float#

Divided by divisor, as the float nearest the exact quotient: rounded once, where toFloat() / divisor.toFloat() rounds three times, and is NaN where both are past the largest float. A subnormal quotient rounded at its own place, an infinity past the largest float, a zero with the quotient's sign (0 divided by a negative is -0.0). An ArithmeticException for a divisor of 0.

(BigInt.ten.pow(400) + 1).divideToFloat(BigInt.ten.pow(399))   // 10.0
fun toString(radix : int) : string#

In base radix, 2 to 36 (digits past 9 in small letters), a minus first where negative.

fun toString() : string#

In base 10, a minus first where negative.

fun int.toBigInt() : BigInt#

Into a BigInt.

fun float.toBigInt() : BigInt#

The whole number the float is, cut toward zero; an ArithmeticException for an infinity or NaN.

fun string.toBigInt(radix : int = 10) : BigInt#

The number the text writes in base radix; a NumberFormatException for anything else.

fun string.toBigIntOrNull(radix : int = 10) : BigInt?#

The number the text writes in base radix, null for anything else.

op int.+(other : BigInt) : BigInt#

An int or a float on the left of a BigInt.

op int.-(other : BigInt) : BigInt#

The int less a BigInt.

op int.*(other : BigInt) : BigInt#

The int times a BigInt.

op int./(other : BigInt) : BigInt#

The int divided by a BigInt, cut toward zero.

op int.%(other : BigInt) : BigInt#

The rest after dividing the int by a BigInt.

op int.<(other : BigInt) : bool#

Whether the int is below the BigInt.

op int.<=(other : BigInt) : bool#

Whether the int is below the BigInt or equal.

op int.>(other : BigInt) : bool#

Whether the int is above the BigInt.

op int.>=(other : BigInt) : bool#

Whether the int is above the BigInt or equal.

op float.+(other : BigInt) : float#

The float plus a BigInt, as a float.

op float.-(other : BigInt) : float#

The float less a BigInt, as a float.

op float.*(other : BigInt) : float#

The float times a BigInt, as a float.

op float./(other : BigInt) : float#

The float divided by a BigInt, as the float nearest the exact quotient (rounded once).

How it is worked out

Floats rounded once

bits.ww

Bits

What codecs read and write bytes with (M9/M10 part one, step 3): bits from any place in bytes, Huffman codes, and deflate both ways. The bit by bit work is C++; a codec's structure, its headers and blocks, is WinterWolf over them.

enum BitOrder#

The order bits are taken from a byte in: its lowest first, as deflate (PNG, ZIP), GIF and WebP's lossless form have them, or its highest first, as JPEG has them. A value of several bits is read the same way: lsbFirst, its first bit is its lowest.

class BitReader#

Reads bits from bytes, one value of up to 32 bits at a time, from any place.

val reader = BitReader(data, 2)          // from byte 2, lowest bits first
val last = reader.readBits(1)
val kind = reader.readBits(2)
val bytes : Bytes#

The bytes it reads.

val order : BitOrder#

The order it takes bits in.

var position : int#

Where the next bit is, counted in bits from the start of the bytes; it may be set, to read from another place.

val bitsLeft : int#

The bits after the position.

fun readBits(n : int) : int throws DataFormatException#

The next n bits (0 to 32) as a number, the position moved past them. A DataFormatException where they go past the end of the bytes.

fun peekBits(n : int) : int#

The next n bits (0 to 32), the position left where it is; zeros for bits past the end.

fun skipBits(n : int) throws DataFormatException#

The position moved past the next n bits. A DataFormatException where they go past the end.

fun alignToByte()#

The position moved on to the start of the next byte, if it is not at one.

class HuffmanTable#

A Huffman code, decoded a code at a time from a BitReader.

val codes = HuffmanTable([2, 1, 3, 3])   // symbol 1 is 0, symbol 0 is 10, 2 is 110, 3 is 111
val symbol = codes.decode(reader)
fun decode(reader : BitReader) : int throws DataFormatException#

The symbol of the next code reader has, its position moved past it. A DataFormatException where the bits there are no code of the table.

fun Bytes.inflate(raw : bool = false, limit : int? = null) : Bytes throws DataFormatException#

The bytes deflate-compressed data expands to: with zlib's header and check, as PNG holds it, or raw (deflate alone, as ZIP and gzip hold it). No more than limit bytes, so that a little data made to expand to a great deal is refused. A DataFormatException where the data is damaged, ends early, or expands past the limit (null, none).

val packed = "hello hello hello".toBytes().deflate()
print(packed.inflate().decodeToString())   // hello hello hello
fun Bytes.deflate(level : int = 6, raw : bool = false) : Bytes#

The bytes deflate-compressed, at level 0 (stored as they are, as fast as copying) to 9 (smallest, slowest), as zlib's levels are; 6 is zlib's own default. With zlib's header and check unless raw.

fun Bytes.crc32(crc : int = 0) : int#

The CRC-32 of the bytes, as PNG, ZIP and gzip have it, carried on from crc for data in parts.

object Gzip#

gzip (RFC 1952): what .gz files and HTTP's "Content-Encoding: gzip" hold, deflate with a header and a CRC-32 after it.

val packed = Gzip.compress(FilePath("log.txt").readBytes())
FilePath("log.txt.gz").writeBytes(packed)
val back = Gzip.decompress(packed)
fun compress(data : Bytes, level : int = 6) : Bytes#

The bytes as one gzip member, deflated at level (0 to 9, as Bytes.deflate).

fun decompress(data : Bytes, limit : int? = null) : Bytes throws DataFormatException#

What gzip data holds: one member, or several one after another (as cat a.gz b.gz makes), joined; its header's name, comment and extra fields passed over. No more than limit bytes. A DataFormatException where it is not gzip, is damaged (its CRC-32 or size not right), or expands past the limit.

fun Bytes.adler32(adler : int = 1) : int#

The Adler-32 of the bytes, as zlib has it, carried on from adler for data in parts.

bmp.ww

BMP

builtins.ww

Built-in functions and types

The functions every program has, and the methods and properties of the built-in types: strings, numbers, lists, maps, sets, ranges and bytes. Where one name takes different shapes (add(item) and add(index, item)), each is listed, and a call means the one its arguments fit.

Functions

fun print(vararg items : any?)#

Writes items to the output as text, a space between each two, and then a line end. A string is written as it is; in a list or a map it is shown in quotes.

print("total", 3, [1, 2])   // total 3 [1, 2]
fun printError(vararg items : any?)#

Writes items to standard error as print writes them to the output, and then a line end: for what went wrong, which a program's output, sent on to a file or another program, should not carry. What print wrote before it goes out first.

printError("cannot read", path)
fun write(vararg items : any?)#

Writes items to the output as print does, but with nothing between them and no line end.

fun Range(first : int, end : int = 0, step : int = 1) : Range#

The ints from first up to end, not including end, going by step; with one argument, from 0 up to that number. A negative step counts down. An IllegalArgumentException for a step of 0.

Range(5).toList()          // [0, 1, 2, 3, 4]
Range(10, 0, -3).toList()  // [10, 7, 4, 1]
fun Bytes(source : any) : Bytes#

New bytes: Bytes(16) is sixteen zeros, Bytes([1, 2, 255]) the numbers of a list, and Bytes(other) a copy of other bytes. An IllegalArgumentException for a negative size, a number outside 0 to 255, or anything else; text becomes bytes with toBytes().

fun hash(vararg values : any?) : int#

The hash of several values together, wrapping at 32 bits inside, as Java's Objects.hash: hash(1, 2) is 994, as there.

fun clock() : float#

A time in seconds, as a float, from a clock that only goes forward: the difference between two readings is the time that passed. What it counts from is not fixed.

fun compareValues(a : any?, b : any?) : int#

How a and b are ordered: negative, zero or positive, as a comparator gives. Numbers by size, strings by their characters, chars by their numbers, false before true, and null before everything else. A ClassCastException for two values that cannot be compared.

fun exit(code : int = 0) : nothing#

Ends the program at once, with code as its exit status; finally blocks do not run.

fun flush()#

Sends on at once whatever print and write have written. Output to a file or to another program is otherwise sent in pieces, and at the latest when the program ends.

fun input(prompt : string = "") : string?#

Writes prompt, then reads one line of input and gives it without its line end; null when the input has ended. Other threads go on while it waits.

fun <T> maxOf(first : T, vararg rest : T) : T#

The largest of the values given, compared as compareValues does; the first of them where several are as large.

maxOf(3, 7, 2)   // 7
fun <T> minOf(first : T, vararg rest : T) : T#

The smallest of the values given, compared as compareValues does; the first of them where several are as small.

string

val string.size : int#

The number of characters.

val string.lastIndex : int#

The position of the last character, size - 1: -1 for "".

val string.indices : Range#

The positions of the characters, 0..<size.

fun string.upper() : string#

The text in upper case as Kotlin's uppercase() gives it: Unicode's full mappings, so "Straße" is "STRASSE" and "ﬁ" is "FI", the same on every system.

fun string.lower() : string#

The text in lower case as Kotlin's lowercase() gives it: Unicode's full mappings ("İ" is "i̇"), and a Σ that ends a word as ς: "ΟΔΟΣ" is "οδος".

fun string.trim() : string#

The text without the whitespace at its start and at its end: whitespace as Kotlin's Char.isWhitespace has it, the spaces of every script (the no-break ones too), tabs and line ends, the same on every system.

fun string.trimStart() : string#

The text without the whitespace (as trim's) at its start.

fun string.trimEnd() : string#

The text without the whitespace (as trim's) at its end.

fun string.trimIndent() : string#

For text written over several lines in the source: the indentation shared by all the lines with something on them taken away, and a first and a last line that are blank dropped.

fun string.trimMargin(marginPrefix : string = "|") : string#

For text written over several lines in the source: on each line, the spaces and tabs up to marginPrefix and the prefix itself taken away, and a first and a last line that are blank dropped. A line without the prefix is kept as it is.

fun string.split() : open List<string>#

The words of the text: the parts between runs of whitespace (as trim's), none of them empty, as a new list.

" a  b ".split()      // ["a", "b"]
fun string.split(delimiter : string) : open List<string>#

The parts of the text between the places where delimiter is, as a new list; an empty delimiter gives each character.

"a,b,,c".split(",")   // ["a", "b", "", "c"]
fun string.split(delimiter : char) : open List<string>#

The parts of the text between the places where the character delimiter is, as a new list.

fun string.lines() : open List<string>#

The lines of the text without their line ends ("\r\n", "\n", or "\r" alone, as Kotlin's), as a new list. Text that ends in a line end gives an empty last line.

fun string.repeat(times : int) : string#

The text times times over; "" for 0. An IllegalArgumentException for a negative times.

fun string.reversed() : string#

The characters in the opposite order.

fun string.contains(other : string, ignoreCase : bool = false) : bool#

True when other is somewhere in the text; always true for "".

fun string.contains(other : char, ignoreCase : bool = false) : bool#

True when the character other is in the text.

fun string.startsWith(prefix : string, ignoreCase : bool = false) : bool#

True when the text begins with prefix.

fun string.startsWith(prefix : char, ignoreCase : bool = false) : bool#

True when the text begins with the character prefix.

fun string.endsWith(suffix : string, ignoreCase : bool = false) : bool#

True when the text ends with suffix.

fun string.endsWith(suffix : char, ignoreCase : bool = false) : bool#

True when the text ends with the character suffix.

fun string.indexOf(other : string, startIndex : int = 0, ignoreCase : bool = false) : int#

The position where other first appears at or after startIndex, or -1 where it does not. A negative startIndex is taken as 0.

fun string.indexOf(other : char, startIndex : int = 0, ignoreCase : bool = false) : int#

The position where the character other first appears at or after startIndex, or -1.

fun string.lastIndexOf(other : string, startIndex : int? = null, ignoreCase : bool = false) : int#

The position where other last appears, beginning at or before startIndex, or -1 where it does not. Without startIndex (or with null) the whole text is searched, from its end; a negative one gives -1.

fun string.lastIndexOf(other : char, startIndex : int? = null, ignoreCase : bool = false) : int#

The position where the character other last appears, at or before startIndex where one is given, or -1.

fun string.replace(oldValue : string, newValue : string, ignoreCase : bool = false) : string#

The text with every oldValue in it replaced by newValue; the text as it is when oldValue is "".

fun string.replace(oldChar : char, newChar : char, ignoreCase : bool = false) : string#

The text with every oldChar in it replaced by newChar.

fun string.equals(other : any?, ignoreCase : bool = false) : bool#

True when other is a string with the same characters; false for anything that is not a string.

fun string.compareTo(other : string, ignoreCase : bool = false) : int#

How the text is ordered against other, character by character by their numbers: negative, zero or positive. "B" comes before "a" unless ignoreCase.

fun string.isEmpty() : bool#

True when the text has no characters.

fun string.isNotEmpty() : bool#

True when the text has at least one character.

fun string.isBlank() : bool#

True when the text is empty or only spaces, tabs and line ends.

fun string.isNotBlank() : bool#

True when the text has something besides spaces, tabs and line ends.

fun string.isAlpha() : bool#

What the text is made of, as Python's str asks it; a character's own are isLetter() and the like. True when every character is a letter; false for "".

fun string.isDigit() : bool#

True when every character is a digit; false for "".

fun string.isAlnum() : bool#

True when every character is a letter or a digit; false for "".

fun string.isSpace() : bool#

True when every character is whitespace: a space, a tab, a line end; false for "".

fun string.isUpper() : bool#

True when the text has a letter that has a case and every such letter is upper case, as in Python: "A1" and "HELLO WORLD" are, "Hello", "123" and "" are not.

fun string.isLower() : bool#

True when the text has a letter that has a case and every such letter is lower case, as in Python: "a1" and "hello world" are, "Hello", "123" and "" are not.

fun string.isTitle() : bool#

True when each run of letters that have a case starts upper case (or title case, as ǅ) and goes on lower case, and there is one at least, as Python's istitle: "Hello World" and "A1b" are, "HeLLo", "hello" and "" are not.

fun string.foldCase() : string#

The text with its case folded, for comparing without case, as Python's casefold: Unicode's full case folding, so "Straße" and "STRASSE" both fold to "strasse" and "ς" folds as "σ". Not for showing: some folds are not lower case (Cherokee's fold to its capitals).

fun string.title() : string#

The text in title case as Python's title(): the first letter of each run of letters that have a case in title case, the rest lower case ("hello wORLD" is "Hello World", "ǆemal" is "ǅemal", "ß" is "Ss"). A word is a run of cased letters, so "they're" is "They'Re", as in Python.

fun string.capitalize() : string#

The first character in title case and the rest in lower case, as Python's capitalize(): "hELLO wORLD" is "Hello world". (Kotlin's old capitalize() changed only the first.)

fun string.swapCase() : string#

The text with upper case lowered and lower case raised, by the full mappings, as Python's swapcase(): "Hello ß" is "hELLO SS"; what has no case stays.

fun string.padStart(size : int, padChar : char = ' ') : string#

The text made size characters long with padChar put before it; the text as it is when it is that long already.

"7".padStart(3, '0')   // "007"
fun string.padEnd(size : int, padChar : char = ' ') : string#

The text made size characters long with padChar put after it; the text as it is when it is that long already.

fun string.toInt(radix : int = 10) : int#

The whole number the text spells in base radix, a sign allowed, spaces not. A NumberFormatException for text that is not one or does not fit in an int; an IllegalArgumentException for a radix outside 2 to 36.

"ff".toInt(16)   // 255
fun string.toIntOrNull(radix : int = 10) : int?#

The whole number the text spells in base radix, or null where it is not one.

fun string.parseUnsigned(radix : int = 10) : int#

The whole number from 0 to 18446744073709551615 the text spells, as an int's 64 bits read as unsigned: "18446744073709551615" gives -1. A NumberFormatException for anything else.

fun string.toFloat() : float#

The number the text spells, such as "1.5", "-2" or "1e3"; a NumberFormatException for text that is not one, spaces around it included.

fun string.toFloatOrNull() : float?#

The number the text spells, or null where it is not one.

fun string.format(vararg values : any?) : string#

The text with each % item in it replaced by the next of values, as Kotlin's String.format: %[n$][flags][width][.precision]kind. The kinds are d (a whole number), x X o (in hex or octal), f e E g G (a fraction), s S (anything, as print shows it), c (a character), b (true or false), %% and %n; the flags - (to the left), 0, +, a space, and , (thousands). A fraction is rounded from its exact binary value, a half to even, as C's printf rounds it: "%.1f" of 0.35 is 0.3 (0.35 is 0.34999... in binary) and "%.0f" of 2.5 is 2. %g keeps its zeros ("%.4g" of 1.0 is 1.000), as Kotlin writes them. An IllegalArgumentException for a bad item or too few values.

"%d items, %.2f each".format(3, 1.5)   // "3 items, 1.50 each"
fun string.toBytes(charset : Charset = Charset.utf8) : Bytes#

The text as bytes, in UTF-8 or the character set given.

List

val List.size : int#

The number of items.

val List.lastIndex : int#

The position of the last item, size - 1: -1 for an empty list.

val List.indices : Range#

The positions of the items, 0..<size.

val List.itemType : Class#

The type of item the list was made to hold, as a Type.

fun <T> open List<T>.add(item : T) : bool#

Adds item at the end; gives true.

fun <T> open List<T>.add(index : int, item : T) : bool#

Puts item in before the item at index, from 0 to size (the end); gives true. An IndexOutOfBoundsException for any other index.

fun <T> open List<T>.addAll(items : any) : bool#

Adds items at the end; true if there were any. items is anything a for loop takes.

fun <T> open List<T>.addAll(index : int, items : any) : bool#

Puts items in before the item at index, from 0 to size; true if there were any. An IndexOutOfBoundsException for any other index.

fun <T> List<T>.get(index : int) : T#

The item at index, from 0, as list[index] gives it for an int (one counted from the end is written list[^k], D146). An IndexOutOfBoundsException where there is none.

fun <T> List<T>.getOrNull(index : int) : T?#

The item at index, from 0, or null where there is none, as for a negative index.

fun <T> open List<T>.set(index : int, item : T) : T#

Puts item at index, from 0, in place of the item there, and gives the item that was there. An IndexOutOfBoundsException where there is none.

fun <T> open List<T>.removeAt(index : Index) : T#

Takes out the item at index and gives it; ^1 is the last. An IndexOutOfBoundsException where there is none, as for a negative int.

fun <T> open List<T>.remove(item : any?) : bool#

Takes out the first item equal to item, compared with ==; true if there was one.

fun <T> open List<T>.removeFirst() : T#

Takes out the first item and gives it; a NoSuchElementException when the list is empty.

fun <T> open List<T>.removeLast() : T#

Takes out the last item and gives it; a NoSuchElementException when the list is empty.

fun <T> open List<T>.removeFirstOrNull() : T?#

Takes out the first item and gives it; null when the list is empty.

fun <T> open List<T>.removeLastOrNull() : T?#

Takes out the last item and gives it; null when the list is empty.

fun <T> open List<T>.clear()#

Takes out every item.

fun <T> open List<T>.ensureCapacity(capacity : int)#

Makes room for capacity items in all, adding none: adding up to that many then takes no new room as it goes, and a loop adding them (to.add(from[i])) is done at once where native code runs it. An IllegalArgumentException for a negative capacity.

fun <T> List<T>.contains(item : any?) : bool#

True when an item is equal to item, compared with ==.

fun <T> List<T>.indexOf(item : any?) : int#

The position of the first item equal to item, or -1 where there is none.

fun <T> List<T>.lastIndexOf(item : any?) : int#

The position of the last item equal to item, or -1 where there is none.

fun <T> List<T>.isEmpty() : bool#

True when the list has no items.

fun <T> List<T>.isNotEmpty() : bool#

True when the list has at least one item.

fun <T> List<T>.toList() : open List<T>#

A new list with the same items, made to hold the same type; the items themselves are not copied.

fun <T> List<T>.toOpenList() : open List<T>#

A new list with the same items, made to hold the same type, as toList().

fun <T> List<T>.frozen() : FrozenList<T>#

A copy nobody may change, the lists, maps and sets in it copied so as well: what can be a key.

fun <T> List<T>.reversed() : open List<T>#

A new list with the items in the opposite order; reverse() turns the list itself round.

fun <T> open List<T>.reverse()#

Turns the list round, in place: the last item first.

fun <T> open List<T>.sort(comparator : ((T, T) -> Number)? = null)#

Sorts the list in place, keeping items that are equal in their order. Without comparator, numbers by size, strings by their characters, chars by their numbers, and objects by their compareTo; a ClassCastException for items that cannot be compared. comparator(a, b) gives a negative number where a goes first, 0 where they are equal, and a positive number otherwise.

names.sort { a, b -> a.size - b.size }
fun <T> open List<T>.sortBy(selector : (T) -> any?)#

Sorts the list in place by what selector gives for each item, smallest first, keys compared as compareValues does; items with equal keys stay in their order.

fun <T> open List<T>.sortByDescending(selector : (T) -> any?)#

Sorts the list in place by what selector gives for each item, largest first; items with equal keys stay in their order.

fun <T> open List<T>.replaceAll(transform : (T) -> T)#

Replaces each item, in place, by what transform gives for it.

fun <T> List<T>.join(separator : string = ", ", prefix : string = "", postfix : string = "", limit : int = -1, truncated : string = "...", transform : ((T) -> any?)? = null) : string#

The items as text: names.join(", ") { it.upper() } (Kotlin's joinToString). separator between the items, prefix and postfix around them, and where limit is 0 or more, at most that many items followed by truncated. Each item is shown as transform makes it, or as print shows it.

[1, 2, 3, 4].join(limit = 2)   // "1, 2, ..."
fun <T> open List<T>.fill(item : T, from : int = 0, to : int = -1)#

Puts item in the place of every item, or of those from from up to to (Kotlin's fill(element, fromIndex, toIndex)); an IndexOutOfBoundsException for a range outside the list.

Set

fun <T> Set(from : any? = null) : open Set<T>#

Each value once, in the order first added, two values the same one where a map would take them as one key. Set() or Set(from): a list's items, a range's, a string's, another set's.

val Set.size : int#

The number of items.

val Set.itemType : Class#

The type of item the set was made to hold, as a Type: any where that was not said.

fun <T> open Set<T>.add(item : T) : bool#

Adds item where it is not there yet; true if it was not.

fun <T> open Set<T>.remove(item : T) : bool#

Takes out item; true if it was there.

fun <T> Set<T>.contains(item : T) : bool#

True when item is in the set.

fun <T> Set<T>.isEmpty() : bool#

True when the set has no items.

fun <T> Set<T>.isNotEmpty() : bool#

True when the set has at least one item.

fun <T> Set<T>.frozen() : FrozenSet<T>#

A copy nobody may change, the lists, maps and sets in it copied so as well.

fun <T> open Set<T>.clear()#

Takes out every item.

fun <T> open Set<T>.addAll(from : any) : bool#

Adds each item of from (a list, a set, a range, a string, bytes or a map's entries) that is not there yet; true if any was added.

fun <T> open Set<T>.removeAll(from : List<T>) : bool#

Takes out each item that is in the list from; true if the set changed.

fun <T> open Set<T>.removeAll(from : Set<T>) : bool#

Takes out each item that is in the set from; true if the set changed.

fun <T> open Set<T>.removeAll(predicate : (T) -> bool) : bool#

Takes out each item for which predicate is true; true if the set changed.

fun <T> open Set<T>.retainAll(from : List<T>) : bool#

Keeps only the items that are in the list from; true if the set changed.

fun <T> open Set<T>.retainAll(from : Set<T>) : bool#

Keeps only the items that are in the set from; true if the set changed.

fun <T> open Set<T>.retainAll(predicate : (T) -> bool) : bool#

Keeps only the items for which predicate is true; true if the set changed.

fun <T> Set<T>.toList() : open List<T>#

The items, in the order they were added, as a new list.

fun <T> Set<T>.first() : T#

The item added first; a NoSuchElementException when the set is empty.

fun <T> Set<T>.last() : T#

The item added last; a NoSuchElementException when the set is empty.

Map

val Map.size : int#

The number of keys.

val <K, V> Map<K, V>.keys : Set<K>#

The keys, in the order they were first put in: a live view, a set that shows the map as it is, and through which nothing is changed.

val <K, V> Map<K, V>.values : Values<V>#

The values, in the order of their keys: a live view, a Values that shows the map as it is and through which nothing is changed. They have no positions: toList() for a list of them.

val <K, V> Map<K, V>.entries : Set<MapEntry<K, V>>#

The entries, each a MapEntry with its key and value: a live view, as keys is.

val Map.keyType : Class#

The type of key the map was made to hold, as a Type: any where that was not said.

val Map.valueType : Class#

The type of value the map was made to hold, as a Type: any where that was not said.

fun <K, V> Map<K, V>.contains(entry : MapEntry<K, V>) : bool#

A whole entry, "a" to 1 or a MapEntry; a key is asked of the keys, k in map.keys.

fun <K, V> Map<K, V>.contains(entry : Pair<K, V>) : bool#

True when the map holds the key of the pair entry with its value: map.contains("a" to 1).

fun <K, V> Map<K, V>.get(key : K, defaultValue : V? = null) : V?#

The value under key; where there is none, defaultValue, or null.

fun <K, V> Map<K, V>.getValue(key : K) : V#

The value under key; a NoSuchElementException where there is none.

fun <K, V> Map<K, V>.getOrDefault(key : K, defaultValue : V) : V#

The value under key, or defaultValue where there is none.

fun <K, V> open Map<K, V>.put(key : K, value : V) : V?#

Puts value under key, and gives the value that was there, or null.

fun <K, V> open Map<K, V>.set(key : K, value : V)#

Puts value under key, as map[key] = value does.

fun <K, V> open Map<K, V>.remove(key : any?) : V?#

Takes out key and its value, and gives the value that was there, or null.

fun <K, V> open Map<K, V>.clear()#

Takes out every key.

fun <K, V> Map<K, V>.isEmpty() : bool#

True when the map has no keys.

fun <K, V> Map<K, V>.isNotEmpty() : bool#

True when the map has at least one key.

fun <K, V> Map<K, V>.toMap() : open Map<K, V>#

A new map with the same keys and values, in the same order.

fun <K, V> Map<K, V>.toOpenMap() : open Map<K, V>#

A new map with the same keys and values, as toMap().

fun <K, V> Map<K, V>.frozen() : FrozenMap<K, V>#

A copy nobody may change, the lists, maps and sets in it copied so as well: what can be a key.

Range

val Range.size : int#

How many numbers the range holds. An IllegalArgumentException for a range with an end counted from the end (2..^1), until of(size) makes it plain.

val Range.start : int#

The number the range starts at; an Index for one counted from the end.

val Range.end : int#

The end as written: included for 0..10 and 10 downTo 0, not for 0..<10 and Range(0, 10); an Index for one counted from the end.

val Range.step : int#

How far apart the numbers are: negative for a range that counts down.

infix fun Range.step(by : int) : Range#

0..10 step 2, 10 downTo 0 step 3: every so many of a range's numbers, from its first.

fun Range.toList() : open List<int>#

The numbers of the range, as a new list.

fun Range.contains(value : any?) : bool#

True when value is an int the range holds; false for anything else, a float among them.

fun Range.first() : int#

The first number; a NoSuchElementException for an empty range.

fun Range.last() : int#

The last number the range holds: 8 for 0..9 step 4. A NoSuchElementException for an empty range.

fun Range.isEmpty() : bool#

True when the range holds no numbers, as Range(3, 1) does.

Bytes

val Bytes.size : int#

The number of bytes.

fun Bytes.toList() : open List<byte>#

The bytes, as a new list.

fun Bytes.decodeToString(charset : Charset = Charset.utf8) : string#

The text these bytes spell, in UTF-8 or the character set given. Bytes that are not UTF-8 become the character U+FFFD.

fun Bytes.fill(value : byte) : Bytes#

Puts value in every place, and gives the bytes themselves.

fun Bytes.indexOf(value : byte, startIndex : int = 0) : int#

The position of the first byte equal to value at or after startIndex, or -1 where there is none; a negative startIndex is taken as 0, as a string's indexOf takes it.

fun Bytes.indicesOf(value : byte, startIndex : int = 0, endIndex : int = size) : List<int>#

The positions of every byte equal to value from startIndex up to endIndex (not included), in order: the line ends of a buffer, say, found in one call rather than a call for each. Positions outside the bytes are taken as their ends.

fun Bytes.contains(value : any?) : bool#

True when a byte equal to value is there, given as a byte or an int; false for anything else.

fun Bytes.mapped(table : Bytes, width : int = 1) : Bytes#

New bytes with each byte b made table[b]: table is 256 bytes, one for each value a byte has. Bytes made letters (Base64), letters made their values, one character set's codes made another's. With a width, each byte made width bytes from table[b * width] on, the table 256 times as long: a palette's indices made colours, four bytes each. An IllegalArgumentException for a table of another size.

indices.mapped(palette, 4)   // RGBA for each index, palette holding 256 colours
fun Bytes.mapInPlace(table : Bytes) : Bytes#

Each byte b made table[b] where it is, as mapped does but with no new bytes made; gives the bytes themselves. An IllegalArgumentException for a table of another size than 256.

fun Bytes.unpackBits(width : int) : Bytes#

The bits in groups of width (1 to 8), the most significant first, each group a byte of its own: 6 for Base64's letters, 1, 2 or 4 for the pixels of a picture of few colours. A last group the bits do not fill is filled with zeros. An IllegalArgumentException for another width.

Bytes([0b10110100]).unpackBits(2)   // [2, 3, 1, 0]
fun Bytes.packBits(width : int, startIndex : int = 0, endIndex : int = size) : Bytes#

The reverse of unpackBits: the low width bits (1 to 8) of each byte from startIndex up to endIndex, the first the most significant, joined into whole bytes; bits over that make no whole byte are dropped.

Bytes([2, 3, 1, 0]).packBits(2)     // [0b10110100]
fun Bytes.without(value : byte) : Bytes#

New bytes with every byte equal to value taken out: the line breaks of text, say.

fun Bytes.copyFrom(source : Bytes, sourceIndex : int, sourceStep : int, index : int, step : int, count : int) : Bytes#

Copies count bytes of source in, from sourceIndex on a step of sourceStep (0, the same byte each time) to index on a step of step; gives the bytes themselves. Pixels' channels moved at once: blue, green, red rows into red, green, blue, alpha, a plane into pixels, one value into every fourth byte. source may be the bytes themselves where the parts do not overlap. An IndexOutOfBoundsException where either side would go outside its bytes.

rgba.copyFrom(bgr, 2, 3, 0, 4, pixels)          // the reds, from every third byte to every fourth
rgba.copyFrom(Bytes([255]), 0, 0, 3, 4, pixels)  // every alpha 255
fun Bytes.int16(at : int, bigEndian : bool = false) : int#

16-, 32- and 64-bit numbers kept in the bytes, at a position, the least significant byte first unless bigEndian; read as an int, a float for float32 and float64. int16 reads the signed 16-bit number at at, from -32768 to 32767. Each of these reads and writes is an IndexOutOfBoundsException where the number would run past the end.

fun Bytes.uint16(at : int, bigEndian : bool = false) : int#

The unsigned 16-bit number at at, from 0 to 65535.

fun Bytes.int32(at : int, bigEndian : bool = false) : int#

The signed 32-bit number at at, from -2147483648 to 2147483647.

fun Bytes.uint32(at : int, bigEndian : bool = false) : int#

The unsigned 32-bit number at at, from 0 to 4294967295.

fun Bytes.float32(at : int, bigEndian : bool = false) : float#

The 32-bit float at at.

fun Bytes.int64(at : int, bigEndian : bool = false) : int#

The signed 64-bit number at at: any int. (An unsigned one past 2^63 reads as negative, as in Kotlin's Long.)

fun Bytes.float64(at : int, bigEndian : bool = false) : float#

The 64-bit float at at.

fun Bytes.putInt16(at : int, value : int, bigEndian : bool = false) : Bytes#

Writes value as a signed 16-bit number at at, and gives the bytes themselves. An ArithmeticException where it is outside -32768 to 32767.

fun Bytes.putUint16(at : int, value : int, bigEndian : bool = false) : Bytes#

Writes value as an unsigned 16-bit number at at, and gives the bytes themselves. An ArithmeticException where it is outside 0 to 65535.

fun Bytes.putInt32(at : int, value : int, bigEndian : bool = false) : Bytes#

Writes value as a signed 32-bit number at at, and gives the bytes themselves. An ArithmeticException where it is outside -2147483648 to 2147483647.

fun Bytes.putUint32(at : int, value : int, bigEndian : bool = false) : Bytes#

Writes value as an unsigned 32-bit number at at, and gives the bytes themselves. An ArithmeticException where it is outside 0 to 4294967295.

fun Bytes.putFloat32(at : int, value : Number, bigEndian : bool = false) : Bytes#

Writes value as a 32-bit float at at, as near as one can hold it, and gives the bytes themselves. A NaN is written as the one quiet NaN, 0x7FC00000, whatever made it.

fun Bytes.putInt64(at : int, value : int, bigEndian : bool = false) : Bytes#

Writes value as a signed 64-bit number at at, and gives the bytes themselves.

fun Bytes.putFloat64(at : int, value : Number, bigEndian : bool = false) : Bytes#

Writes value as a 64-bit float at at, and gives the bytes themselves. A NaN is written as the one quiet NaN, 0x7FF8000000000000, whatever made it: x86 and ARM make NaNs of other bits.

Positions

fun Index.of(size : int) : int#

A position: an int from the start, or ^k from the end (items[^1] is the last). A function taking an Index takes either; of(size) is the position it means in that many items.

val Index.fromEnd : bool#

True for a position counted from the end, ^k; false for an int.

val Index.value : int#

The number written: k for ^k, and the int itself for an int.

fun Range.of(size : int) : Range#

A range with an end counted from the end (2..^1) is a slice's bounds; of(size) makes it plain.

char and byte

val char.code : int#

A char is one character, a Unicode code point: what s[i] gives. c.code is its number, and n.char the character numbered n.

val int.char : char#

The character numbered by the int: 97.char is 'a'. An IllegalArgumentException for a number that is no character's, outside 0 to 1114111 or from 55296 to 57343.

fun char.isLetter() : bool#

What a character is, as Java's Character methods answer, from WinterWolf's own Unicode data (Unicode 16.0, the same on every system). True when the character is a letter (category L: Lu, Ll, Lt, Lm or Lo).

fun char.isDigit() : bool#

True when the character is a decimal digit (category Nd: '7', '٣'; not '²' or 'Ⅻ').

fun char.isLetterOrDigit() : bool#

True when the character is a letter or a decimal digit.

fun char.isWhitespace() : bool#

True when the character is whitespace as Java has it: a space separator but the no-break ones (U+00A0, U+2007, U+202F), a line or paragraph separator, \t to \r, or \x1C to \x1F.

fun char.isUpperCase() : bool#

True when the character is upper case (Unicode's Uppercase: Lu, and Ⓐ and the like).

fun char.isLowerCase() : bool#

True when the character is lower case (Unicode's Lowercase: Ll, and ª, º and the like).

fun char.upper() : char#

The character in upper case, one for one as Java's Character.toUpperCase gives it: 'ǅ' is 'Ǆ'; the char itself where it has none, as 'ß' has none of one character.

fun char.lower() : char#

The character in lower case, one for one as Java's Character.toLowerCase gives it.

fun char.title() : char#

The character in title case, one for one as Java's Character.toTitleCase gives it: 'ǆ' is 'ǅ', and a letter with no title case of its own its upper case ('a' is 'A').

fun char.isTitleCase() : bool#

True when the character is title case (Lt: ǅ, ǈ, ᾈ and the like), as Kotlin's isTitleCase.

fun char.isCased() : bool#

True when the character has a case: upper, lower or title case (Unicode's Cased).

fun char.isCaseIgnorable() : bool#

True when the character is passed over in looking for a word's case (Unicode's Case_Ignorable: ' . : ^ and the combining marks), as the final sigma rule and Python's title() pass it.

fun char.digitToInt(radix : int = 10) : int#

The number the digit stands for in base radix: '7' is 7, and in base 16, 'f' is 15. A decimal digit of any script counts ('٣', Arabic-Indic three, is 3), and so do the fullwidth letters, as Kotlin's digitToInt. A NumberFormatException for a char that is not a digit there; an IllegalArgumentException for a radix outside 2 to 36.

fun byte.toInt() : int#

A byte holds 0 to 255; arithmetic on bytes gives an int. toInt() gives the number the byte holds, as an int.

fun int.toByte() : byte#

The int as a byte; an IllegalArgumentException where it is outside 0 to 255.

fun int.toByteWrapped() : byte#

The low 8 bits of the int as a byte: 300 gives 44, and -1 gives 255.

int and float

fun int.coerceIn(minimumValue : int, maximumValue : int) : int#

The coerce functions give one of the numbers as it was: an int where all of them are ints, a float where all are floats, and either where they are mixed (2.5.coerceIn(0, 1) is 1). coerceIn gives the number itself where it lies from minimumValue to maximumValue, and otherwise the nearer of the two; an IllegalArgumentException where the maximum is the smaller.

fun int.coerceIn(range : Range) : int#

The number itself where the range holds it, and otherwise the range's smallest or largest number, whichever is nearer. An IllegalArgumentException for an empty range.

fun int.coerceIn(minimumValue : Number, maximumValue : Number) : Number#

The number itself where it lies from minimumValue to maximumValue, and otherwise the nearer of the two, as it was given.

fun int.coerceAtLeast(minimumValue : int) : int#

The number itself, or minimumValue where the number is smaller.

fun int.coerceAtLeast(minimumValue : Number) : Number#

The number itself, or minimumValue, as it was given, where the number is smaller.

fun int.coerceAtMost(maximumValue : int) : int#

The number itself, or maximumValue where the number is larger.

fun int.coerceAtMost(maximumValue : Number) : Number#

The number itself, or maximumValue, as it was given, where the number is larger.

fun int.pow(exponent : Number) : Number#

The int to the power exponent: an int for an int exponent of 0 or more, and otherwise a float. An ArithmeticException where the int would not fit.

2.pow(10)   // 1024
2.pow(-1)   // 0.5
fun int.mod(divisor : int) : int#

The remainder after dividing by divisor, with the divisor's sign: (-7).mod(3) is 2, where -7 % 3 is -1. An ArithmeticException for a divisor of 0.

fun float.coerceIn(minimumValue : float, maximumValue : float) : float#

The number itself where it lies from minimumValue to maximumValue, and otherwise the nearer of the two; an IllegalArgumentException where the maximum is the smaller.

fun float.coerceIn(minimumValue : Number, maximumValue : Number) : Number#

The number itself where it lies from minimumValue to maximumValue, and otherwise the nearer of the two, as it was given.

fun float.coerceIn(range : Range) : Number#

The number itself where it lies within the range's smallest and largest numbers, and otherwise the nearer of those, an int. An IllegalArgumentException for an empty range.

fun float.coerceAtLeast(minimumValue : float) : float#

The number itself, or minimumValue where the number is smaller.

fun float.coerceAtLeast(minimumValue : Number) : Number#

The number itself, or minimumValue, as it was given, where the number is smaller.

fun float.coerceAtMost(maximumValue : float) : float#

The number itself, or maximumValue where the number is larger.

fun float.coerceAtMost(maximumValue : Number) : Number#

The number itself, or maximumValue, as it was given, where the number is larger.

fun float.pow(exponent : Number) : float#

The number to the power exponent.

fun float.mod(divisor : Number) : float#

The remainder after dividing by divisor, with the divisor's sign: (-7.5).mod(2) is 0.5. NaN for a divisor of 0.

fun float.toBits() : int#

The float's 64 bits as an int (IEEE 754's binary64: the sign, 11 bits of exponent, 52 of fraction), every NaN as the one quiet NaN, 0x7FF8000000000000, as Kotlin's toBits gives it. Math.floatFromBits goes back.

1.0.toBits()   // 4607182418800017408 (0x3FF0000000000000)
fun float.floor() : float#

The largest whole number at or below the float, as a float: -2.5 gives -3.0. NaN, infinities and -0.0 are themselves, and so is a float past 2^63 (Math.floor gives an int, which ends there).

fun float.ceil() : float#

The smallest whole number at or above the float, as a float: -2.5 gives -2.0.

fun float.frexp() : Pair<float, int>#

The float as m × 2^e, 0.5 <= |m| < 1 with the float's sign, exactly (subnormals too): Pair(m, e). 0, NaN and the infinities are themselves with e 0. C's frexp; Math.ldexp goes back.

8.0.frexp()   // Pair(first=0.5, second=4)
fun Number.roundToInt() : int#

The nearest whole number, a half going up: 2.5 gives 3 and -2.5 gives -2. An ArithmeticException for NaN, or where it does not fit in an int.

fun Number.toInt() : int#

The whole part, what is after the point dropped: -2.9 gives -2. An ArithmeticException where it does not fit in an int, NaN and infinity among them.

fun Number.toFloat() : float#

The number as a float.

fun Number.toString(radix : int = 10) : string#

The number as text, as print shows it; with radix, a whole number in that base, from 2 to 36: 255.toString(16) is "ff". A ClassCastException for a float with a radix.

fun int.toUnsignedString(radix : int = 10) : string#

An int's 64 bits read as unsigned; Kotlin's ushr is >>>. toUnsignedString gives them as text in base radix: (-1).toUnsignedString() is "18446744073709551615".

fun int.compareUnsigned(other : int) : int#

How the int is ordered against other, both read as unsigned: -1, 0 or 1.

fun int.divideUnsigned(divisor : int) : int#

The int divided by divisor, both read as unsigned; an ArithmeticException for a divisor of 0.

fun int.remainderUnsigned(divisor : int) : int#

The remainder of the int divided by divisor, both read as unsigned; an ArithmeticException for a divisor of 0.

fun int.toInt16Wrapped() : int#

The low bits of an int, as the storage widths keep them, read back as an int. toInt16Wrapped keeps the low 16 bits as a signed number: 70000 gives 4464.

fun int.toUint16Wrapped() : int#

The low 16 bits as an unsigned number, from 0 to 65535: -1 gives 65535.

fun int.toInt32Wrapped() : int#

The low 32 bits as a signed number: 2147483648 gives -2147483648.

fun int.toUint32Wrapped() : int#

The low 32 bits as an unsigned number, from 0 to 4294967295: -1 gives 4294967295.

fun int.rotateLeft32(count : int) : int#

For 32-bit work ported from Kotlin: the low 32 bits' rotations, each a signed 32-bit number as Kotlin's Int is. The low 32 bits themselves are toInt32Wrapped(). The low 32 bits turned count places to the left, those leaving at the top coming back at the bottom, as a signed 32-bit number. Only the low 5 bits of count count.

fun int.rotateRight32(count : int) : int#

The low 32 bits turned count places to the right, those leaving at the bottom coming back at the top, as a signed 32-bit number. Only the low 5 bits of count count.

fun Number.compareTo(other : Number) : int#

How the number is ordered against other, by size: -1, 0 or 1.

fun Number.isNaN() : bool#

True for the float NaN, "not a number"; false for every int.

fun Number.isInfinite() : bool#

True for a float that is infinity or minus infinity; false for every int.

fun Number.isFinite() : bool#

True for every int, and for a float that is neither NaN nor infinite.

Math

object Math#

The mathematical functions. pi and e are constants, written in place of each read. The functions of floats are WinterWolf's own, not the system's: the same bits on every system and processor (x86-64 and ARM64), each within an ulp of the true value and almost always the nearest float.

const pi = 3.141592653589793#

The ratio of a circle's circumference to its diameter.

const e = 2.718281828459045#

The base of natural logarithms.

fun sqrt(x : Number) : float#

The square root of x; NaN for a negative x.

fun sin(x : Number) : float#

The sine of x, an angle in radians.

fun cos(x : Number) : float#

The cosine of x, an angle in radians.

fun tan(x : Number) : float#

The tangent of x, an angle in radians.

fun asin(x : Number) : float#

The angle in radians, from -pi/2 to pi/2, whose sine is x; NaN outside -1 to 1.

fun acos(x : Number) : float#

The angle in radians, from 0 to pi, whose cosine is x; NaN outside -1 to 1.

fun atan(x : Number) : float#

The angle in radians, from -pi/2 to pi/2, whose tangent is x.

fun atan2(y : Number, x : Number) : float#

The angle in radians, from -pi to pi, from the x axis to the point (x, y).

fun hypot(x : Number, y : Number) : float#

The length of the hypotenuse, sqrt(x * x + y * y), without overflow on the way.

fun ldexp(x : Number, e : int) : float#

x × 2^e, rounded once: exact unless the result is subnormal or past the largest float (an infinity then). NaN, infinities and zeros are themselves. C's ldexp, Java's Math.scalb.

fun floatFromBits(bits : int) : float#

The float whose 64 bits are bits, as float.toBits gives them (Kotlin's Double.fromBits): a NaN with a payload of its own stays a NaN.

fun ln(x : Number) : float#

The natural logarithm of x, in base e; minus infinity for 0, NaN for a negative x.

fun log(x : Number, base : Number) : float#

The logarithm of x in base base, as Kotlin's: Math.log(8, 2) is 3.0. NaN where the base is 1, 0 or negative; the natural logarithm is ln (the user, 2026-11-08).

fun log10(x : Number) : float#

The logarithm of x in base 10.

fun log2(x : Number) : float#

The logarithm of x in base 2.

fun exp(x : Number) : float#

e to the power x.

fun trunc(x : Number) : float#

x with what is after the point dropped, as a float: -2.7 gives -2.0.

fun sinh(x : Number) : float#

The hyperbolic sine of x: (e^x - e^-x) / 2.

fun cosh(x : Number) : float#

The hyperbolic cosine of x: (e^x + e^-x) / 2.

fun tanh(x : Number) : float#

The hyperbolic tangent of x: sinh(x) / cosh(x), from -1 to 1.

fun asinh(x : Number) : float#

The number whose hyperbolic sine is x.

fun acosh(x : Number) : float#

The number of 0 or more whose hyperbolic cosine is x; NaN below 1.

fun atanh(x : Number) : float#

The number whose hyperbolic tangent is x; an infinity at -1 and 1, NaN outside them.

fun expm1(x : Number) : float#

e to the power x, less 1: exact where x is near 0, where exp(x) - 1 loses its digits.

fun log1p(x : Number) : float#

The natural logarithm of 1 + x: exact where x is near 0.

fun floor(x : Number) : int#

The nearest int at or below x: -2.5 gives -3. An ArithmeticException for NaN, infinity, or a float too large for an int; the same holds for ceil and round.

fun ceil(x : Number) : int#

The nearest int at or above x: -2.5 gives -2.

fun round(x : Number) : int#

The nearest int, a half going away from zero: 2.5 gives 3 and -2.5 gives -3 (where roundToInt() gives -2).

fun abs(x : int) : int#

Of the kind given: an int for ints, a float for floats. abs gives the number without its sign; an ArithmeticException for int.minValue, whose positive does not fit in an int.

fun abs(x : float) : float#

The float without its sign.

fun abs(x : Number) : Number#

The number without its sign, an int for an int and a float for a float.

fun sign(x : int) : int#

-1, 0 or 1, as x is negative, zero or positive.

fun sign(x : float) : float#

-1.0 or 1.0 as x is negative or positive; x itself for 0.0, -0.0 and NaN.

fun sign(x : Number) : Number#

The sign of x, an int for an int and a float for a float.

fun min(first : int, vararg rest : int) : int#

The smallest of the ints given.

fun min(first : float, vararg rest : float) : float#

The smallest of the floats given.

fun min(first : Number, vararg rest : Number) : Number#

The smallest of the numbers given, as it was given; the first of them where several are as small.

fun max(first : int, vararg rest : int) : int#

The largest of the ints given.

fun max(first : float, vararg rest : float) : float#

The largest of the floats given.

fun max(first : Number, vararg rest : Number) : Number#

The largest of the numbers given, as it was given; the first of them where several are as large.

fun pow(base : Number, exponent : Number) : Number#

An int to a power of 0 or more is an int; anything else is a float. An ArithmeticException where an int result would not fit in an int.

fun random() : float#

A random float from 0.0 up to 1.0, not including 1.0.

fun randomInt(low : int, high : int) : int#

A random int from low to high, both included; an IllegalArgumentException where low is greater than high.

fun seed(value : int)#

Starts random() and randomInt() again from value: after the same seed, they give the same numbers in the same order, on every system.

bytesource.ww

Files of any size, a byte at a time (M9/M10, from JadeUI's jadelib-io)

What the hex viewer and the large text viewer stand on: a file of many gigabytes read where it is, a few pages of it kept; a byte changed where it is and nothing else written; the lines of a text found without keeping each line's place.

interface ByteSource : AutoCloseable#

A source of bytes read at any position: a file (PagedFile, of any size) or bytes in memory (InMemoryByteSource, so that what is not saved yet can be read as a file is). A file's read may fail, an IOException; bytes in memory never do.

val length : int#

How many bytes there are.

fun byteAt(offset : int) : int throws IOException#

The byte at offset, 0 to 255; -1 outside the source.

fun bytesAt(offset : int, count : int) : Bytes throws IOException#

Up to count bytes from offset, fewer at the end, none outside the source: for work on a run of bytes, which reads a page at a time where byteAt reads one.

fun invalidate()#

Forgets what it keeps, after what it reads changed. Nothing by default.

override fun close()#

Lets go of what it holds. Nothing by default.

class InMemoryByteSource(private val bytes : Bytes) : ByteSource#

A ByteSource over bytes in memory, which may be changed in place with setByte.

override val length : int#

How many bytes there are.

override fun byteAt(offset : int) : int#

The byte at offset, 0 to 255; -1 outside the bytes.

override fun bytesAt(offset : int, count : int) : Bytes#

Up to count bytes from offset, a copy; fewer at the end, none outside the bytes.

fun setByte(offset : int, value : int)#

Puts the low 8 bits of value at offset; nothing outside the bytes.

fun toByteArray() : Bytes#

A copy of the bytes as they are now.

class PagedFile : ByteSource#

A file read at any position a page at a time, the last 64 pages read kept (4 MB), so that any part of a file of many gigabytes is read without the rest of it being. Its length is the file's as it was opened. Threads may share one: a lock keeps its pages right between them.

val file : FilePath#

The file.

override val length : int#

The file's length as it was opened.

override fun byteAt(offset : int) : int throws IOException#

The byte at offset, 0 to 255; -1 outside the file as it was opened, or where the file has become shorter since.

override fun bytesAt(offset : int, count : int) : Bytes throws IOException#

Up to count bytes from offset, read a page at a time; fewer at the end of the file as it was opened, or where it has become shorter since.

override fun invalidate()#

Forgets the pages read, after the file changed: the next reads are from the file.

override fun close()#

Lets go of the file, and of the pages.

fun writeByteDiff(file : FilePath | string, changes : List<Pair<int, int>>) : int throws IOException#

Changes bytes of a file where they are, each change a position and the value whose low 8 bits go there, and nothing else written: a file of 20 GB with three bytes edited is saved by writing three bytes. A position outside the file is passed over, never added; the changes are made in order of position (the last of two at one position winning) and are on the disk when it returns. How many were made: fewer than given where some were outside, which is how a caller learns the file became shorter under it. Not written to a new file and renamed into place, as a whole-file save is: that would copy the 20 GB.

class LineIndex#

Where the lines of a text file start, kept for every 4096th line only (checkpointInterval): one number for every 4096 lines, where a number for each line would take gigabytes for a file of 20 GB. A line is found from the checkpoint before it, by reading on to its start.

It is built by reading the whole file: with build() where it is called, or with buildInBackground() by a worker of its own on another core, the program going on meanwhile. It can be read while it is built: lineCount and the lookups answer for what is indexed so far.

val checkpointInterval#

How many lines apart the kept starts are.

val file : FilePath#

The file.

val fileLength : int#

Its length when the index was made.

var lineCount#

The lines indexed so far; all of them once complete (an empty file is one empty line).

var complete#

Whether the whole file is indexed.

var indexedBytes#

How many bytes are indexed so far.

fun build(onProgress : () -> unit = {}) throws IOException#

Reads the whole file where it is called, onProgress called after each megabyte. A second build replaces the index rather than adding to it.

fun buildInBackground(onProgress : () -> unit = {}) : Job<int>#

Builds the index in a worker of its own, on another core, and returns at once: what is indexed is here as the worker sends it, about a megabyte at a time, onProgress called on the event loop each time. The job finishes once the whole file is indexed, or fails with the IOException that stopped it, giving the number of lines; cancelling it stops the worker. A second build replaces the index.

fun lineStartOffset(line : int, source : ByteSource) : int throws IOException#

Where line starts, read on from the checkpoint before it in source, which holds the file's bytes; the end of the source for a line past it.

fun lineAtOffset(target : int, source : ByteSource) : int throws IOException#

The line target is on, read on from the checkpoint at or before it.

val checkpointCount : int#

How many line starts it keeps: line 0's and one for every checkpointInterval lines after it, which is what its memory grows with.

complex.ww

Complex numbers (2026-11-19)

A complex number: a real part and an imaginary part, two floats, held as a value (no object of its own, copied where it is passed). The functions follow C99's and Python's cmath: their branch cuts are the same, signed zeros read the same way (1 - z keeps the sign of z's imaginary zero turned round, as a real number less a complex one does there).

val z = Complex.of(3, 4)                    // 3.0+4.0i
print(z.abs(), z * Complex.i, 1 / z, z.sqrt(), Complex.of(-1).sqrt())
print(Complex.i.pow(2) == -1)               // true
print(Complex.fromPolar(2, Math.pi / 2))    // 1.2246467991473532e-16+2.0i

It mixes with ints and floats on either side; a complex number whose imaginary part is 0 is equal to the real number of its real part (Complex.of(3) == 3), and finds it as a key.

value Complex(val re : float, val im : float)#

A complex number, re + im i, two floats held as a value.

fun of(re : Number, im : Number = 0) : Complex#

The number re + im i, from ints or floats.

fun fromPolar(r : Number, theta : Number) : Complex#

The number of size r at the angle theta (radians) from the real axis.

val zero : Complex#

0.

val one : Complex#

1.

val i : Complex#

The imaginary unit: i * i is -1.

op +(other : Complex) : Complex#

The sum.

op +(other : float) : Complex#

The sum with a float.

op +(other : int) : Complex#

The sum with an int.

The difference.

op -(other : float) : Complex#

The difference with a float.

op -(other : int) : Complex#

The difference with an int.

op -() : Complex#

The number with both parts' signs turned round.

The product.

op *(other : float) : Complex#

The product with a float.

op *(other : int) : Complex#

The product with an int.

op /(other : Complex) : Complex#

Divided by Smith's way, which neither overflows nor underflows on the way where the answer itself does not.

op /(other : float) : Complex#

Divided by a float.

op /(other : int) : Complex#

Divided by an int.

fun abs() : float#

Its size: the distance from 0, without overflow on the way.

fun arg() : float#

Its angle from the positive real axis, in radians, from -pi to pi.

fun conjugate() : Complex#

Its mirror in the real axis: the imaginary part's sign turned round.

fun reciprocal() : Complex#

1 divided by it.

fun isNaN() : bool#

Whether either part is NaN.

fun isInfinite() : bool#

Whether either part is an infinity (and neither NaN).

fun pow(n : int) : Complex#

To the power n, worked out by multiplying (exactly where the parts are whole and small: i to the power 2 is -1, not nearly); a negative n gives 1 over the power.

fun pow(w : Complex) : Complex#

To the power w: e to the power w * log(this). 0 to a power whose real part is above 0 is 0, and anything to the power 0 is 1.

fun pow(x : float) : Complex#

To the power of a float x: e to the power x * log(this).

fun sqrt() : Complex#

The principal square root: its real part 0 or more, the cut along the negative real axis (-1+0.0i gives i, -1-0.0i gives -i).

fun exp() : Complex#

e to its power.

fun log() : Complex#

The natural logarithm, its imaginary part from -pi to pi (the cut along the negative real axis).

fun log10() : Complex#

The logarithm in base 10.

fun sin() : Complex#

The sine.

fun cos() : Complex#

The cosine.

fun tan() : Complex#

The tangent.

fun sinh() : Complex#

The hyperbolic sine.

fun cosh() : Complex#

The hyperbolic cosine.

fun tanh() : Complex#

The hyperbolic tangent; exact far from 0, where the parts' exponentials would overflow.

fun asin() : Complex#

The inverse functions, each the principal value, their cuts where C99 puts them.

fun acos() : Complex#

The inverse cosine, its real part from 0 to pi.

fun atan() : Complex#

The inverse tangent.

The inverse hyperbolic sine.

fun acosh() : Complex#

The inverse hyperbolic cosine, its real part 0 or more.

fun atanh() : Complex#

The inverse hyperbolic tangent.

op ==(other : any?) : bool#

Equal to a Complex of equal parts, or to an int or float equal to its real part where its imaginary part is 0. NaN in either part is equal to nothing, as a float NaN is.

fun hashCode() : int#

A real one's is its real part's, so that Complex.of(3) and 3 find each other as keys.

fun toString() : string#

As "3.0+4.0i", "3.0-4.0i".

fun int.toComplex() : Complex#

Into a Complex: the number with an imaginary part of 0.

fun float.toComplex() : Complex#

The float with an imaginary part of 0.

op int.+(other : Complex) : Complex#

An int or a float on the left of a Complex: the real number's imaginary part a 0 with no sign to give (x - z keeps z's imaginary part turned round, -0.0 for a 0.0, as C99's does).

core.ww

The standard library, the part written in WinterWolf. The names are Kotlin's, so that code ported from it reads the same.

Small values that functions hand out

value Pair<A, B>(val first : A, val second : B)#

Two values, made with a to b or Pair(a, b).

first and second, as a new list.

Three values, made with Triple(a, b, c).

fun toList() : open List<any?>#

first, second and third, as a new list.

One key of a map with its value: what map.entries holds.

fun toPair() : Pair<K, V>#

The key and its value, as a Pair.

value IndexedValue<T>(val index : int, val value : T)#

An item with its position: what withIndex() gives.

Checks

Each in two forms, with a message and without, copied in where it is called, so that a check that passes costs a test and nothing is made for its message (8.4i).

inline fun require(condition : bool)#

Throws an IllegalArgumentException unless condition holds: for checking what a function is given. The message is what lazyMessage gives, worked out only then, or "Failed requirement.":

require(n > 0) { "n is $n" }
inline fun require(condition : bool, lazyMessage : () -> any?)#

The same, the IllegalArgumentException's message what lazyMessage gives.

inline fun check(condition : bool)#

Throws an IllegalStateException unless condition holds: for the state things are in. The message is what lazyMessage gives, worked out only then, or "Check failed.".

inline fun check(condition : bool, lazyMessage : () -> any?)#

The same, the IllegalStateException's message what lazyMessage gives.

inline fun <T> requireNotNull(value : T?) : T#

value, now known not to be null; an IllegalArgumentException when it is null, its message what lazyMessage gives or "Required value was null.".

inline fun <T> requireNotNull(value : T?, lazyMessage : () -> any?) : T#

The same, the IllegalArgumentException's message what lazyMessage gives.

inline fun <T> checkNotNull(value : T?) : T#

value, now known not to be null; an IllegalStateException when it is null, its message what lazyMessage gives or "Required value was null.".

inline fun <T> checkNotNull(value : T?, lazyMessage : () -> any?) : T#

The same, the IllegalStateException's message what lazyMessage gives.

inline fun assert(condition : bool, message : string = "assertion failed")#

Nothing when condition holds; otherwise an AssertionError, an Error a bare catch lets through, with message, or with what lazyMessage gives, worked out only then, as Kotlin writes it (8.5d): assert(total >= 0) { "total is $total" }.

inline fun assert(condition : bool, lazyMessage : () -> any?)#

The same, the message what lazyMessage gives.

object Program#

The program as it runs: what it was started with, and its name.

if (Program.arguments.contains("--verbose")) { ... }
Program.name = "bank"            // at the start: what the program's own classes say they come from
val arguments : List<string>#

What it was started with after its file's name, in order: ["--fast", "in.txt"] for ww bank.ww --fast in.txt.

var name : string#

Its name: its own file's, without .ww ("bank" for bank.ww), unless set otherwise. It is the origin of the classes the program declares (Class.origin); set it before it is used.

val environment : Environment#

The environment it was started with, read once: Program.environment["LOCALAPPDATA"] is that variable's value, or null where it is not set. In a sandbox there are none.

val processId : int#

Its own process id, as the system knows it (1 in a sandbox).

val hostName : string#

The computer's name on the network ("sandbox" in a sandbox).

val output : Writer#

Its output, what print writes to, as a Writer: for code that writes to a Writer, a file's or this one. What it writes goes out in turn with print's; closing it does nothing.

report.writeTo(Program.output)
val errors : Writer#

Standard error, what printError writes to, as a Writer: Program.errors.println("lost") as Kotlin's System.err.println. What print wrote before goes out first; closing it does nothing.

var onUncaughtError : ((Throwable) -> unit)?#

What takes an error nobody caught outside the script itself: the one that ends a thread, on that thread as it ends, and a job's that failed with nothing asking for it (by get, then, onError and the like), once nothing can: when the job is collected, or as the program ends (8.7a). Without one, each is reported as an error nobody caught, the program goes on, and its exit code is 70 when it ends.

Program.onUncaughtError = { error -> log.write("lost: $error") }
val memoryUsed : int#

The bytes its objects take now, with what they hold (a list's items, the bytes of Bytes), the garbage not yet collected counted with them: right after collectGarbage(), what is still in use. Each worker has its own; a foreign Memory block is not counted.

Program.collectGarbage()
val before = Program.memoryUsed
load(map)
print("the map takes ${Program.memoryUsed - before} bytes")
val memoryKept : int#

The bytes kept, not given back to the system, for lists to use again: a list of 1 MB or more (a picture's pixels, a large grid), once nothing reaches it, leaves its memory here, up to 256 MB in all, for the next list of about its size, which then finds memory already in use rather than fresh memory, each page of which costs time the first time it is written (a 48 MB picture's copy some 6.5 ms fresh, 2.5 from here). Shared by every worker; not part of memoryUsed. collectGarbage() gives it all back.

fun collectGarbage()#

Collects the garbage now, as Java's System.gc(): every object nothing can reach any more is freed. The collector runs by itself as objects are made; this is for a moment that matters, before something is timed, say, or once a large piece of work is done with, so that its memory is given back then rather than later (the memory kept for lists, memoryKept, too). Inside a parallel section it collects nothing.

class Environment#

The environment variables a program was started with (Program.environment): read like a map, each name giving its value or null. On Windows a name is the same whatever its case, as the system has it (SystemRoot, SYSTEMROOT); elsewhere case counts.

op [](name : string) : string?#

The variable's value: environment["TEMP"]; null where it is not set.

fun get(name : string) : string?#

The variable's value, as [] gives it.

fun containsKey(name : string) : bool#

Whether the variable is set.

val names : List<string>#

The names, as the system spells them.

val size : int#

How many variables there are.

fun toMap() : Map<string, string>#

Every variable, as a map whose names are as the system spells them.

override fun toString() : string#

Environment(42 variables): the values are left out, as some hold what should not be shown.

fun error(message : any?) : nothing#

Throws an IllegalStateException with message as its text: error("no such user $name").

interface Iterator<T>#

What a for goes through, one item at a time, as Kotlin's: hasNext() says whether there is another, next() gives it. A for over an object whose iterator() gives one goes through that.

fun hasNext() : bool#

Whether there is another item.

fun next() : T#

The next item, moving past it.

interface Iterable<T>#

Something a for can go through: each time, it gives an Iterator that goes through its items.

fun iterator() : Iterator<T>#

A new Iterator, before the first item.

interface AutoCloseable#

Something to close when it is done with: a stream, a socket. A class of someone else's that has a close() is fitted to it with extend.

fun close() throws IOException#

Lets go of what it holds; use calls it when its block ends. A class's own may throw less, or nothing checked, as the streams' and the pools' do; use throws what that one throws (M6.7, 8.5i).

inline fun <T : AutoCloseable, R> T.use(block : (T) -> R) : R#

Runs the block on this and closes it afterwards, however the block ends: at its end, by a return, or by an exception. An error from close() after one from the block is set aside for the block's, which goes on, as Java has it. Copied in where it is called. What it may throw is what the block throws and what this object's own close() throws, which the checker finds from its type at each call (8.5i).

fun TODO(reason : string = "") : nothing#

Throws a NotImplementedError: for what is not written yet, as Kotlin's TODO().

inline infix fun <A, B> A.to(that : B) : Pair<A, B>#

a to b is Pair(a, b).

infix fun int.downTo(to : int) : Range#

10 downTo 1: from 10 down to 1, both of them included.

inline fun repeat(times : int, action : (int) -> unit)#

Runs action that many times, giving it 0, 1, 2 and so on.

Every value

fun any?.toString() : string#

The value as text, as "$value" gives it: "null" for null.

fun any?.equals(other : any?) : bool#

Whether it is equal to other, as == says.

fun any?.hashCode() : int#

Equal values give equal hashes: by what they hold for strings, numbers, frozen collections, data classes and values; an object of any other class by which object it is. The same in every run, but for such an object: an int's is Kotlin's (5.hashCode() is 5), a float's and a bool's too (8.2d).

fun any.compareTo(other : any?) : int#

For a value whose class has no compareTo of its own: negative, zero or positive as it comes before, with or after other. Numbers go by size, strings by their characters, chars by their codes, false before true, and null before everything; other values are a ClassCastException.

inline fun <T, R> T.let(block : (T) -> R) : R#

The scope functions, copied into each call with their blocks: a bare return in a block leaves the function the call is in. let and also hand the value to the block, apply, run and with run the block on it; also and apply give the value back, the others the block's result. The value may be null, as T may stand for a type that takes it.

inline fun <T> T.also(block : (T) -> unit) : T#

Runs block with the value and gives the value back: for doing something on the way.

inline fun <T> T.apply(block : T.() -> unit) : T#

Runs block on the value, as this, and gives the value back: for setting it up.

inline fun <T, R> T.run(block : T.() -> R) : R#

Runs block on the value, as this, and gives what the block gives.

inline fun <T, R> with(receiver : T, block : T.() -> R) : R#

Runs block on receiver, as this, and gives what the block gives.

inline fun <T> T.takeIf(predicate : (T) -> bool) : T?#

The value if predicate holds for it, otherwise null.

inline fun <T> T.takeUnless(predicate : (T) -> bool) : T?#

The value if predicate does not hold for it, otherwise null.

fun any?.isNullOrEmpty() : bool#
