For a string, a list, a map or a set that may be null. It is one function for all of them, since null does not say which of them it would have been.

Functions

fun Function.invoke(vararg arguments : any?) : any?#

callback.invoke(a, b) is callback(a, b); with ?. it calls a function that may be null.

Comparators

A comparator is a function of two values that gives a negative number, zero or a positive one. sort, sortedWith and the like take one.

fun <T> compareBy(vararg selectors : (T) -> any?) : (T, T) -> int#

Orders by what each selector gives, the first that tells two values apart deciding.

fun <T> compareByDescending(selector : (T) -> any?) : (T, T) -> int#

Orders by what selector gives, largest first.

fun <T> naturalOrder() : (T, T) -> int#

Orders values from smallest to largest, null first.

fun <T> reverseOrder() : (T, T) -> int#

Orders values from largest to smallest, null last.

fun Function.compare(a : any?, b : any?) : int#

Calls a comparator: comparator.compare(a, b) is comparator(a, b).

fun <T> Function.thenBy(selector : (T) -> any?) : (T, T) -> int#

A comparator that falls back on what selector gives where this one finds two values equal.

fun <T> Function.thenByDescending(selector : (T) -> any?) : (T, T) -> int#

A comparator that falls back on what selector gives, largest first, where this one finds two values equal.

fun <T> Function.then(comparator : (T, T) -> int) : (T, T) -> int#

A comparator that falls back on another comparator where this one finds two values equal.

fun <T> Function.reversed() : (T, T) -> int#

The same comparator the other way round: what it put first comes last.

Making collections

fun <T> listOf(vararg items : T) : open List<T>#

A new list of items, in their order, which can be changed: listOf(1, 2, 3).

fun <T> emptyList() : List<T>#

A new empty list, typed as one that is only read.

fun <T> listOfNotNull(vararg items : T?) : open List<T>#

A new list of the items that are not null, in their order.

inline fun <T> List(size : int, init : (int) -> T) : open List<T>#

List(3) { it * 2 } is [0, 2, 4]: size items, each worked out from its position.

fun <K, V> mapOf(vararg pairs : Pair<K, V>) : open Map<K, V>#

mapOf("a" to 1, "b" to 2): a new map of pairs that can be changed, its keys in the order they came. A key given twice keeps its first place and its last value.

fun <K, V> emptyMap() : Map<K, V>#

A new empty map, typed as one that is only read.

fun <T> setOf(vararg items : T) : open Set<T>#

A new set of items that can be changed, each once, in the order they first came:

setOf(3, 1, 3) is {3, 1}
fun <T> emptySet() : Set<T>#

A new empty set, typed as one that is only read.

inline fun <T> buildList(builder : open List<T>.() -> unit) : open List<T>#

Each runs the lambda on a new collection, so that add(...), put(...) or append(...) inside it fill that collection, and gives what was built.

inline fun <K, V> buildMap(builder : open Map<K, V>.() -> unit) : open Map<K, V>#

Runs builder on a new map, so that put(key, value) inside it fills it, and gives the map.

inline fun <T> buildSet(builder : open Set<T>.() -> unit) : open Set<T>#

Runs builder on a new set, so that add(item) inside it fills it, and gives the set.

inline fun buildString(builder : StringBuilder.() -> unit) : string#

Runs builder on a new StringBuilder, so that append(...) inside it builds the text, and gives the text.

crypto.ww

What import Crypto is built on (networking plan, part 2, 2026-11-20)

The primitives in C++ (src/crypto), declared here with their types, as the standard library's own are: lib/Crypto calls them. A program uses lib/Crypto's classes, not these.

db.ww

Record files (JadeDB) and blob files (JadeHeap)

A file of records all the same size, read whole and written one record at a time, and a file of blobs of any size beside it (JadeLib's DB). A table is a class extending JadeDB that says how big its records are and how each is laid out:

data Item(val name : string, val price : int)

class Items(path : string) : JadeDB(path) {
  override val recordSize = 20                  // a name of 16 bytes, then a 4-byte price
  private val every = cached { mapRecords { bytes, at -> Item(readFixed(bytes, at, 16), readInt32(bytes, at + 16)) } }
  val all : List<Item>
    get() => every.value
  fun add(item : Item) { appendRecord { writeFixed(item.name, 16); writeInt32(item.price) } }
  fun setPrice(index : int, item : Item) { updateRecordAt(index) { writeFixed(item.name, 16); writeInt32(item.price) } }
}

Numbers are kept big-endian, text as UTF-8 in a field of a fixed size. The file is opened on first use and stays open; close() lets go of it (Windows will not delete or replace an open file), and the next read or write opens it again. Each change is made holding the table's lock, so threads can share a table; a subclass doing more than one step with the file holds lock itself. A cached { } value is dropped whenever a record is written, made again when next asked for.

data BlobRef(val offset : int, val length : int)#

A blob kept in a JadeHeap: where its 4-byte length is in the file, and how long it is.

abstract class JadeDB#

A file of records of recordSize bytes each, one after another: subclassed for each table. With appendMode true, writeInt32 and the other writes outside appendRecord and updateRecordAt go to the end of the file, as they do in a file opened to append; otherwise to position.

protected val dbFile : FilePath#

The file the records are kept in.

abstract val recordSize : int#

How many bytes each record takes.

protected var position#

Where the writes outside appendRecord and updateRecordAt go in the file: each moves it on past what it wrote. With appendMode, the end of the file whenever it is opened.

protected val lock : Lock#

The lock every change holds: a subclass holds it too for anything of more than one step with file or position.

protected val file : RandomAccessFile#

The file, open to read and write, made if it is not there; opened again after close(). Only with lock held.

fun exists() : bool#

Whether the file is there.

fun sync() throws IOException#

Returns once what was written is on the disk; nothing if nothing was written.

fun close()#

Lets go of the file, which Windows wants before it is deleted or replaced. The next read or write opens it again.

protected fun appendRecord(writer : () -> unit throws IOException) throws IOException#

Adds a record at the end of the file: writer writes its fields with writeInt32, writeInt64, writeFixed and writeBytes, in order. What it leaves of the record's bytes is zeros; writing past them is an IllegalStateException.

protected fun updateRecordAt(index : int, writer : () -> unit throws IOException) throws IOException#

Writes the record at index over again, as appendRecord makes one. An index past the last record makes the file longer, with zeros between.

protected fun truncate() throws IOException#

Takes every record out: the file left empty.

protected fun removeRecordAt(index : int) throws IOException#

Takes out the record at index, those after it moved down one, in the order they were. Nothing for an index with no record.

protected fun readAllBytes() : Bytes throws IOException#

Every byte of the file: none if it is not there, which does not make it.

protected fun readRecordAt(index : int) : Bytes? throws IOException#

The record at index, or null where there is none.

protected fun recordCount() : int throws IOException#

How many whole records the file holds: 0 if it is not there.

protected fun forEachRecord(action : (Bytes, int) -> unit throws IOException) throws IOException#

Runs action for each record, with the file's bytes and where the record starts in them.

protected fun <T> mapRecords(transform : (Bytes, int) -> T? throws IOException) : open List<T> throws IOException#

What transform makes of each record, as forEachRecord gives them, nulls left out.

protected fun readInt32(bytes : Bytes, at : int) : int#

The signed 32-bit number at at, big-endian.

protected fun readInt64(bytes : Bytes, at : int) : int#

The signed 64-bit number at at, big-endian.

protected fun readFixed(bytes : Bytes, at : int, size : int) : string#

The text in the size bytes at at, as writeFixed keeps it: UTF-8, the zeros and spaces after it left off.

protected fun writeInt32(value : int) throws IOException#

Writes value as a signed 32-bit number, big-endian: an ArithmeticException where it does not fit.

protected fun writeInt64(value : int) throws IOException#

Writes value as a signed 64-bit number, big-endian.

protected fun writeFixed(text : string, size : int) throws IOException#

Writes text as UTF-8 in exactly size bytes: zeros after it, or cut short where it is longer, before the character that would not fit whole.

protected fun writeBytes(data : Bytes) throws IOException#

Writes data as it is: into the record appendRecord or updateRecordAt is making, or else into the file at position.

protected fun <T> cached(initializer : () -> T throws IOException) : JadeDB.Cached<T>#

A value worked out from the file when first asked for, and kept until a record is written or invalidate() is called; then worked out again when next asked for.

private val names = cached { mapRecords { bytes, at -> readFixed(bytes, at, 16) } }
... names.value ...
class Cached<T>#

A value cached { } keeps.

val value : T#

The value: worked out now if it is not kept, once however many threads ask.

fun reset()#

Drops the value: the next value works it out again.

fun invalidate()#

Drops every cached { } value and calls clearIndex(), so what is next asked for is read from the file again. Writing a record does it; call it after the file is changed some other way.

protected fun clearIndex()#

For a subclass's own indexes: dropped here, made again when next wanted.

class JadeHeap#

Blobs of any size in one file, each kept as its 4-byte big-endian length and then its bytes: append gives a BlobRef to read it back by. A blob is let go of by dropping its BlobRef; compact makes the file again with only the blobs still wanted.

fun exists() : bool#

Whether the file is there.

fun append(payload : Bytes) : BlobRef throws IOException#

Adds payload at the end: the BlobRef to read it by.

fun read(ref : BlobRef) : Bytes throws IOException#

The blob ref stands for. An IOException where the file holds no blob of that length there.

fun clear() throws IOException#

Takes every blob out: the file left empty, before it is filled again.

fun sync() throws IOException#

Returns once what was written is on the disk; nothing if nothing was written.

fun close()#

Lets go of the file, which Windows wants before it is deleted or replaced. The next read or append opens it again.

fun compact(live : List<BlobRef>) : Map<BlobRef, BlobRef> throws IOException#

Makes the file again with only the blobs of live, in that order, the space of the rest given back: gives each one's new BlobRef by its old one. The new file is written beside the old one (its name with ".compact" after it), put on the disk, and then moved over the old one in one step, so a crash leaves one or the other whole.

errors.ww

What can be thrown

Java's tree, which Kotlin code uses. Only a Throwable is thrown. It has a message, which may be null, and the Throwable that caused it, if any.

Error: what the VM cannot go on from. A bare `catch (e)` does not catch one; it is caught
  only where it is named, `catch (e : Error)` or `catch (e : Throwable)` (D151).
Exception: everything else, which a bare `catch (e)` catches.
  RuntimeException and the classes under it: mistakes in the program. Unchecked.
  Every other Exception (IOException, TimeoutException, ExecutionException, a program's
  own): checked. The compiler works out where one can go, and asks for it to be caught
  where nothing can pass it on (D35).
class Throwable(val message : string? = null, val cause : Throwable? = null)#

What can be thrown, the root of every exception and error. message says what went wrong and may be null; cause is the Throwable that led to this one, if any.

val stackTrace : List<string>#

Where it was made, as Kotlin's stackTrace: the calls under way then, innermost first, each as an uncaught error shows it, "at f (main.ww:3)"; a deep stack keeps its ten innermost calls and its two outermost, with how many were left out between. Its own constructors are not among them. Empty for an OutOfMemoryError.

fun stackTraceToString() : string#

The error and where it was made, as Kotlin's stackTraceToString: its toString(), then its stackTrace, each line indented, then what was set aside for it ("Suppressed: ") and what caused it ("Caused by: "), each with its own. Each line ends with "\n".

catch (e : IOException) { log.write(e.stackTraceToString()) }
val suppressed : List<Throwable>#

The errors set aside for this one, in the order they came.

fun addSuppressed(e : Throwable)#

Sets e aside for this one, to be shown with it. An error is not set aside for itself.

override fun toString() : string#

The name of the class, then ": " and the message when there is one.

IllegalStateException("no file").toString()   // "IllegalStateException: no file"
class Error(message : string? = null, cause : Throwable? = null) : Throwable(message, cause)#

What the program cannot go on from. A bare catch (e) does not catch one; only a catch that names it, catch (e : Error) or catch (e : Throwable), does.

class Exception(message : string? = null, cause : Throwable? = null) : Throwable(message, cause)#

What a program can go on from, which a bare catch (e) catches. One that is not a RuntimeException is checked: the compiler asks for it to be caught where nothing can pass it on.

class RuntimeException(message : string? = null, cause : Throwable? = null) : Exception(message, cause)#

A mistake in the program: unchecked, so it need not be caught or passed on.

Errors: the VM is in trouble

class OutOfMemoryError(message : string? = "out of memory") : Error(message)#

The memory ran out. An Error, which a bare catch (e) does not catch.

class StackOverflowError(message : string? = "stack overflow") : Error(message)#

The calls went too deep, as in a function that calls itself with no end. An Error, which a bare catch (e) does not catch.

class InternalError(message : string? = null, cause : Throwable? = null) : Error(message, cause)#

Something wrong with the program as compiled, as against the program's own code.

class AssertionError(message : string? = "assertion failed", cause : Throwable? = null) : Error(message, cause)#

assert(...) that does not hold.

class NotImplementedError(message : string? = "an operation is not implemented") : Error(message)#

TODO(): what is not written yet.

Mistakes in the program: unchecked

class ArithmeticException(message : string? = null, cause : Throwable? = null) : RuntimeException(message, cause)#

Division by zero, or an int whose answer does not fit.

class IndexOutOfBoundsException(message : string? = null, cause : Throwable? = null) : RuntimeException(message, cause)#

A position that is not in a list, a string or bytes.

class NullPointerException(message : string? = null, cause : Throwable? = null) : RuntimeException(message, cause)#

!! on null, or null used where a value is needed.

class ClassCastException(message : string? = null, cause : Throwable? = null) : RuntimeException(message, cause)#

A value of one type where another is needed: as, or a typed place given the wrong value.

class IllegalArgumentException(message : string? = null, cause : Throwable? = null) : RuntimeException(message, cause)#

An argument a function cannot take; require() that does not hold.

class NumberFormatException(message : string? = null, cause : Throwable? = null) : IllegalArgumentException(message, cause)#

Text that is not the number asked of it.

class PatternSyntaxException(val description : string, val pattern : string, val index : int)#

A pattern that is no regular expression (Regex): what is wrong, the pattern, and where in it (-1 where no one place is to blame). Its message is Java's, the pattern under it and a ^ at the place.

class IllegalStateException(message : string? = null, cause : Throwable? = null) : RuntimeException(message, cause)#

Something done at a time it cannot be; check() and error().

class UnsupportedOperationException(message : string? = null, cause : Throwable? = null) : RuntimeException(message, cause)#

Something a value does not do: changing what is frozen, a member it has not.

class SecurityException(message : string? = null, cause : Throwable? = null) : RuntimeException(message, cause)#

What a safe script may not do: reach files, the network, processes, native libraries, threads and the rest outside the program (Script, the scripting plan's part 3).

class NoSuchElementException(message : string? = null, cause : Throwable? = null) : RuntimeException(message, cause)#

The first of nothing, a key that is not there.

class ConcurrentModificationException(message : string? = null, cause : Throwable? = null) : RuntimeException(message, cause)#

A collection changed while it was gone through: a set or a map whose keys change during a for over it (8.2i).

class InterruptedException(message : string? = "the thread was interrupted") : RuntimeException(message)#

A wait that was interrupted (sleep, join, wait or a queue): unchecked, so that waiting needs no try.

class CancellationException(message : string? = "the task was cancelled", cause : Throwable? = null) : IllegalStateException(message, cause)#

A task that was cancelled, thrown by what waits for it: unchecked.

Checked

class IOException(message : string? = null, cause : Throwable? = null) : Exception(message, cause)#

Reading, writing, connecting or starting a program went wrong.

class DataFormatException(message : string? = null, cause : Throwable? = null) : Exception(message, cause)#

Data that is not in the form it should be: compressed data that is damaged or ends early, bits that are no code (Bytes.inflate, BitReader, HuffmanTable), a picture file a codec cannot read.

class SocketTimeoutException(message : string? = "the read timed out") : IOException(message)#

A socket's read that waited longer than its readTimeout, an IOException as in Java.

class UnknownHostException(message : string? = "the host was not found") : IOException(message)#

A name the system's resolver could not find (Dns.lookup, a Socket given a host), an IOException as in Java.

class UncheckedIOException(cause : IOException) : RuntimeException(cause.message, cause)#

An IOException where nothing checked can be thrown: a line read lazily by a sequence. cause is the IOException, as in Java.

class TimeoutException(message : string? = "the time ran out") : Exception(message)#

A wait with a time limit ran out of time.

class ExecutionException(cause : Throwable? = null) : Exception(cause?.message, cause)#

What a task threw, given by what waited for its result: cause is that.

class InvocationTargetException(cause : Throwable? = null) : Exception(cause?.toString(), cause)#

What a method, a constructor, or a property's getter or setter called by its name threw, as Java's: cause is that. A failure that came through reflection says so.

file.ww

Paths

class FilePath#

A file or directory on disk, by its path: Java's File and java.nio.file.Path in one. Making a FilePath touches nothing; what is in the path (name, parent, extension) is a property, and what asks the disk (exists(), size(), list()) is a function.

val notes = FilePath("notes.txt")
if (notes.exists()) { print(notes.readText()) }
FilePath(directory, "out.bin").writeBytes(data)
for file in FilePath("src").walk() { if (file.extension == "ww") { print(file.name) } }

Text is UTF-8 unless a Charset says otherwise.

val text : string#

The path as it was made.

val separator : string get()#

What separates the names in a path: "\" on Windows, and in a sandbox with Windows' paths.

fun createTempDirectory(prefix : string = "ww") : FilePath throws IOException#

A new, empty directory or file in the system's temporary directory, its name starting with prefix.

fun createTempFile(prefix : string = "ww", suffix : string = ".tmp") : FilePath throws IOException#

A new, empty file in the system's temporary directory, named prefix, a number, then suffix, as ww240932127838.tmp; no other program gets the same one. An IOException where none can be made.

fun roots() : List<FilePath>#

The tops of the file system: each drive on Windows (C:\, D:\), / elsewhere.

val name : string#

The last name in the path: FilePath("a/b.txt").name is "b.txt".

val extension : string#

What follows the last dot of the name, without the dot; "" if there is none.

val nameWithoutExtension : string#

The name without its last dot and what follows: "a.tar" for a.tar.gz, the whole name where it has no dot, and "" for a name such as .bashrc whose only dot comes first.

val parent : FilePath?#

The directory it is in; null at the top (C:\, /) and for a bare name.

val absolute : FilePath#

Made whole from the current directory; it need not exist.

fun resolve(child : FilePath | string) : FilePath#

Something inside this directory: FilePath("a").resolve("b.txt") is a/b.txt.

fun exists() : bool#

Whether something is there, a file or a directory.

fun isFile() : bool#

Whether a file is there; false for a directory and where nothing is there.

fun isDirectory() : bool#

Whether a directory is there; false for a file and where nothing is there. Through a link, whether it leads to one.

fun isSymbolicLink() : bool#

Whether it is a symbolic link itself (on Windows, a junction too), whatever it leads to.

fun isReadable() : bool#

Whether this program may read it; false where nothing is there.

fun isWritable() : bool#

Whether this program may write it (on Windows, whether it is not read-only); false where nothing is there.

fun isExecutable() : bool#

Whether it can be run as a program: on Windows, a file whose extension PATHEXT lists; elsewhere, one marked so (or a directory that may be entered).

fun size() : int#

The size in bytes; 0 for a file that is not there.

fun lastModified() : Instant?#

When it was last written; null where nothing is there.

fun canonical() : FilePath throws IOException#

With ., .. and links worked out; an IOException if it is not there.

fun list() : open List<FilePath> throws IOException#

What is directly in this directory, in order of name; an IOException if it is not a directory.

fun entries() : open List<DirEntry> throws IOException#

What is directly in this directory, in order of name, each with what it is, its size and when it was last written, as the system gives them while listing it: on Windows in the same call, where list() and then each one's size() and lastModified() ask again for each (as Python's os.scandir has it). An IOException if it is not a directory, as for list().

for e in FilePath("logs").entries() { if (e.isFile and e.size > 1_000_000) { print(e.name) } }
fun walk(onError : ((FilePath, IOException) -> unit)? = null) : Sequence<FilePath>#

Every path below this directory, each directory before what is in it, read as it goes. A symbolic link is given but not gone into, as Java's Files.walk does (8.8a): one to a directory above would go round for ever. A directory it cannot read, this one too, is an UncheckedIOException where the walk comes to it, the IOException its cause, as Java's Files.walk throws; with onError, that is given the directory and the IOException, and the walk goes on past it.

val totalSpace : int#

The room on the volume it is on, in bytes, as Java's File gives it: all of it, what is free, and what is free to this program (less, where the system keeps some back or the user has a quota). An IOException where the path is on no volume there is.

val freeSpace : int#

What is free on its volume, in bytes.

val usableSpace : int#

What is free on its volume to this program, in bytes.

fun readText(charset : Charset = Charset.utf8) : string throws IOException#

The whole file as text in charset; an IOException where it cannot be read, as when it is not there or is a directory.

fun readBytes() : Bytes throws IOException#

The whole file as bytes; an IOException where it cannot be read.

fun readLines(charset : Charset = Charset.utf8) : open List<string> throws IOException#

The lines of the file, without their line ends.

fun forEachLine(charset : Charset = Charset.utf8, action : (string) -> unit) throws IOException#

The lines one at a time, as they are read.

fun <R> useLines(charset : Charset = Charset.utf8, block : (Sequence<string>) -> R) : R throws IOException#

The block given the lines as a sequence, read as it goes; the file is closed afterwards.

fun writeText(text : string, charset : Charset = Charset.utf8) throws IOException#

Makes text, in charset, the whole file: made where it is not there, emptied first where it is. The directory it is in is not made: an IOException where it is missing, or where the file cannot be written.

fun appendText(text : string, charset : Charset = Charset.utf8) throws IOException#

Adds text, in charset, to the end of the file, which is made where it is not there; an IOException where it cannot be written.

fun writeBytes(data : Bytes) throws IOException#

Makes data the whole file, as writeText does with text.

fun appendBytes(data : Bytes) throws IOException#

Adds data to the end of the file, as appendText does with text.

fun reader(charset : Charset = Charset.utf8) : Reader throws IOException#

A piece at a time: close each when it is done with, or let use { } do it.

path.reader().use { it.readLine() }
fun writer(append : bool = false, charset : Charset = Charset.utf8) : Writer throws IOException#

A Writer to the file, made where it is not there: emptied first, or with append written after what it holds. An IOException where it cannot be opened.

fun mkdir(parents : bool = true) throws IOException#

The directory, and any missing above it unless parents is false. One there already is no failure.

fun delete() throws IOException#

A file or an empty directory: an IOException if it is not there or cannot go.

fun deleteIfExists() : bool throws IOException#

The same, giving whether it was there to delete; an IOException only where it could not go.

fun deleteRecursively() : bool#

Everything below it too; whether anything was deleted.

fun moveTo(target : FilePath | string, overwrite : bool = false) : FilePath throws IOException#

Each gives the FilePath it went to; an IOException if something is there and overwrite is false, or it cannot be done.

fun copyTo(target : FilePath | string, overwrite : bool = false) : FilePath throws IOException#

A copy of the file at target, given as a FilePath. Only a file is copied: a directory is an IOException, as is a target already there while overwrite is false.

fun setWritable(writable : bool) throws IOException#

Lets it be written, or keeps it from being written (on Windows, its read-only flag; elsewhere, written by its owner, or by no one); an IOException where nothing is there.

fun setLastModified(time : Instant) throws IOException#

Sets when it was last written; an IOException where nothing is there.

op ==(other : any?) : bool#

Whether other is a FilePath with the same text. Paths that name one file in different words, as "a" and "./a", are not ==; their canonical() Paths are, where the file is there.

fun hashCode() : int#

The hash code of its text, the same for two Paths that are ==.

override fun toString() : string#

The path as it was made, its text.

value DirEntry( val path : FilePath, val isDirectory : bool, val isFile : bool, val isSymbolicLink : bool, val size : int, val lastModified : Instant?)#

An entry of a directory, as FilePath.entries() gives it: its path, and what the system said of it while listing the directory. What it is is the entry's own: a symbolic link (on Windows a junction too) is neither a file nor a directory, whatever it leads to, so that a walk going by isDirectory does not go through one.

val name : string#

Its name: the last part of its path.

class RandomAccessFile : AutoCloseable#

What FilePath is built on (stdlib.cpp), declared with its types and what it throws. ---- A file at any position ---- A file read and written at a position, rather than from its start to its end as a stream is (M9/M10; Java's RandomAccessFile): a page of a file of many gigabytes read where it is, a byte changed where it is and nothing else written.

val file = RandomAccessFile(FilePath("big.bin"))      // to read
val page = file.readAt(65536, 4096)                   // 4096 bytes from 65536, fewer at the end
val edit = RandomAccessFile("big.bin", "rw")         // to read and write, made if it is not there
edit.writeAt(10, Bytes([255]))
edit.sync()                                          // on the disk before going on
edit.close()

Other programs may read and write the file while it is open, as Java lets them. Each read and write is at its own position, so threads may share one without moving each other's place.

val path : FilePath#

The file.

val writable : bool#

Whether it was opened to write as well ("rw").

fun readAt(position : int, count : int) : Bytes throws IOException#

Up to count bytes from position: fewer at the end of the file, none past it.

fun readAt(position : int, into : Bytes, offset : int = 0, count : int = into.size - offset) : int throws IOException#

Reads into into from offset, up to count bytes (all there is room for if not given), from position: how many were read, fewer at the end of the file, 0 past it. Nothing new is made, for a loop that reads page after page into one buffer.

fun writeAt(position : int, data : Bytes, offset : int = 0, count : int = data.size - offset) throws IOException#

Writes count of data's bytes from offset (all of them if not given) at position; the file grows where they go past its end. An IOException on a file opened to read.

fun size() : int throws IOException#

The file's size now, in bytes.

fun setSize(size : int) throws IOException#

Makes the file size bytes long: what is past it goes, and a file made longer is filled with zeros. An IllegalArgumentException for a size below 0, an IOException on a file opened to read.

fun sync() throws IOException#

Returns once what was written is on the disk, not only in the system's memory.

override fun close()#

Lets go of the file; reading or writing after it is an IOException. Closing again does nothing.

val isOpen : bool#

Whether it is still open.

fonts.ww

Fonts, text and pictures

The text engine: reading font files, measuring text and drawing it into a picture, all by code of WinterWolf's own. The system is asked for one thing only, which font files are installed. The same font file gives the same numbers and the same pixels everywhere.

val font = Font("Segoe UI", 14, bold = true)
val page = Image(font.width("Hello") + 20, font.lineHeight + 20, Color.white)
val canvas = page.canvas()
canvas.font = font
canvas.drawString("Hello", 10, 10 + font.ascent)
page.save("hello.png")

Measuring and drawing go by one rule. A character's width is its width in the font, scaled to the size and rounded to a whole pixel; a text's width is the sum of its characters' widths; and each character is drawn where the widths before it add up to. So width("abc") is exactly where "abc" ends when drawn, and a caret placed by measuring sits on the text.

On a dense screen a font is made with a scale (1.5, 2): its numbers are then in the screen's own pixels, which is where the rounding has to happen, and a size in the units the layout works in is the number divided by the scale.

What it does not do yet: scripts whose letters join or reorder (Arabic, Devanagari), right-to-left text, and marks placed on other letters. One character is one glyph.

data FontAxis(val tag : string, val minimum : Number, val defaultValue : Number, val maximum : Number)#

One axis a variable font can be varied along: "wght" (weight), "wdth" (width), "opsz" (the size it is to be read at) and so on.

class Typeface#

One font out of a font file: a family in one style, at no particular size.

val handle : int#

The text engine's number for the typeface; every Typeface of the same font has the same one.

val path : string#

The file the typeface was read from; "<bytes>" for one loaded from bytes.

val index : int#

Which font of its file it is: 0 unless the file is a collection (.ttc).

val family : string#

The family's name, as the font gives it: "Segoe UI".

val style : string#

The style within the family, as the font names it: "Bold Italic".

val fullName : string#

The family and the style together, as the font names them: "Segoe UI Bold Italic".

val legacyFamily : string#

The family as programs that know only four styles (regular, bold, italic, bold italic) see it, which may name a weight: "Segoe UI Semibold". Fonts.typefaces finds a family by either name.

val weight : Number#

How heavy its letters are, usually from 100 to 900: 400 is regular, 700 bold. A variable font's is its default weight.

val stretch : int#

How wide its letters are, from 1 (the narrowest) to 9 (the widest); 5 is the normal width.

val isItalic : bool#

Whether the typeface is italic or oblique.

val isMonospace : bool#

Whether the font says every character is the same width.

val isSymbol : bool#

Whether it is a symbol font, such as Wingdings: its characters are pictures, not letters.

val isVariable : bool#

Whether it is a variable font, one file holding a range of weights or other styles: axes lists what it can be varied along.

val hasCubicOutlines : bool#

Whether its outlines are cubic curves, the kind in an .otf file, rather than the quadratic curves of a .ttf; outline gives "C" in place of "Q".

val hasColor : bool#

Whether it has glyphs in colour, drawn or as pictures (emoji).

val glyphCount : int#

How many glyphs the font has; glyph numbers go from 0 to one less.

val unitsPerEm : int#

How many of the font's own units make its size (the em): what advance and outline measure in, often 1000 or 2048.

val axes : List<FontAxis>#

The axes of a variable font; none for any other.

fun has(character : any) : bool#

Whether the font has a character: a one-character string, or its number.

fun glyph(character : any) : int#

The number of the glyph for a character; 0, the "missing" glyph, if it has none.

fun font(size : Number = 12, bold : bool = false, italic : bool = false, weight : Number? = null) : Font#

This typeface at a size: Font(typeface, size, ...).

fun advance(glyph : int, weight : Number? = null) : Number#

In the font's own units, for a weight (which only a variable font has more than one of).

fun outline(glyph : int, weight : Number? = null) : open List<any?>#

A glyph's outline as a list: "M" x y, "L" x y, "Q" cx cy x y, "C" c1x c1y c2x c2y x y, "Z". In the font's units with y upwards; empty for a glyph that draws nothing.

fun check(step : int = 1, weight : Number? = null) : open List<int>#

Reads every stepth glyph's outline, to test the reader: [how many have one, how many points, how many are damaged].

op ==(other : any?) : bool#

Whether other is a Typeface of the same font.

fun hashCode() : int#

A hash code, the same for equal typefaces.

override fun toString() : string#

The typeface's full name in "Typeface(...)": "Typeface(Segoe UI Bold Italic)".

object Fonts#

The fonts installed on this machine.

fun families() : open List<string>#

Every family's name, in alphabetical order.

fun has(family : string) : bool#

Whether a family is installed. "SansSerif", "Serif" and "Monospaced" always are, if any font is: they are the system's own font of each kind.

fun typefaces(family : string? = null) : open List<Typeface>#

The typefaces of a family, or every one installed.

fun find(family : string, weight : Number = 400, italic : bool = false) : Typeface?#

The typeface of a family nearest to a weight and style; null if it is not installed.

fun fallback(character : any, weight : Number = 400, italic : bool = false) : Typeface?#

A typeface that has a character, for text in a font that lacks it; null if none has.

fun load(path : FilePath | string | Bytes, index : int = 0) : Typeface throws IOException#

A font from a file that is not installed, or from the bytes of one. A collection (.ttc) holds several: count says how many, index picks one. An IOException where the file cannot be read or is no font.

fun count(path : FilePath | string) : int#

How many fonts the file at path holds: more than 1 for a collection (.ttc), 0 if it cannot be read or is not a font file.

Fonts

class Font#

A font: a family or a Typeface, a size and its options, free of any screen's scale.

Font("Consolas", 13)                          by family; "SansSerif", "Serif" and
Font("Segoe UI", 12, bold = true)             "Monospaced" are the system's own
Font("Bahnschrift", 20, weight = 350)         any weight, where the font has it
Font(Fonts.load("my.ttf"), 16)                a typeface of your own
font.copy(size = 16, bold = false)            the same with some options changed

It is measured in layout units: font.width("text"), font.ascent, font.lineHeight. Where a screen's own pixels are wanted, the scale says how many make a unit: font.width("text", scale = 1.5), font.ascent(1.5). A canvas draws it at its own scale. A family that is not installed gives the system's sans-serif font (family says which was used); a style the family lacks is made up, a bold by thickening and an italic by slanting.

const dialog = "Dialog"#

"Dialog", Java's name for the system's sans-serif family.

const dialogInput = "DialogInput"#

"DialogInput", Java's name for the system's monospaced family.

const sansSerif = "SansSerif"#

"SansSerif", the system's sans-serif family, such as Segoe UI on Windows.

const serif = "Serif"#

"Serif", the system's serif family, such as Times New Roman on Windows.

const monospaced = "Monospaced"#

"Monospaced", the system's monospaced family, such as Consolas on Windows.

val name : string#

The family asked for (a name), or the typeface given.

val source : Typeface?#

The Typeface the font was made from; null when it was made from a family's name.

val size : Number#

The size in layout units: the height of the font's em, so a 14 is 14 pixels at scale 1.

val isBold : bool#

Whether bold was asked for.

val isItalic : bool#

Whether italic was asked for.

val weightAsked : Number?#

The weight asked for, from 100 to 900; null for the usual, 400, or 700 when bold.

fun copy(size : Number? = null, bold : bool? = null, italic : bool? = null, weight : Number? = null) : Font#

The same font with some of its options changed.

val family : string#

The family the font is drawn from: the one asked for if it is installed, otherwise the system's sans-serif.

val fontName : string#

The full name of the typeface used: "Segoe UI Bold".

val typeface : Typeface#

The typeface used: the one of the family nearest to the weight and style asked for.

val weight : Number#

The weight it is drawn at: the one asked for when the typeface has it (a variable font has its whole range), otherwise the nearest the family has.

val ascent : int#

From the baseline up to the top of a line, in layout units.

val descent : int#

From the baseline down to the bottom of a line, in layout units.

val leading : int#

The extra room the font asks for between one line and the next, in layout units; often 0.

val lineHeight : int#

The height of a line, in layout units: ascent, descent and leading together.

val xHeight : int#

The height of a small letter such as x, in layout units.

val capHeight : int#

The height of a capital letter, in layout units.

fun ascent(scale : Number) : int#

The ascent in the pixels of a screen with scale of them to a unit. It is measured at that size, so it is not always scale times ascent. A scale of 0 or less throws an IllegalArgumentException.

fun descent(scale : Number) : int#

The descent in the pixels of a screen with scale of them to a unit, measured at that size; a scale of 0 or less throws an IllegalArgumentException.

fun leading(scale : Number) : int#

The leading in the pixels of a screen with scale of them to a unit, measured at that size; a scale of 0 or less throws an IllegalArgumentException.

fun lineHeight(scale : Number) : int#

The lineHeight in the pixels of a screen with scale of them to a unit, measured at that size; a scale of 0 or less throws an IllegalArgumentException.

fun xHeight(scale : Number) : int#

The xHeight in the pixels of a screen with scale of them to a unit, measured at that size; a scale of 0 or less throws an IllegalArgumentException.

fun capHeight(scale : Number) : int#

The capHeight in the pixels of a screen with scale of them to a unit, measured at that size; a scale of 0 or less throws an IllegalArgumentException.

fun width(text : string | char, scale : Number = 1) : int#

The width of text, a string or a char, in layout units, or with scale in the pixels of a screen with that many to a unit: the sum of its characters' widths, and so where it ends when drawn.

fun outline(text : string, x : Number = 0, y : Number = 0, scale : Number = 1) : Path#

A line of text as a Path (N3), as Java's GlyphVector.getOutline: its glyphs' outlines, the start of its baseline at (x, y), in units; placed as the font places them at scale pixels to a unit. What a canvas fills to draw text through a transform; a colour glyph (an emoji) gives its shape, in one colour.

fun offsets(text : string | char, scale : Number = 1) : open List<int>#

Where each character of text starts and where the last one ends, in layout units or with scale in that screen's pixels: one number more than it has characters, the first 0.

font.offsets("abc")   [0, font.width("a"), font.width("ab"), font.width("abc")]
fun breakLines(text : string, width : Number, scale : Number = 1, breakWords : bool = false) : List<TextLine>#

The lines a paragraph breaks into at width, as text is laid out: measured in one call to the text engine (offsets), in layout units, or with scale in that screen's pixels, width too. A line breaks after spaces, after a hyphen between letters, and between Chinese, Japanese and Korean characters, never before a closing mark (, . ! ? ) and their wide forms); a line break in the text always breaks. The spaces a line ends with hang past its width. A word wider than a line is left whole unless breakWords says to break it between its characters, as CSS's overflow-wrap: break-word does. An empty text is one empty line.

for (line in font.breakLines(text, 300)) { canvas.drawString(text[line.start ..< line.end], 0, y); y += font.lineHeight }
fun typefaceFor(character : any) : Typeface?#

The typeface a character is drawn from: this font's own, or another one that has it when this one does not. Null if no installed font has it.

op ==(other : any?) : bool#

Whether other is a Font asked for in the same way: the same family name or typeface, size, bold, italic and weight. One made from a family's name is not equal to one made from its Typeface.

fun hashCode() : int#

A hash code, the same for equal fonts.

override fun toString() : string#

The font as it was asked for: "Font(Segoe UI, 14, bold)", "Font(Segoe UI, 14, weight 350)".

value TextLine(val start : int, val end : int, val width : int)#

One line of a paragraph as Font.breakLines breaks it: its characters from start up to end (the spaces it ends with among them, a line break not), and its width without those spaces.

foreign.ww

Foreign functions

Calling a function in a library of the system's (a DLL on Windows, a shared library on Linux), with whole numbers, pointers and text, and reading and writing the structures it takes in blocks of native memory:

val kernel32 = Library("kernel32")
val getPerformanceInfo = kernel32.function("K32GetPerformanceInfo", [CType.pointer, CType.int32], CType.int32)
Memory(104).use { info ->
  info.putInt32(0, 104)
  if (getPerformanceInfo.call(info, 104) != 0) { print("commit:", info.int64(8) * info.int64(80)) }
}
print(Foreign.lastError)            // what the system recorded, as GetLastError (errno on Linux) gives it

Reading and writing a Memory is checked against its size, as for Bytes; what a foreign function does with what it is given is not: a wrong declaration or a wrong pointer can end the program as it would in C. A sandbox refuses all of it. While a foreign function runs, the program's other threads go on.

enum CType#

What a foreign function's parameter or result is, as C declares it. A pointer takes a Memory, an address (an int) or null; wideString and utf8String take text (or null), handed over as UTF-16 or UTF-8 with a 0 after it for the length of the call; void is a result of nothing.

class Library#

A library of the system's, loaded once and kept while the program runs: Library("kernel32"), Library("libc.so.6"). On Linux "m" is tried as libm.so too. An IOException where there is none.

val name : string#

The name it was loaded by.

fun function(name : string, params : List<CType> = [], result : CType = CType.void) : ForeignFunction#

A function of the library, with what it takes and what it gives (nothing by default). An IllegalArgumentException where the library has no function of that name.

override fun toString() : string#

Library(kernel32).

class ForeignFunction#

A function of a library, found by Library.function.

val name : string#

Its name in the library.

val params : List<CType>#

What it takes.

val result : CType#

What it gives.

fun call(vararg args : any?) : int#

Calls it with as many arguments as it takes: whole numbers for numbers, a Memory, an address or null for a pointer, text or null for text. Gives its result as an int, cut to its type (0 for void).

override fun toString() : string#

ForeignFunction(K32GetPerformanceInfo).

object Foreign#

What foreign calls leave behind.

val lastError : int#

The error the system recorded during this thread's last foreign call: GetLastError on Windows, errno on Linux; 0 if it recorded none.

class Memory : AutoCloseable#

A block of native memory, all zero to begin with, for the structures and buffers a foreign function takes. Read and written at offsets in bytes, in the machine's order (the low byte first), each read and write checked against its size. use { } or close() frees it; one left open is freed when the collector finds nothing holds it.

val size : int#

How many bytes it has.

val address : int#

Where it is, for a pointer in a structure and for working out where in it a pointer the system gave back points: pointer - memory.address is an offset in it. An IllegalStateException once it is closed.

val isOpen : bool#

Whether it is still there to be used.

fun int8(at : int) : int#

The signed 8-bit number at at.

fun uint8(at : int) : int#

The unsigned 8-bit number at at.

fun int16(at : int) : int#

The signed 16-bit number at at.

fun uint16(at : int) : int#

The unsigned 16-bit number at at.

fun int32(at : int) : int#

The signed 32-bit number at at.

fun uint32(at : int) : int#

The unsigned 32-bit number at at.

fun int64(at : int) : int#

The 64-bit number at at.

fun pointer(at : int) : int#

The pointer at at, as an address.

fun putInt8(at : int, value : int)#

Writes a signed 8-bit number at at; an ArithmeticException where it does not fit.

fun putUint8(at : int, value : int)#

Writes an unsigned 8-bit number at at.

fun putInt16(at : int, value : int)#

Writes a signed 16-bit number at at.

fun putUint16(at : int, value : int)#

Writes an unsigned 16-bit number at at.

fun putInt32(at : int, value : int)#

Writes a signed 32-bit number at at.

fun putUint32(at : int, value : int)#

Writes an unsigned 32-bit number at at.

fun putInt64(at : int, value : int)#

Writes a 64-bit number at at.

fun putPointer(at : int, value : Memory? | int)#

Writes a pointer at at: another Memory's address, an address, or null for 0.

fun bytes(at : int, count : int) : Bytes#

A copy of count bytes from at.

fun putBytes(at : int, data : Bytes)#

Copies bytes in at at.

fun string16(at : int = 0, length : int = -1) : string#

The UTF-16 text at at: up to its 0, or length characters of it, within the block.

fun putString16(at : int, text : string)#

Writes text as UTF-16 at at, a 0 after it.

fun string8(at : int = 0, length : int = -1) : string#

The UTF-8 text at at: up to its 0, or length bytes of it, within the block.

fun putString8(at : int, text : string)#

Writes text as UTF-8 at at, a 0 after it.

override fun close()#

Frees it now; nothing may be read or written in it after.

override fun toString() : string#

Memory(104 bytes).

fun ofString16(text : string) : Memory#

A block holding text as UTF-16 with a 0 after it, for a structure that points at text.

gif.ww

GIF, and animations

class Animation#

An animation: whole pictures shown one after another, each for its delay, loops times over (0, for ever). Read from a GIF or a WebP (a still picture of another format is an animation of one frame), and written as either; how each frame is laid over the one before (GIF's disposal, WebP's blending) is worked out on reading, into whole frames, and an animation written is made into each format's frames of only what changed.

val spinner = Animation.load("spinner.gif")
for (frame in spinner.frames) { show(frame.image); sleep(frame.delay) }
val frames : List<AnimationFrame>#

Its frames, in order: at least one.

val loops : int#

How many times it plays, 0 for ever.

val width : int#

Its width, its frames'.

val height : int#

Its height, its frames'.

fun load(source : FilePath | string | Bytes) : Animation throws IOException#

Reads an animation from a file, or the bytes of one, whatever its format: a GIF's frames, or any other picture as one frame. An IOException where it cannot be read.

fun save(path : FilePath | string, quality : Number? = null) throws IOException#

Writes it as a GIF or WebP file, as its name's extension asks, at quality as encode. An IllegalArgumentException for another extension.

fun encode(format : ImageFormat, quality : Number? = null) : Bytes#

The bytes of an animated picture file of it in format: GIF or WebP. A WebP's frames are lossless at quality 1 (with none, too) and lossy below it, as Image.encode's; a GIF has no quality, its colours made at most 256 for each frame.

override fun toString() : string#

Its size, frames and loops, as Animation(16 x 12, 4 frames, loops 0).

class AnimationFrame(val image : Image, val delay : Duration)#

One frame of an animation: the whole picture as it is shown, and how long for.

override fun toString() : string#

Its size and delay, as AnimationFrame(16 x 12, 200ms).

graphics.ww

Colours and geometry

Value types: a Color, a Point, a Size, a Rect or an Insets is what it holds, with no identity, compared by its parts and copied where it is kept. Kept in a list, a field or a variable, it takes no allocation of its own. A var field changes through the place holding the value (bounds.x = 5), and so do the mutating methods (bounds.translate(1, 1)).

value Color(val red : byte, val green : byte, val blue : byte, val alpha : byte = 255) : Paint#

A colour: red, green and blue from 0 to 255, and how solid it is (alpha: 255 solid, 0 clear). Four bytes, red first, so a list or a grid of colours is the pixels themselves.

Color(255, 128, 0)            Color(255, 128, 0, 128)      half see-through
Color(0xFF8000)               Color(0x80FF8000, true)      one number, with its alpha
Color(1.0, 0.5, 0.0)          parts from 0.0 to 1.0        color.argb, 0xAARRGGBB
val white#

White: Color(255, 255, 255).

val lightGray#

Light grey: Color(192, 192, 192).

val gray#

Grey: Color(128, 128, 128).

val darkGray#

Dark grey: Color(64, 64, 64).

val black#

Black: Color(0, 0, 0).

val red#

Red: Color(255, 0, 0).

val pink#

Pink: Color(255, 175, 175).

val orange#

Orange: Color(255, 200, 0).

val yellow#

Yellow: Color(255, 255, 0).

val green#

Green: Color(0, 255, 0).

val magenta#

Magenta: Color(255, 0, 255).

val cyan#

Cyan: Color(0, 255, 255).

val blue#

Blue: Color(0, 0, 255).

val clear#

Nothing at all, wholly see-through: Color(0, 0, 0, 0).

fun decode(text : string) : Color#

"#RRGGBB" or "0xRRGGBB".

fun HSBtoRGB(hue : float, saturation : float, brightness : float) : int#

From hue, saturation and brightness, each from 0.0 to 1.0, as one number (0xFFRRGGBB). The hue goes round, so 1.25 is the same as 0.25; a saturation or a brightness outside 0.0 to 1.0, or a hue that is not a number or is infinite, is an IllegalArgumentException.

fun getHSBColor(hue : float, saturation : float, brightness : float) : Color#

The solid colour of a hue, a saturation and a brightness, each from 0.0 to 1.0; the hue goes round, so 1.25 is the same as 0.25.

fun RGBtoHSB(r : int, g : int, b : int) : List<float>#

The other way: [hue, saturation, brightness], from parts each from 0 to 255.

val argb : int#

The colour as one number, 0xAARRGGBB: Color(argb, true) makes it again.

fun brighter() : Color#

A lighter shade, by the step Java takes: each part divided by 0.7, at most 255, with the same alpha; black becomes Color(3, 3, 3).

fun darker() : Color#

A darker shade, by the step Java takes: each part times 0.7, rounded down, with the same alpha.

fun withAlpha(alpha : int) : Color#

The same colour, as see-through as alpha says.

override fun toString() : string#

The colour as text, "Color(255, 128, 0)", with the alpha last when it is not 255: "Color(255, 128, 0, 128)".

value Point(var x : int = 0, var y : int = 0)#

A point, in whole units.

mutating fun translate(dx : int, dy : int)#

Moves the point by dx across and dy down.

mutating fun setLocation(x : int, y : int)#

Moves the point to x, y.

override fun toString() : string#

The point as text: "Point(4, 1)".

value Size(var width : int = 0, var height : int = 0)#

A width and a height (Java's Dimension, D102).

val isEmpty : bool#

Whether the size holds nothing: its width or its height is 0 or less.

override fun toString() : string#

The size as text: "Size(30 x 40)".

value Insets(var top : int = 0, var left : int = 0, var bottom : int = 0, var right : int = 0)#

Top, left, bottom, right: the order Java gives them in; named arguments make it safe either way, Insets(top = 4, right = 8).

override fun toString() : string#

The insets as text: "Insets(top=1, left=2, bottom=3, right=4)".

interface Shape#

What a canvas draws as a shape, carrying its own place: canvas.draw(shape) its outline, canvas.fill(shape) the whole of it. As Java's shapes, each says where it is and whether a point is inside it, which is how a chart finds what the pointer is over.

val bounds : Rect#

The smallest rectangle of whole units that holds the whole shape.

fun contains(px : float, py : float) : bool#

Whether the point (px, py) is inside the shape, as Java's contains says it.

value Rect(var x : int, var y : int, var width : int, var height : int) : Shape#

A rectangle: its top left corner and its size, always all four.

val isEmpty : bool#

Whether the rectangle holds nothing: its width or its height is 0 or less.

val location : Point#

The top left corner, as a Point.

val size : Size#

The width and the height, as a Size.

val minX : int#

The left edge: x.

val minY : int#

The top edge: y.

val maxX : int#

Just past the right edge: x + width.

val maxY : int#

Just past the bottom edge: y + height.

val centerX : float#

The middle across, x + width / 2.0.

val centerY : float#

The middle down, y + height / 2.0.

override val bounds : Rect#

The rectangle itself, as a shape's bounds.

fun contains(px : int, py : int) : bool#

Whether the point px, py is inside: on the left or the top edge it is, on the right or the bottom edge it is not. An empty rectangle holds no point.

override fun contains(px : float, py : float) : bool#

The same for a point anywhere, not only on whole units.

fun contains(p : Point) : bool#

Whether the point p is inside, as for contains(p.x, p.y).

fun contains(rx : int, ry : int, w : int, h : int) : bool#

Whether the whole of the rectangle w by h at rx, ry is inside; never when either rectangle is empty.

fun contains(r : Rect) : bool#

Whether the whole of the rectangle r is inside; never when either rectangle is empty.

fun intersects(other : Rect) : bool#

Whether the two rectangles share any part; never when either is empty. Rectangles that only touch at an edge share none.

fun intersection(other : Rect) : Rect#

The part two rectangles share; an empty one if they share none.

fun union(other : Rect) : Rect#

The smallest rectangle holding both.

mutating fun translate(dx : int, dy : int)#

Moves the rectangle by dx across and dy down.

mutating fun grow(horizontal : int, vertical : int)#

Grows the rectangle by horizontal on the left and on the right, and by vertical at the top and at the bottom. Negative numbers shrink it, and can leave it empty.

mutating fun setBounds(r : Rect)#

Makes the rectangle the same as r.

mutating fun setBounds(x : int, y : int, width : int, height : int)#

Gives the rectangle a new top left corner and size.

mutating fun setLocation(x : int, y : int)#

Moves the top left corner to x, y, keeping the size.

mutating fun setSize(width : int, height : int)#

Gives the rectangle a new size, keeping its top left corner.

override fun toString() : string#

The rectangle as text: "Rect(10, 20, 30 x 40)".

value Oval(val x : float, val y : float, val width : float, val height : float) : Shape#

An oval filling a rectangle.

override val bounds : Rect#

The rectangle it fills, out to whole units.

override fun contains(px : float, py : float) : bool#

Whether the point is inside the oval (on its edge it is not), as Java's Ellipse2D says.

override fun toString() : string#

The oval as text, by the rectangle it fills: "Oval(1.0, 2.0, 3.0 x 4.5)".

value Line(val x1 : float, val y1 : float, val x2 : float, val y2 : float) : Shape#

A straight line from one point to another.

override val bounds : Rect#

The rectangle from one end to the other, out to whole units.

override fun contains(px : float, py : float) : bool#

Never: a line has no inside, as in Java.

override fun toString() : string#

The line as text: "Line(1.0, 2.0 to 3.0, 4.0)".

enum ArcKind#

How an Arc is closed, as Java's Arc2D: the curve alone, a straight line from its end back to its start, or lines from both ends to the middle (a slice of a pie).

value Arc(val x : float, val y : float, val width : float, val height : float, val start : float, val extent : float, val kind : ArcKind = ArcKind.open) : Shape#

A part of the oval filling a rectangle, as Java's Arc2D: from start degrees through extent more, 0 at three o'clock and counter-clockwise (an extent below 0 goes clockwise); on an oval 45 always points at the rectangle's top right corner. canvas.fill(arc) fills it (an open arc as a chord), canvas.draw(arc) draws its outline.

override val bounds : Rect#

The rectangle of the oval it is part of, out to whole units, as Java's Arc2D.getBounds() gives it.

fun containsAngle(degrees : float) : bool#

Whether an angle, in degrees, is inside the arc's sweep (its start included, its end not).

override fun contains(px : float, py : float) : bool#

Whether the point is inside the arc's shape, as Java's Arc2D says: inside the oval, and in the sweep for a pie, on the arc's side of its chord otherwise.

override fun toString() : string#

The arc as text: "Arc(0.0, 0.0, 10.0 x 10.0, 90.0 + 180.0, pie)".

interface Drawable#

What a canvas draws as a picture: an Image in memory, a Jade Surface on the graphics card. Its size is in units.

val width : int#

The picture's width, in units (an Image's are its pixels).

val height : int#

The picture's height, in units (an Image's are its pixels).

grid.ww

Grids

class Grid<T>#

A rectangle of cells, width by height, each a T: an image's pixels (a Grid<Color>), a map of walls (a Grid<bool>), the heights of a terrain (a Grid<float32>). It is packed by T as a List is: a Color 4 bytes a cell, a bool or a byte 1, an int16 2, a float32 4; a grid of objects keeps references.

val walls = Grid<bool>(width = 64, height = 64)        every cell false, the type's zero
walls[3, 4] = true
val heights = Grid<float32>(100, 100, fill = 1.0)
val part = heights[10..19, 0..9]                        a new grid of 10 by 10
walls .= appendRows(2)                                  two rows more, a new grid

A grid's size is fixed for its life: adding or removing rows and columns gives a new grid, and leaving that result unused is a compile error. A cell outside the grid is an IndexOutOfBoundsException, as a list's is; getOrNull gives null there. Work across cores goes by rows, so that no two pieces touch one cell: grid.parallelRows { rows -> ... }.

var width : int#

How many columns it has, fixed for the grid's life.

var height : int#

How many rows it has, fixed for the grid's life.

val size : Size#

Its width and height.

inline op [](x : int, y : int) : T#

The cell in column x of row y; an IndexOutOfBoundsException outside the grid. Put in place where it is used (B2).

inline op []=(x : int, y : int, value : T)#

Sets the cell in column x of row y: an IndexOutOfBoundsException outside the grid, an IllegalStateException on a grid that may only be read (an image's pixels).

inline fun get(x : int, y : int) : T#

The cell at (x, y), as grid[x, y] gives it.

inline fun set(x : int, y : int, value : T)#

Sets the cell at (x, y) to value, as grid[x, y] = value does.

inline fun getOrNull(x : int, y : int) : T?#

The cell, or null outside the grid: for work near the edges.

op [](xs : Range, ys : Range) : Grid<T>#

The cells of the columns xs and rows ys (inclusive, as a slice is, and counted from the end with ^k), as a new grid.

op []=(xs : Range, ys : Range, from : Grid<T>)#

Another grid written in over those columns and rows: it must be their size.

inline fun <R> map(transform : (T) -> R) : Grid<R>#

Each cell made into another, as a grid of the same size: pixels.map { it.alpha }. Inline, the lambda put in place (2026-11-13); the new grid's cells made first, each its type's zero, and each set where it is, so that the loop is a plain one (a grid of objects, which have no zero, has its cells added one by one).

fun toList() : open List<T>#

The cells, row by row, as a new list.

fun toBytes() : Bytes#

A copy of its storage, its cells' bytes one after another: for a grid of numbers, bools or value types (an image's pixels: four bytes each, red, green, blue, alpha). A function, as it copies everything (8.9f).

fun insertRows(at : int, count : int = 1, fill : T? = null) : Grid<T>#

count rows before row at (0 to height), each cell fill or T's zero.

fun insertRows(at : int, rows : Grid<T>) : Grid<T>#

The rows of another grid as wide as this one, before row at.

fun insertColumns(at : int, count : int = 1, fill : T? = null) : Grid<T>#

count columns before column at (0 to width).

fun insertColumns(at : int, columns : Grid<T>) : Grid<T>#

The columns of another grid as high as this one, before column at.

fun prependRows(count : int = 1, fill : T? = null) : Grid<T>#

count rows before the first, each cell fill or T's zero; a grid of objects needs a fill, or it is an IllegalArgumentException.

fun appendRows(count : int = 1, fill : T? = null) : Grid<T>#

count rows after the last, each cell fill or T's zero.

fun prependColumns(count : int = 1, fill : T? = null) : Grid<T>#

count columns before the first, each cell fill or T's zero.

fun appendColumns(count : int = 1, fill : T? = null) : Grid<T>#

count columns after the last, each cell fill or T's zero.

fun removeRows(at : Index, count : int = 1) : Grid<T>#

Without count rows from row at, which may count from the end: removeRows(^1).

fun removeColumns(at : Index, count : int = 1) : Grid<T>#

Without count columns from column at, which may count from the end: removeColumns(^1). An IndexOutOfBoundsException when they are not all in the grid.

fun parallelRows(action : (Range) -> unit)#

Runs action on every core, each given rows of its own to work on: what it changes it changes only in those rows.

op ==(other : any?) : bool#

Whether other is a grid of the same size with equal cells in the same places.

fun hashCode() : int#

A hash of its size and its cells: equal grids give equal hashes.

override fun toString() : string#

Its size, as Grid(64 x 64), not its cells.

image.ww

Images and canvases

data TextMetrics(val width : int, val ascent : int, val descent : int, val height : int)#

What a text takes up in a font, in units.

abstract class Canvas#

What everything is drawn on: an Image's canvas in memory (ImageCanvas, below), and in Jade a window's or a Surface's on the graphics card, or one that records what it is asked to draw. All have the same calls:

val canvas = image.canvas()
canvas.color = Color.white
canvas.fillRect(0, 0, canvas.width, canvas.height)
canvas.fill(Rect(10, 10, 80, 40))             a shape carries its own place (D106)
canvas.draw(photo, 100, 10)                   a picture, an Image or a Surface
canvas.font = Font("Segoe UI", 14)
canvas.drawString("Hello", 20, 60)            the text's baseline at y

What is drawn is given in units; a canvas has scale pixels to the unit. fillRect(x, y, w, h) covers w by h units; drawRect is an outline one unit thick that covers w + 1 by h + 1, as in Java; shapes have smooth edges, and rectangles and straight lines sit on whole pixels. Outlines and lines are drawn with the canvas's stroke, lineWidth units thick, 1 to begin with (M8, N3). A canvas for a part of another (sub, clipped) draws only inside it.

This class holds what every kind has (its size, scale, place, clip, colour and font); each kind draws the shapes, the text and the pictures, in its own pixels.

val width : int#

Its width, in units.

val height : int#

Its height, in units.

val scale : Number#

How many of its pixels make a unit: 1 for an image's canvas(), more on a dense screen (1.5, 2).

protected val originX : int#

Where its (0, 0) is across the pixels drawn on, in pixels.

protected val originY : int#

Where its (0, 0) is down the pixels drawn on, in pixels.

protected val clipLeft : int#

The first column of pixels it may draw on.

protected val clipTop : int#

The first row of pixels it may draw on.

protected val clipRight : int#

The column just past the last one it may draw on.

protected val clipBottom : int#

The row just past the last one it may draw on.

var color : Color#

The colour that shapes and text are drawn in: black to begin with. Setting it makes it the paint, as Java's setColor does.

var paint : Paint#

What shapes, outlines and text are filled with (N3), as Java's setPaint: the colour, or a LinearGradient or RadialGradient, whose points go through the transform as what is drawn does. Setting a Color sets color. Pictures are drawn as they are, whatever the paint.

var composite : Composite#

How what is drawn is put together with what is there (N3): laid over it (srcOver, to begin with), or clearing it where it would have been drawn (clear), as Java's setComposite.

var interpolation : Interpolation#

How pictures are read between their pixels when they are drawn larger, smaller or turned (N3): bilinear to begin with.

var font : Font#

The font that text is drawn in: Dialog at 12 to begin with.

var stroke : Stroke#

How outlines and lines are drawn (N3): their width, ends, corners and dashes, as Java's Graphics2D.setStroke. Stroke(), Java's default, to begin with: 1 unit wide, square ends, miter corners.

The outlines of drawRect, drawOval, drawLine and the rest keep to whole pixels as Java's do: the line a coordinate names runs through the middle of the pixels from it to it plus one, and a wider one spreads around it, its larger half to the right and below, its width made whole pixels. draw(path) strokes a path where it says, as fill(path) fills it.

var lineWidth : Number#

How thick outlines and lines are, in units: the stroke's width, 1 to begin with. Setting it keeps the rest of the stroke, as Java's BasicStroke(w) would not.

val deviceScaleX : float#

How many pixels there are to a unit.

val deviceScaleY : float#

How many pixels there are to a unit down: the same as across, scale.

val visibleBounds : Rect#

The part of the canvas that can be drawn on, in units: the rest is clipped away.

fun sub(x : int, y : int, width : int, height : int) : Canvas#

A canvas for a part of this one: its (0, 0) is at (x, y) here, and nothing it draws lands outside the part. Through a transform, the part is where the transform puts it, and the new canvas goes through the transform too.

fun clipped(x : Number, y : Number, w : Number, h : Number) : Canvas#

The same canvas, drawing only inside a rectangle of it, where the transform puts it.

fun clipped(shape : Shape) : Canvas#

The same canvas, drawing only inside a shape (N3), as Java's Graphics2D.clip: a Rect, an Oval, an Arc or a Path, through the transform, its edge smooth; inside whatever it was clipped to already. An IllegalArgumentException for any other shape.

protected abstract fun another(width : int, height : int, originX : int, originY : int, clipLeft : int, clipTop : int, clipRight : int, clipBottom : int) : Canvas#

A canvas of this kind over the same pixels, at another place, with another clip.

var transform : Transform#

What is done to everything drawn before it lands on the canvas: moved, turned, stretched or slanted, as Java's Graphics2D does with its transform. Transform.identity to begin with.

canvas.save()
canvas.translate(60, 40)
canvas.rotate(Math.pi / 6)
canvas.fillRect(-20, -10, 40, 20)      a rectangle turned about (60, 40)
canvas.restore()

Everything goes through it: shapes, filled or their outlines drawn, and paths; text, as its glyphs' outlines when it is turned or stretched; pictures, read between their pixels by the canvas's interpolation; sub and clipped. An outline's width stretches as what is drawn stretches.

fun save()#

Keeps the transform, the paint, the font, the stroke, the composite and the interpolation, for restore() to put back. Saves can be inside each other: each restore() puts back the last one not yet put back.

fun restore()#

Puts back what the last save() kept. An IllegalStateException with nothing kept.

fun translate(dx : Number, dy : Number)#

Moves what is drawn from now on by dx across and dy down, inside what the transform already does: transform = transform.translate(dx, dy).

fun rotate(angle : Number, cx : Number = 0, cy : Number = 0)#

Turns what is drawn from now on by angle radians, clockwise, about (cx, cy), inside what the transform already does: transform = transform.rotate(angle, cx, cy).

fun fillRect(x : Number, y : Number, w : Number, h : Number)#

Fills the rectangle of w by h units whose top-left corner is at (x, y), in the canvas's colour.

fun drawRect(x : Number, y : Number, w : Number, h : Number)#

The outline of that rectangle, one unit thick, covering w + 1 by h + 1 units, as Java's does.

fun clearRect(x : Number, y : Number, w : Number, h : Number)#

Makes a rectangle clear again: what is under the picture shows through it.

fun fillRoundRect(x : Number, y : Number, w : Number, h : Number, arcW : Number, arcH : Number)#

Fills a rectangle with rounded corners, each corner a quarter of an oval arcW wide and arcH high.

fun drawRoundRect(x : Number, y : Number, w : Number, h : Number, arcW : Number, arcH : Number)#

The outline of a rectangle with rounded corners, one unit thick, covering w + 1 by h + 1 units as drawRect does.

fun fillOval(x : Number, y : Number, w : Number, h : Number)#

Fills the oval that fits inside the rectangle of w by h units at (x, y).

fun drawOval(x : Number, y : Number, w : Number, h : Number)#

The outline of that oval, one unit thick, covering w + 1 by h + 1 units.

fun drawLine(x1 : Number, y1 : Number, x2 : Number, y2 : Number)#

A line one unit thick from (x1, y1) to (x2, y2), both ends included: with the stroke's square ends, as Java's default; its ends are the stroke's.

fun drawPolyline(xs : List<Number>, ys : List<Number>, n : int? = null)#

Lines from point to point of (xs[i], ys[i]), the first n of them or all when n is null, left open at the last point.

fun drawPolygon(xs : List<Number>, ys : List<Number>, n : int? = null)#

The same lines closed: a last one goes from the last point back to the first.

fun fillPolygon(xs : List<Number>, ys : List<Number>, n : int? = null)#

Fills the polygon whose corners are those points.

fun fillArc(x : Number, y : Number, w : Number, h : Number, startAngle : Number, arcAngle : Number)#

Fills a slice of the oval in the rectangle, as Java's fillArc: from startAngle degrees through arcAngle more, 0 at three o'clock and counter-clockwise.

fun drawArc(x : Number, y : Number, w : Number, h : Number, startAngle : Number, arcAngle : Number)#

The curve of that part of the oval's outline, as Java's drawArc: as drawOval draws it, one line width thick.

fun draw(shape : Shape)#

A shape's outline, drawn with the canvas's stroke: a Rect's, an Oval's or an Arc's as drawRect, drawOval and drawArc draw them, a Line as drawLine does, a Path's where it says (D106, N3); an IllegalArgumentException for any other shape.

fun fill(shape : Shape)#

The whole of a shape: a Rect, an Oval, an Arc or a Path filled, a Line drawn; an IllegalArgumentException for any other shape. A Path is filled by its fill rule, each of its contours closed.

protected abstract fun plainFillRect(x : Number, y : Number, w : Number, h : Number)#

What each kind of canvas draws, the transform done already: the calls above, through no transform.

protected abstract fun plainDrawRect(x : Number, y : Number, w : Number, h : Number)#

drawRect, through no transform.

protected abstract fun plainClearRect(x : Number, y : Number, w : Number, h : Number)#

clearRect, through no transform.

protected abstract fun plainFillRoundRect(x : Number, y : Number, w : Number, h : Number, arcW : Number, arcH : Number)#

fillRoundRect, through no transform.

protected abstract fun plainDrawRoundRect(x : Number, y : Number, w : Number, h : Number, arcW : Number, arcH : Number)#

drawRoundRect, through no transform.

protected abstract fun plainFillOval(x : Number, y : Number, w : Number, h : Number)#

fillOval, through no transform.

protected abstract fun plainDrawOval(x : Number, y : Number, w : Number, h : Number)#

drawOval, through no transform.

protected abstract fun plainDrawLine(x1 : Number, y1 : Number, x2 : Number, y2 : Number)#

drawLine, through no transform.

protected abstract fun plainDrawPolyline(xs : List<Number>, ys : List<Number>, n : int?)#

drawPolyline, through no transform.

protected abstract fun plainDrawPolygon(xs : List<Number>, ys : List<Number>, n : int?)#

drawPolygon, through no transform.

protected abstract fun plainFillPolygon(xs : List<Number>, ys : List<Number>, n : int?)#

fillPolygon, through no transform.

protected abstract fun arcShape(x : Number, y : Number, w : Number, h : Number, start : Number, extent : Number, kind : ArcKind, fill : bool)#

What fillArc, drawArc and an Arc ask of each kind of canvas, through no transform: the part of the oval from start through extent degrees, filled as fillOval fills (a slice for a pie, the arc and its chord otherwise) or its outline drawn as drawOval draws (the curve, and the chord or the two lines to the middle).

protected abstract fun plainFillPath(path : Path)#

A path filled by its fill rule, each contour closed, its points already through the transform: path.flatten(scale) gives it in straight lines, fine enough for this canvas's pixels.

protected abstract fun plainLay(cells : List<Color>, width : int, height : int, left : int, top : int, clear : bool)#

Pixels laid on, pixel for pixel (N3): cells, width by height of them row by row, red, green, blue and alpha not multiplied by alpha, at (left, top) in the pixels drawn on, inside the clip; laid over what is there, or with clear clearing it by their alpha. What a layer drew (a gradient, a clip to a shape, the Clear composite) and a picture drawn through a transform are laid on so.

fun drawString(text : string, x : Number, y : Number) : int#

Text in the canvas's font and colour, starting at x with its baseline at y: letters sit on that row and tails hang below it. Gives the width it took, in units.

protected abstract fun plainDrawString(text : string, x : Number, y : Number) : int#

drawString, through no transform.

fun measure(text : string, font : Font = this.font) : TextMetrics#

What a text takes up in a font, on this canvas: measured in its pixels, in units rounded up.

fun draw(picture : Drawable, x : Number, y : Number, w : Number? = null, h : Number? = null, alpha : Number = 1.0)#

A picture at (x, y): at its own size, or scaled into w by h; alpha below 1.0 makes it see-through.

fun drawPart(picture : Drawable, sourceX : Number, sourceY : Number, sourceW : Number, sourceH : Number, x : Number, y : Number, w : Number, h : Number, alpha : Number = 1.0)#

A part of a picture, given in its own pixels, put into a rectangle.

protected abstract fun drawPicture(picture : Drawable, x : Number, y : Number, w : Number?, h : Number?, alpha : Number)#

What draw(picture, ...) asks of each kind of canvas: the picture at (x, y), at its own size or scaled into w by h, see-through by alpha.

protected abstract fun drawPicturePart(picture : Drawable, sourceX : Number, sourceY : Number, sourceW : Number, sourceH : Number, x : Number, y : Number, w : Number, h : Number, alpha : Number)#

What drawPart asks of each kind of canvas: the part of picture from (sourceX, sourceY), sourceW by sourceH of its own pixels, put into w by h units at (x, y).

fun dispose()#

Lets go of what the canvas holds. A canvas needs no disposing; some kinds may use it.

enum ImageFormat#

The kinds of picture file Image.load reads and save and encode write (M9/M10).

fun of(data : Bytes) : ImageFormat?#

The format of a file's bytes, by how they start; null for none of them.

fun ofName(name : string) : ImageFormat?#

The format a file's name asks for, by its extension (.png, .jpg or .jpeg, .gif, .bmp, .webp, in either case); null for another.

enum ColorSpace#

How a picture's colours are kept as planes, one byte a sample each (Image.fromPlanes, image.toPlanes): one plane of grey (luma, as ITU-R BT.601 weighs red, green and blue); red, green and blue; or YCbCr as JPEG (JFIF) has it, full range, Cb and Cr centred on 128.

value Plane(val bytes : Bytes, val width : int, val height : int, val stride : int = width)#

A plane of one byte a sample, width by height, its rows stride bytes apart from the start of bytes (Image.fromScaledPlanes): a JPEG's chroma at half size, say, or a plane in a buffer wider than its picture.

class Image : Drawable#

A picture in memory: width by height pixels, each a Color that may be see-through. What a program makes, reads and changes a pixel at a time, draws on with its canvas, loads from a picture file and saves as one (Java's BufferedImage, D122).

val image = Image(200, 100, Color.white)
image.canvas().fill(Oval(20.0, 20.0, 60.0, 60.0))
image[10, 10] = Color.red                     one pixel; get, set and getOrNull too
image.editPixels { pixels -> pixels[3, 4] = Color.blue }    many at once, a Grid<Color>
image.save(FilePath("out.png"))

A pixel outside the image is an IndexOutOfBoundsException. Its canvas draws in memory, on any thread, so a worker can draw a thumbnail; Jade keeps a copy of it on the graphics card for drawing it there, brought up to date from what has changed.
