Threads may draw on one image together: each shape, text or picture is drawn whole before another thread draws, and the copy on the card misses none of them. In a parallel section a piece draws on an image it made itself, or through a part of a split canvas handed to it (ImageCanvas.split, G64).

override var width : int#

Its width, in pixels.

override var height : int#

Its height, in pixels.

fun of(width : int, height : int, pixels : Bytes) : Image#

An image of pixels given as bytes, four to a pixel: red, green, blue, alpha, top row first.

fun fromPlanes(width : int, height : int, planes : List<Bytes>, space : ColorSpace = ColorSpace.rgb) : Image#

A solid picture made from planes of one byte a sample, each width by height, row by row: one for grey, three for red, green and blue or Y, Cb and Cr. An IllegalArgumentException for planes of another size or number.

fun fromScaledPlanes(width : int, height : int, planes : List<Plane>, space : ColorSpace = ColorSpace.rgb) : Image#

The same from planes each of its own size, scaled to the picture's as Bytes.scaledPlane scales (twice either way by libjpeg's triangle filter, say), a row at a time as the colours are worked out: no plane is made at the picture's size. An IllegalArgumentException for a plane smaller than its size says, or planes of another number.

Image.fromScaledPlanes(w, h, [Plane(y, w, h), Plane(cb, w / 2, h / 2), Plane(cr, w / 2, h / 2)], ColorSpace.ycbcr)
fun load(source : FilePath | string | Bytes) : Image throws IOException#

Reads a picture file, or the bytes of one, whatever its format (ImageFormat), known by its content rather than its name. An IOException where it cannot be read or is no picture.

val size : Size#

Its width and height.

inline op [](x : int, y : int) : Color#

A pixel, as a Color; outside the image an IndexOutOfBoundsException, or null for getOrNull. Put in place where it is used (B2), and read from the grid's list where it is: the pixel checked here once, not again by the grid.

inline op []=(x : int, y : int, color : Color)#

Sets a pixel to color, which takes its place, alpha and all, rather than being laid over it; an IndexOutOfBoundsException outside the image. Set in the grid's list where it is, as op [] reads it.

inline fun get(x : int, y : int) : Color#

The pixel at (x, y), as image[x, y] gives it.

inline fun set(x : int, y : int, color : Color)#

Sets the pixel at (x, y) to color, as image[x, y] = color does.

inline fun getOrNull(x : int, y : int) : Color?#

The pixel at (x, y), or null outside the image.

val pixels : Grid<Color>#

All of them, to be read: the same grid as the image's, costing no copy.

fun editPixels(block : (Grid<Color>) -> unit)#

All of them, to be changed in the block: the image is marked changed when it ends.

fun canvas() : ImageCanvas#

Something to draw on it with, one unit to the pixel.

fun canvasAt(width : int, height : int, scale : Number) : ImageCanvas#

The same for an image that stands for width by height units at scale pixels to the unit: what a window's picture is on a dense screen.

fun crop(x : int, y : int, w : int, h : int) : Image#

A part of it as an image of its own; what is outside the image is clear. The part inside copied a row at a time (2026-11-14; was C++'s), each row one move in native code.

fun copy() : Image#

A new image of the same size with the same pixels, changed apart from this one. Its pixels copied once (2026-11-13: as a crop, a clear image was made first and then written over).

fun blurred(radius : Number) : Image#

A copy blurred by a Gaussian whose standard deviation is radius pixels, as CSS's blur() and SVG's stdDeviation (N3): each pixel a mix of those round it out to three times that, across and then down, their colours weighed by their alpha so that a see-through edge does not darken, the edge pixels repeated beyond the edges. An IllegalArgumentException for a radius below 0. Worked out in doubles, across and then down with nothing rounded between (2026-11-15; was C++'s, in single precision, rounded to bytes between the two).

fun scaled(width : int, height : int, interpolation : Interpolation = Interpolation.bilinear) : Image#

The picture at another size: each way it shrinks, each new pixel the average of those it covers; each way it grows, read between pixels by interpolation; colours weighed by alpha. An IllegalArgumentException for a size below 0.

fun transformed(transform : Transform, interpolation : Interpolation = Interpolation.bilinear) : Image#

The picture moved, turned, stretched or slanted by transform, in a new image just large enough to hold all of it, its top left corner where the transformed picture's leftmost and topmost points are; what is around it clear, its edges smooth. Read between pixels by interpolation. An IllegalStateException for a transform that flattens everything onto a line.

fun adjusted(brightness : Number = 1, contrast : Number = 1, saturation : Number = 1) : Image#

The picture with its brightness, contrast and saturation changed, as CSS's brightness(), contrast() and saturate() change them: 1 leaves each as it is; 0 makes the picture black, flat grey, or grey; more than 1 makes it brighter, harder, more colourful. Alpha is kept.

fun greyscale() : Image#

The picture in greys, as CSS's grayscale(1): each pixel 0.2126 of its red, 0.7152 of its green and 0.0722 of its blue, as the eye weighs them; alpha kept.

fun inverted() : Image#

The picture with its colours turned round, each part 255 less itself; alpha kept. Through mapChannels, a table for each part (2026-11-13: a loop over the bytes took 71 ms for 12 million pixels, this 16).

fun floodRegion(x : int, y : int, tolerance : int = 0) : Mask#

The pixels joined to (x, y) through pixels whose colour differs from its own by no more than tolerance in any part, alpha too: the magic wand's selection, a flood fill's area. Filled with image.editPixels { it.fill(color, where = mask) }.

fun inkBox(background : Color = Color.clear) : Rect?#

The smallest rectangle outside which every pixel is background; null if all of them are.

fun save(path : FilePath | string, quality : Number? = null) throws IOException#

Writes it as a picture file of the format its name's extension asks for (ImageFormat.ofName), PNG where it asks for none: quality, from 0 to 1, for the formats that lose some of the picture to be smaller (JPEG, lossy WebP), null for the format's own (as encode). An IllegalArgumentException for an extension of another format.

fun encode(format : ImageFormat, quality : Number? = null) : Bytes#

The bytes of a picture file of it in format, at quality (0 to 1) where the format loses some of the picture. PNG, BMP and lossless forms lose nothing: loading them gives the same pixels. WebP is lossless at quality 1 and lossy below it, its alpha kept exactly either way; its quality gives about the size and loss libwebp's does at 100 times it. With no quality, JPEG's is 0.9 and WebP's 1, lossless.

fun toPlanes(space : ColorSpace = ColorSpace.rgb) : List<Bytes>#

Its colours as planes, one byte a sample, each width by height, row by row: one for grey, three for red, green and blue or Y, Cb and Cr. Alpha is left out.

fun rgbaBytes() : Bytes#

Its pixels as bytes, four to a pixel: red, green, blue, alpha, top row first, as Image.of takes them. A copy: changing it changes nothing here.

val isOpaque : bool#

Whether every pixel is solid, an alpha of 255: what an encoder asks before leaving alpha out. A look at each pixel, with nothing copied.

fun writePixels(to : Bytes, at : int = 0, order : string = "RGBA", rowStride : int = 0, bottomUp : bool = false) : Bytes#

Its pixels written into to from at, each pixel's channels in order (any of R, G, B and A, one to four of them: "BGR" for a BMP's, "RGBA" as rgbaBytes gives them), a row every rowStride bytes (a row's own bytes when 0; what is between rows is left as it is), the top row first or, with bottomUp, the bottom one. Nothing else is made: a file's header and its pixels in one set of bytes. Gives to. An IndexOutOfBoundsException where they would run past its end, an IllegalArgumentException for another order.

val out = Bytes(54 + stride * height)
image.writePixels(out, 54, "BGR", stride, bottomUp = true)
fun toPng() : Bytes#

The bytes of a PNG file of it. An image with no pixels (0 wide or 0 high) is an IllegalArgumentException, here and in save.

op ==(other : any?) : bool#

The same size and the same pixels.

fun hashCode() : int#

A hash of its size and its pixels: equal images give equal hashes. It reads every pixel.

override fun toString() : string#

Its size, as Image(200 x 100).

class ImageCanvas : Canvas#

What draws on an Image: the software engine, in the image's pixels.

fun split(columns : int = 1, rows : int = availableProcessors()) : List<ImageCanvas>#

The canvas in columns by rows parts that do not overlap, for drawing on the image on several cores at once: each part draws as this canvas does, in its units from its (0, 0), and keeps what lands in its own part (its visibleBounds). Handed to the pieces of a parallel section, a part to a piece, they draw together; meanwhile nothing else reads the image or draws on it.

canvas.split().parallelStream().forEach { part -> drawScene(part) }

Split by rows, as it is unless told otherwise, each core works out only its own rows of a shape. The parts are cut in pixels, as evenly as can be, and there are no more than the canvas has pixels each way. Once the image's canvas is split again, these parts draw outside parallel sections only.

protected override fun another(width : int, height : int, originX : int, originY : int, clipLeft : int, clipTop : int, clipRight : int, clipBottom : int) : Canvas#

An ImageCanvas over the same image at another place, with another clip: what sub and clipped give. One made from a part is a part too, inside it, so it is made only by the code that may draw through the part: in a parallel section, the piece it was handed to.

protected override fun plainFillRect(x : Number, y : Number, w : Number, h : Number)#

Fills the rectangle in the image's pixels, laid over what is there by the colour's alpha; nothing when w or h is 0 or less.

protected override fun plainDrawRect(x : Number, y : Number, w : Number, h : Number)#

The rectangle's outline, one unit thick, covering w + 1 by h + 1 units; nothing when w or h is below 0.

protected override fun plainClearRect(x : Number, y : Number, w : Number, h : Number)#

Makes the pixels of the rectangle clear, all four bytes 0, its edges on whole pixels, inside the clip.

protected override fun plainFillRoundRect(x : Number, y : Number, w : Number, h : Number, arcW : Number, arcH : Number)#

Fills a rectangle with rounded corners, smooth at its edges, laid over what is there.

protected override fun plainDrawRoundRect(x : Number, y : Number, w : Number, h : Number, arcW : Number, arcH : Number)#

The outline of a rectangle with rounded corners, one unit thick.

protected override fun plainFillOval(x : Number, y : Number, w : Number, h : Number)#

Fills the oval inside the rectangle, smooth at its edge, laid over what is there.

protected override fun plainDrawOval(x : Number, y : Number, w : Number, h : Number)#

The outline of the oval inside the rectangle, one unit thick, covering w + 1 by h + 1 units.

protected override fun plainDrawLine(x1 : Number, y1 : Number, x2 : Number, y2 : Number)#

A line one unit thick with square ends: one across or up and down sits on whole pixels, a slanted one has smooth edges.

protected override fun plainDrawPolyline(xs : List<Number>, ys : List<Number>, n : int?)#

Lines through the first n points, or all of them, left open; n is cut down to the shorter list, and fewer than 2 points draw nothing.

protected override fun plainDrawPolygon(xs : List<Number>, ys : List<Number>, n : int?)#

Lines through the first n points, or all of them, closed back to the first; fewer than 2 points draw nothing.

protected override fun plainFillPolygon(xs : List<Number>, ys : List<Number>, n : int?)#

Fills the polygon through the first n points, or all of them; fewer than 2 draw nothing.

protected override fun arcShape(x : Number, y : Number, w : Number, h : Number, start : Number, extent : Number, kind : ArcKind, fill : bool)#

A part of the oval, filled or its outline drawn, smooth at its edges.

protected override fun plainLay(cells : List<Color>, width : int, height : int, left : int, top : int, clear : bool)#

Pixels laid over the image's, or clearing them.

protected override fun plainFillPath(path : Path)#

A path, smooth at its edges, laid over what is there.

protected override fun plainDrawString(text : string, x : Number, y : Number) : int#

Draws text in the canvas's font and colour, its baseline at y, and gives the width it took, in units rounded up.

protected override fun drawPicture(picture : Drawable, x : Number, y : Number, w : Number?, h : Number?, alpha : Number)#

Draws an Image, or a picture that can give one (a ToImage), at its own size in units or scaled into w by h; an IllegalArgumentException for a picture that can do neither.

protected override fun drawPicturePart(picture : Drawable, sourceX : Number, sourceY : Number, sourceW : Number, sourceH : Number, x : Number, y : Number, w : Number, h : Number, alpha : Number)#

Draws the part of the picture's pixels into the rectangle, smoothly when it is made larger or smaller; alpha is kept from 0.0 to 1.0, and nothing is drawn at 0 or for an empty part.

interface ToImage#

A picture kept somewhere other than in memory that can give its pixels as an Image: a Jade Surface, from the graphics card.

fun toImage() : Image#

Its pixels, as a new Image: a Surface's at its size in pixels.

io.ww

Readers, writers, processes and sockets

Reading and writing a piece at a time, programs started from this one, and connections over the network.

Anything here that waits (a read, an accept, waitFor) is written as an ordinary call:

- on a thread it blocks that thread, and the other threads go on;
- in a task on the main thread the task is set aside meanwhile, and the event loop goes
  on, so a window stays live;
- on the main thread outside a task it blocks the main thread.

Character sets

enum Charset#

What text is read and written in. WinterWolf's own text is UTF-8, the default everywhere; the others are for files, programs and protocols that use them (mail's Latin-1 and Windows-1252, Windows' UTF-16). A character the set has no place for is written as '?', and bytes that spell no character are read as U+FFFD. utf16 is Java's "UTF-16": read in the order a byte order mark says, big-endian where there is none, and written big-endian after a mark; utf16le and utf16be take no mark. latin9 is ISO-8859-15, Latin-1 with the euro.

Reader and Writer

interface ByteChannel#

A connection that WinterWolf code carries rather than the system: a TLS connection of import Crypto is one (networking plan, part 5). A Reader or Writer made over one reads and writes it as it would a socket, so code that reads lines from a Socket's reader reads them from a TlsSocket's the same way.

fun receive(count : int) : Bytes? throws IOException#

Up to count bytes (one or more), waiting until there are some; null at the end.

fun send(data : Bytes) throws IOException#

All of data, returning when it has been handed on.

fun closeChannel()#

Closes it; closing it again does nothing.

val isChannelOpen : bool#

Whether it has not been closed yet.

class Reader : AutoCloseable#

Something to read from, bytes or text: a file, what a program writes, a socket, a ByteChannel. It reads ahead into a buffer of its own, so there is nothing to wrap it in.

var charset : Charset#

What text is read in; a change takes effect from the next read.

fun read(count : int = 65536) : Bytes? throws IOException#

Up to count bytes as soon as there are any; null at the end.

fun readBytes() : Bytes throws IOException#

Everything up to the end, as bytes or as text.

fun readText() : string throws IOException#

Everything up to the end as text in charset; "" when nothing is left.

fun readLine() : string? throws IOException#

The next line without its line end; null at the end.

fun readLines() : open List<string> throws IOException#

The lines that are left.

fun forEachLine(action : (string) -> unit) throws IOException#

Calls action with each line that is left, without its line end, as it is read.

fun lineSequence() : Sequence<string>#

The lines that are left as a sequence, each read when it is asked for. A read that fails there throws an UncheckedIOException, its cause the IOException.

fun <R> useLines(block : (Sequence<string>) -> R) : R throws IOException#

The block given the lines as a sequence, read as it goes; the reader is closed afterwards.

override fun close()#

Closes it; closing it again does nothing. A read after that is an IOException.

val isOpen : bool#

Whether it has not been closed yet.

class Writer : AutoCloseable#

Something to write to, bytes or text: a file, what a program reads, a socket, a ByteChannel. A write returns when the system (or the channel) has taken all of it, so flush() has nothing left to do.

var charset : Charset#

What text is written in; a change takes effect from the next write.

fun write(data : any?) throws IOException#

Text, or bytes. Anything else is written as print would show it.

fun print(value : any?) throws IOException#

Writes value as print would show it (null as "null"), with no line end.

fun println(value : any? = "") throws IOException#

Writes value as print would show it, then "\n"; with no value, only the "\n".

fun newLine() throws IOException#

Writes a line end, "\n".

fun append(value : any?) : Writer throws IOException#

Writes value as print would show it and gives the writer, for more:

out.append("total: ").append(sum)
fun flush()#

Does nothing: each write has already handed all of it to the system.

override fun close()#

Closes it; closing it again does nothing. A write after that is an IOException. Closing a program's input is how it reads the end.

val isOpen : bool#

Whether it has not been closed yet.

Processes

enum Stream#

Where a stream of a program started from this one goes: pipe (this program reads or writes it, the default), inherit (the same place as this program's own), discard (nowhere), and for errors toOutput (into the output). A FilePath in their place reads or writes that file.

class Process#

A program started from this one.

val git = Process("git", "status", directory = repo)
for line in git.output.readLines() { print(line) }
print("exit code", git.waitFor())

val result = Process.run("git", "status", directory = repo)   // runs it to its end
if (result.exitCode == 0) { print(result.output) }

The command is the program and its arguments, as separate words or one list. environment holds variables added to this program's own (null takes one out). input is what the program reads, a Writer; output and error what it writes, Readers; each is there when it is a pipe.

The program and every program it starts in turn are one tree (2026-11-20): kill() and a timeout end all of it at once, so nothing it started runs on. With limits, the tree is held to them together, and ended where it goes past one, and when this program ends:

Process.run(ww, "test.ww", limits = ProcessLimits(memory = 8 * 1024 * 1024 * 1024, processes = 64))
fun run(vararg command : any, directory : (FilePath | string)? = null, environment : Map<string, string?>? = null, input : Stream | FilePath = Stream.discard, error : Stream | FilePath = Stream.pipe, charset : Charset = Charset.utf8, timeout : Duration? = null, limits : ProcessLimits? = null) : ProcessResult throws IOException | TimeoutException#

Runs a program to its end and gives what it wrote, as text: Process(...)'s arguments, its output and errors read whatever they are said to be, and its input nothing unless said. With a timeout, a program that runs longer is killed with all it started, and run throws a TimeoutException (8.8h). With limits, as Process's; the result says which one the program went past, if any.

fun currentCommand() : open List<string>#

What started this program: the path of its executable and, when it is a script run by ww, the script. Process(Process.currentCommand() + ["--second"]) starts it again.

fun currentPid() : int#

The process id the system gave this program.

val input : Writer#

What the program reads, writes and writes as errors, where each is a pipe.

val output : Reader#

What the program writes, to read; an IllegalStateException where its output is not a pipe.

val error : Reader#

What the program writes as errors, to read; an IllegalStateException where they are not a pipe.

fun waitFor() : int#

Waits for the program to end and gives its exit code; with a time limit, null if it is still running when the time is up.

fun waitFor(timeout : Duration) : int?#

Waits at most timeout for the program to end: its exit code, or null if it is still running then.

val exitCode : int?#

Its exit code once it has ended; null while it runs.

val isAlive : bool#

Whether the program is still running.

val pid : int#

The process id the system gave the program; it can still be read after the program ends.

val limitReached : string?#

The limit the program's tree went past, and was ended for: "memory" or "processes"; null where it went past none, or was started without limits.

fun kill()#

Ends the program now, and every program it started that is still running.

data ProcessResult(val exitCode : int, val output : string, val error : string, val limitReached : string? = null)#

What a program run to its end with Process.run gave; limitReached as Process's.

data ProcessLimits(val memory : int? = null, val processes : int? = null)#

Limits for a program and everything it starts in turn, together (2026-11-20): memory in bytes, and processes running at once; null for none. A tree that goes past one is ended, the whole of it; and a tree started with limits is ended when the program that started it ends. On Windows the system holds the tree to them as it runs (a job object); on Linux the tree is looked at ten times a second.

Sockets

class Socket : AutoCloseable#

One end of a TCP connection, or of a Unix domain socket's: given a FilePath where a host and a port go, the connection is to the socket file of that path, which a program on this machine listens on (a ServerSocket given the same path). Both are read and written alike.

Socket("example.org", 80).use { socket ->
  socket.writer.write("GET / HTTP/1.0\r\n\r\n")
  print(socket.reader.readLine())
}
Socket(FilePath("/run/app.sock")).use { it.writer.write("status\n") }

Its reads and writes block the thread they run on; a server serves each connection from a thread or a pool's job. Unix domain sockets are the system's own: on Linux, macOS and Windows (10, from version 1803; a path at most 107 bytes long there as everywhere).

fun connect(host : string, port : int, timeout : Duration? = null) : Socket throws IOException#

With a time limit, gives up when it runs out.

fun connect(to : SocketAddress, timeout : Duration? = null) : Socket throws IOException#

To an address and port (networking plan, part 1); with a time limit, gives up when it runs out.

fun connect(path : FilePath, timeout : Duration? = null) : Socket throws IOException#

To the Unix domain socket whose file is path (2026-11-19); with a time limit, gives up when it runs out. Not in a sandbox, which reaches no socket but its own (an IOException).

val reader : Reader#

What comes from the other side, and what goes to it.

val writer : Writer#

What goes to the other side; an IllegalStateException while the socket is not connected.

val isConnected : bool#

Whether it has been connected; it stays true after close().

val isClosed : bool#

Whether it was connected and has been closed since; false for one never connected.

val localPort : int#

The port of this end of the connection. An IllegalStateException while the socket is not connected, or where it is a Unix domain socket's (which has a path, no port), an IOException once it is closed.

val remoteAddress : string#

The address of the other side, as "127.0.0.1"; the same errors as localPort.

val port : int#

The port of the other side; the same errors as localPort.

val path : FilePath?#

The file of a Unix domain socket's connection; null for a TCP one, or a socket not connected.

var readTimeout : Duration?#

How long a read waits before it fails with a SocketTimeoutException, an IOException; null for as long as it takes (Java's soTimeout). It can be set before connect().

fun shutdownOutput() throws IOException#

Says that nothing more will be written: the other side reads the end.

override fun close()#

Closes the connection; closing it again, or a socket never connected, does nothing.

class ServerSocket : AutoCloseable#

A socket that waits for connections to this machine: at a port, or at a Unix domain socket's file, given a FilePath in place of the port (2026-11-19).

val server = ServerSocket(8080)
while (true) {
  val client = server.accept()          // a Socket
  thread { serve(client) }
}
val local = ServerSocket(FilePath("/run/app.sock"))   // for programs on this machine alone

Port 0 lets the system pick a free one (see localPort). With no address it takes connections from anywhere, over IPv4 and IPv6 both (IPv4 alone where the machine has no IPv6); IpAddress.loopback (or "127.0.0.1") keeps it to programs on this machine. A Unix domain socket's file is made as it starts listening (an IOException where a file of that name is there already: one an earlier program left is deleted first) and removed as it is closed; who may connect is who may open that file.

val localPort : int#

The port it listens on, the one the system picked where it was made with port 0; an IllegalStateException where it listens at a Unix domain socket's file, an IOException once it is closed.

val path : FilePath?#

The file it listens at, for a Unix domain socket; null where it listens on a port.

val isClosed : bool#

Whether it has been closed.

fun accept() : Socket throws IOException#

Waits for a connection and gives it.

override fun close()#

Stops listening. A thread waiting in accept() gets an IOException.

jpeg.ww

JPEG

Writing

json.ww

JSON

object Json#

JSON read and written (RFC 8259), the way Python's json module reads and writes it, and a tree of JSON values to build and look through (JadeLib's).

val config = Json.read(text) as Map<string, any?>        // plain values
val p = Json.read<Point>("{\"x\": 1, \"y\": 2}")          // a data class, made from it
print(Json.write(p, indent = 2))
val tree = toJsonVal(text)                               // a JsonValue tree
print(tree["items"]?[0]?.toInt())

Reading takes what Python's does and gives what it gives: whole numbers of any size (a BigInt past an int), NaN, Infinity and -Infinity, the last of two keys of one name, and nothing after the value but spaces, tabs and line ends. Text that is not JSON is a JsonParseException saying what was expected and where, in Python's words: "Expecting ',' delimiter: line 3 column 7 (char 41)". A \u escape of half a pair of UTF-16 surrogates with no other half reads as U+FFFD, which a string can hold where half a pair it cannot.

Writing gives what Python's does, its options with theirs: floats in Python's form ("1e-05", "1e+16", "0.1"), every character past ASCII as a \u escape unless ensureAscii is false, and an error for a value JSON has no form of, unless default makes it one.

A data class is read and written as an object of the fields in its header, in their order, each by its name; an enum's entry by its name, as a string. @JsonField on a field gives it another key, or leaves it out.

data Row(@JsonField("row_id") val id : int, val label : string, @JsonField(skip = true) val cache : int = 0)
fun <T> read(text : string, strict : bool = true, objectHook : ((Map<string, any?>) -> any?)? = null, objectPairsHook : ((List<MapEntry<string, any?>>) -> any?)? = null, parseFloat : ((string) -> any?)? = null, parseInt : ((string) -> any?)? = null, parseConstant : ((string) -> any?)? = null) : T throws JsonParseException#

The JSON value in text, as plain values: an object a Map<string, any?> with its keys in the order they come, an array a List<any?>, a string a string, a whole number an int (a BigInt past an int), any other number a float, true and false a bool, null null.

Given a type, the value made into one: Json.read<Point>(text), Json.read<List<Point>>(text). A data class is made through its constructor from the object's keys, a key left out given the parameter's default; an enum's entry by its name; a list, a set or a map of anything that can be read; a JsonValue (Json.read<JsonObject>(text)) as toJsonVal gives it. A value that does not fit the type is a JsonParseException naming where it is, as "$.points[2].x: a string where an int is wanted".

The rest are Python's json.loads's, by the same names:

strict          false lets a string hold the control characters below 0x20 as they are
objectHook      what each object is made into, given it as a map
objectPairsHook the same, given its keys and values in order, duplicates and all
parseFloat      what each number with a fraction or an exponent is made into, given its text
parseInt        the same for each whole number
parseConstant   the same for NaN, Infinity and -Infinity
fun rawDecode(text : string, at : int = 0, strict : bool = true) : JsonRead throws JsonParseException#

The JSON value at at in text, and where it ends, with whatever follows it left: as Python's raw_decode, which takes no spaces before the value.

fun write(value : any?, indent : (int | string)? = null, itemSeparator : string? = null, keySeparator : string = ": ", sortKeys : bool = false, ensureAscii : bool = true, allowNaN : bool = true, skipKeys : bool = false, checkCircular : bool = true, default : ((any?) -> any?)? = null) : string#

value written as JSON. Plain values are written as Python writes them: null, a bool, an int or a BigInt, a float, a string (a char as one), a list as an array, a map as an object; and a JsonValue as itself, a JsonNode as its toJson(), a data class as an object of its header's fields, an enum's entry as its name.

The rest are Python's json.dumps's, by the same names:

indent          null for all on one line; an int, that many spaces at each level, or a
                string put there, each item on a line of its own
itemSeparator   between items: ", " on one line, "," with an indent
keySeparator    between a key and its value: ": "
sortKeys        each object's keys in order: strings by their characters, numbers by size
ensureAscii     every character past ASCII as a \u escape (false: as it is)
allowNaN        NaN and the infinities written as NaN, Infinity and -Infinity (false: an error)
skipKeys        a map's key that is not a string, a number, a bool or null left out (false: an error)
checkCircular   a list or map inside itself an error (false: not looked for)
default         what a value JSON has no form of is written as instead

A value JSON has no form of, with no default, is an IllegalArgumentException.

fun write(value : any?, to : Writer, indent : (int | string)? = null, itemSeparator : string? = null, keySeparator : string = ": ", sortKeys : bool = false, ensureAscii : bool = true, allowNaN : bool = true, skipKeys : bool = false, checkCircular : bool = true, default : ((any?) -> any?)? = null) throws IOException#

value written as JSON to to as it is made, a piece at a time (each about 64 KB), so a large document is never held whole: the same text as write gives, with the same options. A value JSON has no form of may be found after some of the text is written; the IllegalArgumentException then comes with that part already in to.

FileWriter(path).use { Json.write(rows, it, indent = 2) }
data JsonRead(val value : any?, val end : int)#

What Json.rawDecode gives: the value read, and where in the text it ended.

class JsonParseException(val description : string, val position : int = -1, val line : int = 0, val column : int = 0, message : string = description) : IllegalStateException(message)#

Text that is not JSON, or a value that does not fit the type it was read as. description is what was wrong, as "Expecting value"; position the character it was found at, counted from 0, and line and column where that is, each counted from 1 (-1 and 0 for a value that does not fit). The message is all of them: "Expecting value: line 1 column 5 (char 4)"; JsonParseException(message) alone is one with nothing more to say.

annotation JsonField(val name : string = "", val skip : bool = false)#

On a data class's field: the key it is read and written by, in place of its name, or (skip = true) left out of JSON altogether, read as its default.

The tree (JadeLib's)

abstract class JsonValue#

A JSON value: a JsonObject, JsonArray, JsonString, JsonInt (JsonBigInt past an int), JsonDouble, JsonBool or JsonNull. Made by toJsonVal and toJsonObj, or built with JsonObject's []=, and written by toJsonText. A whole number and one with a fraction are different values, so 1 and 1.0 are not equal and diffJson tells them apart.

fun toFloat() : float#

As a float: a number's value, or a string's read as one; a NumberFormatException for anything else.

fun toInt() : int#

As an int: a number's value (a fraction cut off), or a string's read as one; a NumberFormatException for anything else.

fun stringValue() : string#

A string's text; an IllegalStateException for anything else.

op [](key : string) : JsonValue?#

An object's value for key; null where it has none, or for anything but an object.

op [](index : int) : JsonValue?#

An array's item at index; null past its end, or for anything but an array.

fun toBool() : bool?#

A bool's value; null for anything else.

fun toFloatArray() : List<float>?#

An array's items as floats; null for anything but an array.

fun <T> toListFromIndexList(sourceList : List<T>) : List<T>?#

An array of indexes as the items of sourceList they point at, those past its end left out; null for anything but an array.

fun asIntOrNull() : int?#

A number as an int, as toInt() gives it; null for anything else.

fun asDoubleOrNull() : float?#

A number as a float; null for anything else.

fun toJsonText(prettyPrint : bool = true, level : int = 0, tab : string = " ") : string#

Written as JSON text: with prettyPrint each item on a line of its own, tab once more at each level (starting at level), and ": " after each key; else all on one line, with nothing between the parts. Characters past ASCII are written as they are.

data JsonObject(val map : open Map<string, JsonValue> = {:}, val keyOrder : open List<string> = []) : JsonValue()#

A JSON object: its keys and values in map, and in keyOrder the order it is written in.

fun add(key : string, value : JsonValue)#

Sets key to value, adding it to the end of keyOrder where it is new.

op []=(key : string, value : float?)#

Sets key to a float; null takes the key out.

op []=(key : string, value : int?)#

Sets key to an int; null takes the key out.

op []=(key : string, value : string?)#

Sets key to a string; null takes the key out.

op []=(key : string, value : bool)#

Sets key to a bool.

op []=(key : string, value : nothing?)#

Sets key to JSON's null.

op []=(key : string, value : JsonValue?)#

Sets key to a JsonValue; null takes the key out.

op []=(key : string, value : any?)#

Sets key to anything jsonValueOf makes a JsonValue of.

fun intValue(key : string) : int#

The int at key; an IllegalStateException where there is none.

fun intValue(key : string, default : int) : int#

The int at key, or default where there is none or it is not a number.

fun doubleValue(key : string) : float#

The number at key as a float; an IllegalStateException where there is none.

fun stringValue(key : string) : string#

The string at key; a ClassCastException where it is not one.

fun arrayValue(key : string) : List<JsonValue>?#

The array at key as a new list; null where there is none.

fun objectValue(key : string) : JsonObject#

The object at key; a ClassCastException where it is not one.

override fun toString() : string#

Its keys and values, as {a:1,b:"x"}: for reading, not JSON (toJsonText writes that).

data JsonArray(val list : List<JsonValue> = []) : JsonValue(), Iterable<JsonValue>#

A JSON array, gone through by a for as its items.

override fun iterator() : Iterator<JsonValue>#

Its items, in order.

override fun toString() : string#

Its items, as [1,"x"]: for reading, not JSON.

data JsonString(val value : string) : JsonValue()#

A JSON string.

override fun toString() : string#

The text in quotes, as it is.

data JsonInt(val value : int) : JsonValue()#

A JSON whole number that fits in an int.

override fun toString() : string#

The number.

data JsonBigInt(val value : BigInt) : JsonValue()#

A JSON whole number past an int.

override fun toString() : string#

The number.

data JsonDouble(val value : float) : JsonValue()#

A JSON number with a fraction or an exponent, or NaN or an infinity.

override fun toString() : string#

The number, as JSON writes it: "1.0", "1e-05".

data JsonBool(val value : bool) : JsonValue()#

A JSON bool.

override fun toString() : string#

"true" or "false".

object JsonNull : JsonValue()#

JSON's null.

override fun toString() : string#

"null".

fun toJsonVal(jsonText : string) : JsonValue throws JsonParseException#

The JsonValue in jsonText, read as Json.read reads it.

fun toJsonObj(jsonText : string) : JsonObject throws JsonParseException#

The JsonObject in jsonText; a ClassCastException where the value is not an object.

fun toJsonText(jsonStruct : JsonValue) : string#

jsonStruct written as JSON text, as its toJsonText() writes it.

fun jsonValueOf(value : any?) : JsonValue#

Anything as a JsonValue: a JsonValue as it is, a JsonNode as its toJson(), a data class as an object of its header's fields, an enum's entry as its name, null, a bool, a number or a string as one, a list or a set as an array of its items, a map as an object of the items whose keys are strings. An IllegalArgumentException for anything else.

abstract class JsonNode#

A class written to and read from JSON by hand, with no reflection: it fills an object in toJson(value) and, where it is read back, reads one in fromJson(obj).

fun toJson() : JsonValue#

A new JsonObject that toJson(value) fills.

fun fromJson(txt : string) throws JsonParseException#

txt read and given to fromJson(obj).

protected fun toJson(value : JsonObject)#

Puts what it holds in value: written by each class that is written.

protected fun fromJson(obj : JsonValue)#

Takes what it holds from obj: written by each class that is read.

fun jsonList(nodeList : List<JsonNode>) : JsonArray#

The nodes' toJson(), in an array.

fun jsonList(intList : List<int>?) : JsonArray?#

The ints in an array; null for null.

fun jsonList(floatList : List<float>?) : JsonArray?#

The floats in an array; null for null.

fun jsonMap(intMap : Map<string, int>?) : JsonObject?#

A map of ints as an object; null for null.

fun <T : JsonNode> jsonListByIndex(dependantList : List<T>, sourceList : List<T>) : JsonArray#

The places in sourceList of the items of dependantList, in an array, those not in it left out.

abstract class JsonNodeFactory<T : JsonNode>#

What makes a JsonNode of type T from JSON: newFromJson(obj), written by each such class.

fun newFromJson(txt : string) : T throws JsonParseException#

txt read and given to newFromJson(obj).

fun newFromJson(obj : JsonValue) : T#

A new T made from obj.

abstract class JsonDiff#

One difference between two JsonValue trees, as diffJson finds it, with where it is: "/a/b[2]".

data ValueChanged(val path : string, val old : any?, val new : any?) : JsonDiff()#

A value in both trees, changed.

data MissingInNew(val path : string, val old : any?) : JsonDiff()#

A value in the old tree and not the new.

data MissingInOld(val path : string, val new : any?) : JsonDiff()#

A value in the new tree and not the old.

fun diffJson(old : JsonValue, new : JsonValue, path : string = "") : List<JsonDiff>#

Every difference between old and new, gone through as objects and arrays. A whole number and one with a fraction are never equal, so 1 against 1.0 is a change.

fun jsonValuesEqual(a : JsonValue, b : JsonValue) : bool#

Whether two values that are neither objects nor arrays are equal: of one kind, with one value.

Reading

Writing

Data classes and enums

Floats

list.ww

Lists

The methods a list has besides the built-in ones (add, get, slice, sort and so on). Every method that takes a function is written here, in WinterWolf: the function then runs as part of the program, and a task can be set aside inside it.

The oldest ones

inline fun <T, R> List<T>.map(transform : (T) -> R) : open List<R>#

What transform gives for each item, in order, as a new list.

inline fun <T> List<T>.filter(predicate : (T) -> bool) : open List<T>#

The items for which predicate is true, in their order, as a new list.

inline fun <T> List<T>.find(predicate : (T) -> bool) : T?#

The first item for which predicate is true, or null when there is none (as firstOrNull).

inline fun <T> List<T>.reduce(operation : (T, T) -> T) : T#

reduce(operation) starts from the first item; reduce(operation, initial) from initial.

inline fun <T, R> List<T>.reduce(operation : (R, T) -> R, initial : R) : R#

Combines the items from the first to the last, starting from initial: result becomes operation(result, item) for each in turn. An empty list gives initial. The function comes first here; fold takes initial first.

The item at one end, or with a condition the nearest to that end that meets it

fun <T> List<T>.first() : T#

The first item; a NoSuchElementException when the list is empty.

inline fun <T> List<T>.first(predicate : (T) -> bool) : T#

The first item for which predicate is true; a NoSuchElementException when there is none.

fun <T> List<T>.firstOrNull() : T?#

The first item, or null when the list is empty.

inline fun <T> List<T>.firstOrNull(predicate : (T) -> bool) : T?#

The first item for which predicate is true, or null when there is none.

fun <T> List<T>.last() : T#

The last item; a NoSuchElementException when the list is empty.

inline fun <T> List<T>.last(predicate : (T) -> bool) : T#

The last item for which predicate is true, looking from the end; a NoSuchElementException when there is none.

fun <T> List<T>.lastOrNull() : T?#

The last item, or null when the list is empty.

inline fun <T> List<T>.lastOrNull(predicate : (T) -> bool) : T?#

The last item for which predicate is true, looking from the end, or null when there is none.

Going through the items

inline fun <T> List<T>.forEach(action : (T) -> unit)#

Calls action with each item, in order.

inline fun <T> List<T>.forEachIndexed(action : (int, T) -> unit)#

Calls action with the index of each item and the item, in order.

inline fun <T> List<T>.onEach(action : (T) -> unit) : List<T>#

Calls action with each item, in order, and gives the list itself, so that a chain of calls can go on.

Asking about the items

fun <T> List<T>.any() : bool#

True when the list has at least one item.

inline fun <T> List<T>.any(predicate : (T) -> bool) : bool#

True when predicate is true for at least one item, stopping at the first; false for an empty list.

inline fun <T> List<T>.all(predicate : (T) -> bool) : bool#

True when predicate is true for every item, stopping at the first for which it is not; true for an empty list.

fun <T> List<T>.none() : bool#

True when the list is empty.

inline fun <T> List<T>.none(predicate : (T) -> bool) : bool#

True when predicate is true for no item, stopping at the first for which it is; true for an empty list.

fun <T> List<T>.count() : int#

The number of items, as size.

inline fun <T> List<T>.count(predicate : (T) -> bool) : int#

The number of items for which predicate is true.

fun <T> List<T>.containsAll(items : List<T>) : bool#

True when every one of items is in this list, compared with ==; true when items is empty.

fun <T> List<T>.containsAll(items : Set<T>) : bool#

True when every item of the set items is in this list; true when the set is empty.

fun <T> List<T>?.orEmpty() : List<T>#

The list, or an empty list when it is null.

inline fun <T> List<T>.ifEmpty(defaultValue : () -> List<T>) : List<T>#

The list, or the list defaultValue gives when this one is empty; defaultValue is called only then.

Finding

inline fun <T> List<T>.indexOfFirst(predicate : (T) -> bool) : int#

The index of the first item for which predicate is true, or -1 when there is none.

inline fun <T> List<T>.indexOfLast(predicate : (T) -> bool) : int#

The index of the last item for which predicate is true, or -1 when there is none.

inline fun <T> List<T>.findLast(predicate : (T) -> bool) : T?#

The last item for which predicate is true, or null when there is none (as lastOrNull).

fun <T> List<T>.single() : T#

The only item: a NoSuchElementException when the list is empty, an IllegalArgumentException when it has more than one, as in Kotlin.

inline fun <T> List<T>.single(predicate : (T) -> bool) : T#

The only item for which predicate is true: a NoSuchElementException when there is none, an IllegalArgumentException when there are more. predicate is called for every item.

fun <T> List<T>.singleOrNull() : T?#

The only item, or null when the list is empty or has more than one.

inline fun <T> List<T>.singleOrNull(predicate : (T) -> bool) : T?#

The only item for which predicate is true, or null when there is none or more than one.

inline fun <T> List<T>.getOrElse(index : int, defaultValue : (int) -> T) : T#

The item at index, or what defaultValue gives for index when there is no item there (a negative index among them).

fun <T> List<T>.elementAt(index : int) : T#

The item at index, as get; an IndexOutOfBoundsException when there is none, as for a negative index.

fun <T> List<T>.elementAtOrNull(index : int) : T?#

The item at index, or null when there is none, as for a negative index.

Making another list

inline fun <T, R> List<T>.mapIndexed(transform : (int, T) -> R) : open List<R>#

What transform gives for the index of each item and the item, in order, as a new list.

inline fun <T, R> List<T>.mapNotNull(transform : (T) -> R?) : open List<R>#

What transform gives for each item, in order, with the nulls left out, as a new list.

inline fun <T, R> List<T>.mapIndexedNotNull(transform : (int, T) -> R?) : open List<R>#

What transform gives for the index of each item and the item, in order, with the nulls left out, as a new list.

inline fun <T, R> List<T>.flatMap(transform : (T) -> List<R>) : open List<R>#

The items of the lists transform gives for each item, one list after another, as one new list.

["a b", "c"].flatMap { it.split(" ") }   // ["a", "b", "c"]
fun <T> List<List<T>>.flatten() : open List<T>#

The items of each list in this list of lists, one list after another, as one new list.

inline fun <T> List<T>.filterNot(predicate : (T) -> bool) : open List<T>#

The items for which predicate is false, in their order, as a new list.

fun <T> List<T?>.filterNotNull() : open List<T>#

The items that are not null, in their order, as a new list.

inline fun <T> List<T>.filterIndexed(predicate : (int, T) -> bool) : open List<T>#

The items for which predicate, given the index of the item and the item, is true, in their order, as a new list.

inline fun <T> List<T>.partition(predicate : (T) -> bool) : Pair<List<T>, List<T>>#

The items that meet the condition and those that do not, as a Pair of lists.

fun <T> List<T>.take(count : int) : open List<T>#

The first count items as a new list, or all of them when there are fewer; an IllegalArgumentException when count is negative.

fun <T> List<T>.takeLast(count : int) : open List<T>#

The last count items as a new list, or all of them when there are fewer; an IllegalArgumentException when count is negative.

fun <T> List<T>.drop(count : int) : open List<T>#

The items after the first count as a new list, empty when there are no more; an IllegalArgumentException when count is negative.

fun <T> List<T>.dropLast(count : int) : open List<T>#

The items before the last count as a new list, empty when there are no more; an IllegalArgumentException when count is negative.

inline fun <T> List<T>.takeWhile(predicate : (T) -> bool) : open List<T>#

The items from the start up to, not including, the first for which predicate is false, as a new list.

inline fun <T> List<T>.dropWhile(predicate : (T) -> bool) : open List<T>#

The items from the first for which predicate is false to the end, as a new list.

fun <T> List<T>.chunked(size : int) : open List<List<T>>#

The list cut into lists of size items; the last may be shorter.

fun <T> List<T>.windowed(size : int, step : int = 1, partialWindows : bool = false) : open List<List<T>>#

Every run of size items in a row, moving along by step.

fun <T, U> List<T>.zip(other : List<U>) : open List<Pair<T, U>>#

Pairs each item with the one at the same position in other, as far as the shorter goes.

inline fun <T, U, R> List<T>.zip(other : List<U>, transform : (T, U) -> R) : open List<R>#

What transform gives for each item and the one at the same position in other, as far as the shorter list goes, as a new list.

fun <T> List<T>.zipWithNext() : open List<Pair<T, T>>#

Each item paired with the one after it, as a list of Pairs: one fewer than the items, and empty for a list of fewer than two.

inline fun <T, R> List<T>.zipWithNext(transform : (T, T) -> R) : open List<R>#

What transform gives for each item and the one after it, as a new list: one fewer than the items, and empty for a list of fewer than two.

[1, 4, 9].zipWithNext { a, b -> b - a }   // [3, 5]
fun <T> List<T>.withIndex() : open List<IndexedValue<T>>#

Each item with its index, as a new list of IndexedValue(index, value).

fun <T> List<T>.distinct() : open List<T>#

The items without repeats, each where it first comes, as a new list. Items count as the same when they would be the same map key: 1 and 1.0 are one.

inline fun <T> List<T>.distinctBy(selector : (T) -> any?) : open List<T>#

The items whose key from selector has not come before, each where it first comes, as a new list.

fun <T> List<T>.plus(other : List<T>) : open List<T>#

The items of this list and then those of other, as a new list; as this + other.

fun <T> List<T>.plus(item : T) : open List<T>#

The items of this list and then item, as a new list.

fun <T> List<T>.minus(other : List<T>) : open List<T>#

The items that are not in other, as a new list: every copy of an item in other is left out.

fun <T> List<T>.minus(other : Set<T>) : open List<T>#

The items that are not in the set other, as a new list: every copy of one is left out.

fun <T> List<T>.minus(item : T) : open List<T>#

The list without the first item equal to item, as a new list; any other copies of it stay.

fun <T> List<T>.asReversed() : List<T>#

The items from the last to the first, as a new list: a copy, as reversed() gives, not a view of this list.

fun <T> List<T>.copyOf() : open List<T>#

A copy of the list, one that can be changed; as toList.

Order

fun <T> List<T>.sorted() : open List<T>#

The items from the least to the greatest, as a new list. The sort is stable: equal items keep their order.

fun <T> List<T>.sortedDescending() : open List<T>#

The items from the greatest to the least, as a new list; equal items keep their order.

fun <T> List<T>.sortedBy(selector : (T) -> any?) : open List<T>#

The items in the order of the key selector gives for each, the least first, as a new list. Each key is worked out once, and items with equal keys keep their order.

fun <T> List<T>.sortedByDescending(selector : (T) -> any?) : open List<T>#

The items in the order of the key selector gives for each, the greatest first, as a new list. Each key is worked out once, and items with equal keys keep their order.

fun <T> List<T>.sortedWith(comparator : (T, T) -> int) : open List<T>#

The items in the order comparator sets, as a new list: it is given two items and gives a negative number when the first goes before the second. Equal items keep their order.

These sort the list itself. sortBy and sortByDescending, which do too, are built in: each item's key is worked out once, and each item moved once.

fun <T> open List<T>.sortWith(comparator : (T, T) -> int)#

Sorts the list itself in the order comparator sets, as sort(comparator) does.

fun <T> open List<T>.sortDescending()#

Sorts the list itself from the greatest to the least; equal items keep their order.

fun <T> open List<T>.shuffle()#

Puts the items of the list itself in a random order, every order as likely. Math.seed makes the order the same on every run.

fun <T> List<T>.shuffled() : open List<T>#

The items in a random order, as a new list.

fun <T> List<T>.random() : T#

An item picked at random; a NoSuchElementException when the list is empty.

Working a value out of the items

inline fun <T, R> List<T>.fold(initial : R, operation : (R, T) -> R) : R#

Combines the items from the first to the last, starting from initial: result becomes operation(result, item) for each in turn. An empty list gives initial.

[1, 2, 3].fold(0) { total, item -> total + item }   // 6
fun <T : Number> List<T>.sum() : T#

A list of ints sums to an int, one with a float in it to a float; an empty list to a zero of its item type, 0 or 0.0.

inline fun <T, R : Number> List<T>.sumOf(selector : (T) -> R) : R#

The sum of what selector gives for each item: an int when every one is an int, otherwise a float; for an empty list a zero of the kind selector gives, 0 or 0.0.

fun <T : Number> List<T>.average() : float#

The mean of the items, as a float; NaN (isNaN() is true) for an empty list.

fun <T> List<T>.maxOrNull() : T?#

The greatest item, the first of them if several are equal, or null when the list is empty. Items that cannot be compared, such as a string and an int, give a ClassCastException.

fun <T> List<T>.minOrNull() : T?#

The least item, the first of them if several are equal, or null when the list is empty. Items that cannot be compared, such as a string and an int, give a ClassCastException.

fun <T> List<T>.max() : T#

The greatest item; a NoSuchElementException when the list is empty.

fun <T> List<T>.min() : T#

The least item; a NoSuchElementException when the list is empty.

inline fun <T> List<T>.maxByOrNull(selector : (T) -> any?) : T?#

The item for which the selector gives the most, or the least.

inline fun <T> List<T>.minByOrNull(selector : (T) -> any?) : T?#

The item for which selector gives the least, the first of them if several, or null when the list is empty.

inline fun <T> List<T>.maxBy(selector : (T) -> any?) : T#

The item for which selector gives the most, the first of them if several; a NoSuchElementException when the list is empty.

inline fun <T> List<T>.minBy(selector : (T) -> any?) : T#

The item for which selector gives the least, the first of them if several; a NoSuchElementException when the list is empty.

inline fun <T, R> List<T>.maxOfOrNull(selector : (T) -> R) : R?#

The most, or the least, that the selector gives for any item.

inline fun <T, R> List<T>.minOfOrNull(selector : (T) -> R) : R?#

The least that selector gives for any item, or null when the list is empty.

inline fun <T, R> List<T>.maxOf(selector : (T) -> R) : R#

The most that selector gives for any item; a NoSuchElementException when the list is empty.

inline fun <T, R> List<T>.minOf(selector : (T) -> R) : R#

The least that selector gives for any item; a NoSuchElementException when the list is empty.

Maps and sets from a list

inline fun <T, K, V> List<T>.associate(transform : (T) -> Pair<K, V>) : open Map<K, V>#

A map from the Pair of a key and its value that transform gives for each item; where a key comes again, the later value is kept. toMap does the same for a list that holds Pairs.

fun <K, V> List<Pair<K, V>>.toMap() : open Map<K, V>#

A map from a list of Pairs, each a key and its value; where a key comes again, the later value is kept.

inline fun <T, K> List<T>.associateBy(keySelector : (T) -> K) : open Map<K, T>#

A map from the key keySelector gives for each item to the item; of items with the same key, the last is kept.

inline fun <T, K, V> List<T>.associateBy(keySelector : (T) -> K, valueTransform : (T) -> V) : open Map<K, V>#

A map from the key keySelector gives for each item to what valueTransform gives for it; of items with the same key, the last is kept.

inline fun <T, V> List<T>.associateWith(valueSelector : (T) -> V) : open Map<T, V>#

A map from each item to what valueSelector gives for it.

inline fun <T, K> List<T>.groupBy(keySelector : (T) -> K) : open Map<K, open List<T>>#

A map from each key to the list of items that gave it, or of what the transform makes of them.

inline fun <T, K, V> List<T>.groupBy(keySelector : (T) -> K, valueTransform : (T) -> V) : open Map<K, open List<V>>#

A map from each key keySelector gives to the list of what valueTransform gives for the items with that key, in their order.

fun <T> List<T>.toSet() : open Set<T>#

The items as a new set, each once. The set can be changed, as one from toOpenSet.

fun <T> List<T>.toOpenSet() : open Set<T>#

The items as a new set that can be changed, each once; the same as toSet.

Changing the list itself

fun <T> open List<T>.addFirst(item : T)#

Puts item at the front of the list.

fun <T> open List<T>.addLast(item : T)#

Puts item at the end of the list, as add.

fun <T> open List<T>.removeAll(predicate : (T) -> bool) : bool#

removeAll and retainAll take a condition or a collection of items. True if anything went.

fun <T> open List<T>.removeAll(items : List<T>) : bool#

Removes from the list every item that is in items; true if anything was removed.

fun <T> open List<T>.removeAll(items : Set<T>) : bool#

Removes from the list every item that is in the set items; true if anything was removed.

fun <T> open List<T>.retainAll(predicate : (T) -> bool) : bool#

Keeps in the list only the items for which predicate is true; true if anything was removed.

fun <T> open List<T>.retainAll(items : List<T>) : bool#

Keeps in the list only the items that are in items; true if anything was removed.

fun <T> open List<T>.retainAll(items : Set<T>) : bool#

Keeps in the list only the items that are in the set items; true if anything was removed.

fun <T> open List<T>.removeIf(predicate : (T) -> bool) : bool#

Removes from the list the items for which predicate is true, as removeAll(predicate); true if anything was removed.

localtime.ww

Dates and times where the program runs

A date, a time of day, and the two together, as a calendar and a clock show them in the system's time zone, named and written as java.time and kotlinx-datetime have them:

val stamp = LocalDateTime.now().format("yyyyMMdd-HHmmss")     // 20261106-143005
print(LocalTime.now().copy(nanosecond = 0))                    // 14:30:05
val local = path.lastModified()!!.toLocalDateTime()

None of them is a moment by itself: toInstant() makes one in the system's zone. A sandbox's zone is UTC.

value LocalDate(val year : int, val month : int, val dayOfMonth : int)#

A day in the calendar: its year, its month (1 to 12) and its day of the month (from 1).

val dayOfWeek : int#

The day of the week, 1 for Monday to 7 for Sunday, as ISO 8601 numbers them.

val dayOfYear : int#

The day of the year, from 1.

op <(other : LocalDate) : bool#

Whether this date comes before other.

op <=(other : LocalDate) : bool#

Whether this date comes before other or is the same.

op >(other : LocalDate) : bool#

Whether this date comes after other.

op >=(other : LocalDate) : bool#

Whether this date comes after other or is the same.

fun compareTo(other : LocalDate) : int#

Negative, zero or positive as this comes before, with or after other.

fun format(pattern : string) : string#

The date written with Java's pattern letters (see LocalDateTime.format).

fun toString() : string#

2026-11-06, as ISO 8601 writes it.

fun now() : LocalDate#

Today, in the system's time zone.

value LocalTime(val hour : int, val minute : int, val second : int = 0, val nanosecond : int = 0)#

A time of day: its hour (0 to 23), minute, second and the nanoseconds past that second.

op <(other : LocalTime) : bool#

Whether this time comes before other.

op <=(other : LocalTime) : bool#

Whether this time comes before other or is the same.

op >(other : LocalTime) : bool#

Whether this time comes after other.

op >=(other : LocalTime) : bool#

Whether this time comes after other or is the same.

fun compareTo(other : LocalTime) : int#

Negative, zero or positive as this comes before, with or after other.

fun format(pattern : string) : string#

The time written with Java's pattern letters (see LocalDateTime.format).

fun toString() : string#

As Java writes it: 14:30, 14:30:05, or 14:30:05.250 with the fraction in 3, 6 or 9 digits; the seconds only where they or the fraction are not zero.

fun now() : LocalTime#

The time of day now, in the system's time zone.

value LocalDateTime(val year : int, val month : int, val dayOfMonth : int, val hour : int, val minute : int, val second : int = 0, val nanosecond : int = 0)#

A date and a time of day together.

val date : LocalDate#

Its date alone.

val time : LocalTime#

Its time of day alone.

val dayOfWeek : int#

The day of the week, 1 for Monday to 7 for Sunday.

val dayOfYear : int#

The day of the year, from 1.

op <(other : LocalDateTime) : bool#

Whether this date and time comes before other.

op <=(other : LocalDateTime) : bool#

Whether this date and time comes before other or is the same.

op >(other : LocalDateTime) : bool#

Whether this date and time comes after other.

op >=(other : LocalDateTime) : bool#

Whether this date and time comes after other or is the same.

fun compareTo(other : LocalDateTime) : int#

Negative, zero or positive as this comes before, with or after other.

fun toInstant() : Instant#

The moment it is in the system's time zone. A time the clocks skip when summer time starts is taken as the moment an hour later; one they pass twice, as the first.

fun format(pattern : string) : string#

Written with Java's pattern letters, as DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern takes them:

yyyy, yy  the year (uuuu the same)       M, MM, MMM, MMMM  the month: 3, 03, Mar, March
d, dd     the day of the month           D  the day of the year
E, EEEE   the day of the week: Mon, Monday
H, HH     the hour, 0 to 23              h, hh  the hour, 1 to 12, with a for AM or PM
m, mm     the minute                     s, ss  the second
S...      the fraction of the second, as many digits as letters

Text in single quotes is written as it is ('' is a quote), and so is anything not a letter. Any other letter is an IllegalArgumentException.

fun toString() : string#

2026-11-06T14:30:05, as ISO 8601 and Java write it (the time as LocalTime writes it).

fun now() : LocalDateTime#

The date and time now, in the system's time zone.

fun Instant.toLocalDateTime() : LocalDateTime#

The date and time this moment is in the system's time zone.

map.ww

Maps

The methods a map has besides the built-in ones. A function given to one of them takes an entry, which has a key and a value and can be taken apart: { (key, value) -> ... }

inline fun <K, V> open Map<K, V>.getOrPut(key : K, defaultValue : () -> V) : V#

What key holds; where it is not there or holds null, what defaultValue() gives, which is put in under key first.

inline fun <K, V> Map<K, V>.getOrElse(key : K, defaultValue : () -> V) : V#

What key holds, or what defaultValue() gives where it is not there or holds null; the map is left as it is.

inline fun <K, V> open Map<K, V>.update(key : K, start : V, change : (V) -> V) : V#

Changing what a key holds, where a key that is not there starts from start, for a change that is not one operator (L118; counting is counts[word, default = 0]++). Gives the new value.

fun <K, V> open Map<K, V>.putAll(from : Map<K, V>)#

Puts in every entry of from; a key the map has already takes from's value.

fun <K, V> open Map<K, V>.putAll(pairs : List<Pair<K, V>>)#

Puts in each pair of pairs, in their order: a key that comes twice keeps the later value.

fun <K, V> Map<K, V>?.orEmpty() : Map<K, V>#

The map, or a new empty one when it is null.

inline fun <K, V> Map<K, V>.ifEmpty(defaultValue : () -> Map<K, V>) : Map<K, V>#

The map, or what defaultValue() gives when the map is empty.

inline fun <K, V> Map<K, V>.forEach(action : (MapEntry<K, V>) -> unit)#

forEach { entry -> }, forEach { (key, value) -> } or forEach { key, value -> }: the function says which it wants by how many parameters it has.

inline fun <K, V> Map<K, V>.forEach(action : (K, V) -> unit)#

Calls action with the key and the value of each entry, in the map's order.

Asking about the entries

fun <K, V> Map<K, V>.any() : bool#

Whether the map has any entries.

inline fun <K, V> Map<K, V>.any(predicate : (MapEntry<K, V>) -> bool) : bool#

Whether predicate is true of at least one entry; it stops at the first that it is true of.

inline fun <K, V> Map<K, V>.all(predicate : (MapEntry<K, V>) -> bool) : bool#

Whether predicate is true of every entry; true for an empty map.

fun <K, V> Map<K, V>.none() : bool#

Whether the map is empty.

inline fun <K, V> Map<K, V>.none(predicate : (MapEntry<K, V>) -> bool) : bool#

Whether predicate is true of no entry; true for an empty map.

fun <K, V> Map<K, V>.count() : int#

How many entries the map has: its size.

inline fun <K, V> Map<K, V>.count(predicate : (MapEntry<K, V>) -> bool) : int#

How many entries predicate is true of.

Making a list

inline fun <K, V, R> Map<K, V>.map(transform : (MapEntry<K, V>) -> R) : open List<R>#

What transform gives for each entry, in the map's order, as a new list.

inline fun <K, V, R> Map<K, V>.mapNotNull(transform : (MapEntry<K, V>) -> R?) : open List<R>#

What transform gives for each entry, the nulls left out, as a new list.

inline fun <K, V, R> Map<K, V>.flatMap(transform : (MapEntry<K, V>) -> List<R>) : open List<R>#

The items of the lists transform gives for each entry, one after the other, as a new list.

fun <K, V> Map<K, V>.toList() : open List<Pair<K, V>>#

The entries as a list of pairs.

Making another map

inline fun <K, V, R> Map<K, V>.mapValues(transform : (MapEntry<K, V>) -> R) : open Map<K, R>#

A new map with the same keys, each holding what transform gives for its entry.

inline fun <K, V, R> Map<K, V>.mapKeys(transform : (MapEntry<K, V>) -> R) : open Map<R, V>#

A new map whose keys are what transform gives for each entry, with the same values. Where two entries give the same key, the later one's value is kept.

inline fun <K, V> Map<K, V>.filter(predicate : (MapEntry<K, V>) -> bool) : open Map<K, V>#

The entries predicate is true of, in their order, as a new map.

inline fun <K, V> Map<K, V>.filterNot(predicate : (MapEntry<K, V>) -> bool) : open Map<K, V>#

The entries predicate is false of, in their order, as a new map.

inline fun <K, V> Map<K, V>.filterKeys(predicate : (K) -> bool) : open Map<K, V>#

The entries whose key predicate is true of, as a new map.

inline fun <K, V> Map<K, V>.filterValues(predicate : (V) -> bool) : open Map<K, V>#

The entries whose value predicate is true of, as a new map.

fun <K, V> Map<K, V>.plus(pair : Pair<K, V>) : open Map<K, V>#

A new map with a pair, a list of pairs or another map added.

fun <K, V> Map<K, V>.plus(pairs : List<Pair<K, V>>) : open Map<K, V>#

A new map with each pair of pairs put in, in order; a key already there takes the pair's value.

fun <K, V> Map<K, V>.plus(other : Map<K, V>) : open Map<K, V>#

A new map with the entries of other put in; where both have a key, other's value wins.

fun <K, V> Map<K, V>.minus(key : K) : open Map<K, V>#

A new map without a key, or without each key of a list or a set.

fun <K, V> Map<K, V>.minus(keys : List<K>) : open Map<K, V>#

A new map without the keys in keys; a key the map does not have is passed over.

fun <K, V> Map<K, V>.minus(keys : Set<K>) : open Map<K, V>#

A new map without the keys in the set keys.

Picking an entry

inline fun <K, V> Map<K, V>.maxByOrNull(selector : (MapEntry<K, V>) -> any?) : MapEntry<K, V>?#

The entry for which selector gives the most; the first such where several tie, and null for an empty map.

inline fun <K, V> Map<K, V>.minByOrNull(selector : (MapEntry<K, V>) -> any?) : MapEntry<K, V>?#

The entry for which selector gives the least; the first such where several tie, and null for an empty map.

fun <K, V> Map<K, V>.firstOrNull() : MapEntry<K, V>?#

The first entry in the map's order; null for an empty map.

inline fun <K, V> Map<K, V>.firstOrNull(predicate : (MapEntry<K, V>) -> bool) : MapEntry<K, V>?#

The first entry predicate is true of; null where there is none.

inline fun <K, V, R : Number> Map<K, V>.sumOf(selector : (MapEntry<K, V>) -> R) : R#

The sum of what selector gives for each entry; 0 for an empty map.

net.ww

Addresses, names and datagrams (networking plan, part 1, 2026-11-20)

What a program needs of the network besides a TCP connection (Socket and ServerSocket, in io.ww): an internet address, an address with a port, looking a name up, and UDP's datagrams. Cryptography and TLS are import Crypto, HTTP is import Web (docs/networking-plan.html).

class IpAddress#

An internet address, IPv4 or IPv6: the number alone (Java's InetAddress without its name). Making one never asks the network; a name is looked up with Dns.

val home = IpAddress("192.168.1.10")
val here = IpAddress("::1")
print(home.isPrivate, here.isLoopback, IpAddress.parse("example.org"))   // true true null

Equal addresses are equal (==) and one key; "$address" is its text, IPv6 in its shortest form (RFC 5952: "2001:db8::1"). IPv6 with IPv4 inside (::ffff:1.2.3.4) is IPv6 here, as written; one a socket hands over (a datagram's sender, say) is given as the IPv4 address.

val isIpv6 : bool#

Whether it is an IPv6 address; false for IPv4.

val loopback#

127.0.0.1, this machine over IPv4.

val loopback6#

::1, this machine over IPv6.

val any#

0.0.0.0, every IPv4 address of this machine, where a socket listens.

val any6#

::, every IPv6 address of this machine, where a socket listens.

fun parse(text : string) : IpAddress?#

The address written in text, or null where it is none.

val isIpv4 : bool#

Whether it is an IPv4 address.

val bytes : Bytes#

Its 4 bytes (IPv4) or 16 (IPv6), the highest first: a copy each time.

val isLoopback : bool#

Whether it is this machine: 127.0.0.0 to 127.255.255.255, or ::1.

val isAny : bool#

Whether it is no address in particular, the one a socket listens at for all: 0.0.0.0 or ::.

val isLinkLocal : bool#

Whether it reaches only the local link: 169.254.0.0/16, or fe80::/10.

val isPrivate : bool#

Whether it is a private network's (RFC 1918's 10/8, 172.16/12 and 192.168/16; IPv6's unique local fc00::/7), never seen on the internet.

val isMulticast : bool#

Whether it is a group's, sent to all who joined it: 224.0.0.0/4, or ff00::/8.

op ==(other : any?) : bool#

Whether other is the same address: IPv4's 0.0.0.1 and IPv6's ::1 differ.

fun hashCode() : int#

The same for equal addresses, so that an address is a key.

override fun toString() : string#

"192.168.1.10", or IPv6 in its shortest form: "2001:db8::1", "::ffff:1.2.3.4".

data SocketAddress(val address : IpAddress, val port : int)#

An IP address and a port: where a socket connects, listens, or sends a datagram (Java's InetSocketAddress, never looked up). "$address" is "1.2.3.4:53", or "[::1]:53" for IPv6.

fun parse(text : string) : SocketAddress?#

The address and port written in text, "1.2.3.4:53" or "[::1]:53"; null where it is not.

override fun toString() : string#

"1.2.3.4:53", or "[::1]:53" for IPv6, as parse reads it.

object Dns#

Looking names up, through the system's resolver (getaddrinfo: its hosts file, its DNS servers, its cache). Each call waits for the answer, as a socket's read does.

val addresses = Dns.lookup("example.org")     // IPv4 and IPv6, in the order the system prefers
print(Dns.reverse(IpAddress.loopback))          // this machine's name is Program.hostName

Records of DNS's own (MX, TXT and the rest) are not the resolver's to give: a program that needs them speaks DNS over a DatagramSocket. Not in a sandbox, whose network has no names to look up (an IOException).

fun lookup(host : string) : List<IpAddress> throws UnknownHostException#

The addresses host is at, each once; an address written as text is its own answer. An UnknownHostException, an IOException, where the name is not found.

fun lookupFirst(host : string) : IpAddress throws UnknownHostException#

The address the system prefers of those host is at; an UnknownHostException where none.

fun reverse(address : IpAddress) : string?#

The name the address has, as the resolver gives it; null where it has none.

class DatagramSocket : AutoCloseable#

A UDP socket: datagrams sent and received whole, each on its own, with no connection (Java's DatagramSocket). A datagram may be lost, come twice or come out of order; what needs them all, in order, is built on top (a DNS client asks again).

DatagramSocket(5353).use { udp ->              // no address: IPv4 and IPv6 both
  udp.readTimeout = 2.seconds
  val query = udp.receive()                   // waits; a Datagram
  udp.send(answer(query.data), query.from)
}
DatagramSocket().send("ping".toBytes(), SocketAddress(IpAddress.loopback, 5353))

Port 0 lets the system pick a free one (see localPort). With no address it takes datagrams at every address of this machine, IPv4 and IPv6; IpAddress.loopback keeps it to programs on this machine. receive() waits on the thread it runs on, as a socket's read does. Not in a sandbox, whose network has TCP alone (an IOException).

fun send(data : Bytes, to : SocketAddress, offset : int = 0, size : int = data.size - offset) throws IOException#

Sends size of the bytes from offset as one datagram to to. It does not wait for it to arrive, nor say whether it did. An IOException where the system will not send it (too large, say: 65,507 bytes is the most IPv4 carries).

fun receive(maxSize : int = 65535) : Datagram throws IOException#

Waits for the next datagram and gives it with where it came from. Of one longer than maxSize bytes, the rest is lost. A SocketTimeoutException, an IOException, where none came within readTimeout; an IOException where the socket is closed while it waits.

var readTimeout : Duration?#

How long receive() waits before it fails with a SocketTimeoutException; null for as long as it takes.

val localPort : int#

The port it is at, the one the system picked where it was made with port 0.

val localAddress : IpAddress#

The address it is at: IpAddress.any6 (or any) where it takes datagrams at every address.

val isClosed : bool#

Whether it has been closed.

override fun close()#

Closes it. A thread waiting in receive() gets an IOException.

data Datagram(val data : Bytes, val from : SocketAddress)#

One datagram as received: its bytes, and the address and port it came from (the one to answer to).

numeric.ww

Whole lists and grids at once (M9/M10, questions 15 and 17)

numpy's kind of work on the lists and grids WinterWolf has: a List<int> or List<float>, a list of a narrow width (List<float32>, List<int16>), Bytes, and a Grid of any of them are each one packed block of numbers, and an operation on the whole of one is a loop in C++ over it.

val prices : List<float> = ...
val withTax = prices.timesEach(1.2)
val total = prices.dot(amounts)
val heights = Grid<float>(100, 100)
val smooth = heights.convolve(Grid<float>(3, 3, fill = 1.0 / 9))

The answers are those WinterWolf's operators give: an int with an int stays an int, its overflow an ArithmeticException as + gives (the ...Wrapped forms wrap round instead, as &+ does); ints divided are whole numbers, a division by zero an error; anything with a float is a float. An int list's operations take ints; make it floats first for float answers. A list of a narrow width gives a list of its own width, and a result written into one is checked against its range as storing it is. Sizes must match, or an IllegalArgumentException says both; a single number may stand for a list, for every item.

Each gives a new list or grid, or, given into, writes its answer there and makes nothing: numpy's out=, for an inner loop that should not make a list each time.

- Lists -

Item by item, this plus other (a list of the same size, or one number for every item).

Item by item, this plus other, written into into (of this list's size), which it gives.

How many items fall in each of bins equal parts of from to to (the least and the most item when they are null), the last part taking to itself; items outside are not counted.

Each cell held from low to high.

fun <T : Number> Grid<T>.absEach() : Grid<T>#

Each cell made positive.

fun Grid<int>.sum() : int#

The total of every cell: an int for ints, a float otherwise.

fun Grid<float>.sum() : float#

The total of every cell, as a float.

fun <T : Number> Grid<T>.sum() : Number#

The total of every cell.

fun <T : Number> Grid<T>.average() : float#

The mean of the cells; NaN for an empty grid.

fun <T : Number> Grid<T>.min() : T#

The least cell; a NoSuchElementException for an empty grid.

fun <T : Number> Grid<T>.max() : T#

The greatest cell; a NoSuchElementException for an empty grid.

fun <T : Number> Grid<T>.rowSums() : List<Number>#

Each row's total, top to bottom.

fun <T : Number> Grid<T>.columnSums() : List<Number>#

Each column's total, left to right.

fun <T> Grid<T>.fill(value : T)#

Every cell set to value.

fun <T> Grid<T>.transposed() : Grid<T>#

The grid turned on its diagonal: height by width, column x of row y moved to column y of row x.

fun <T : Number> Grid<T>.convolve(kernel : Grid<float>, into : Grid<float>? = null) : Grid<float>#

Each cell the sum of its neighbours weighted by kernel (centred on it, its middle the cell itself), the edge cells repeated past the edge: blurs, sharpening, edges, a terrain smoothed.

fun <T : Number> Grid<T>.scaled(width : int, height : int, interpolation : Interpolation = Interpolation.bilinear) : Grid<T>#

The grid resampled to width by height: each cell the average of the part it covers each way it shrinks, read between cells by interpolation each way it grows; ints rounded.

enum Reduce#

How a cell's neighbourhood is reduced, by window(): its least, its greatest, its middle value, its mean, its total. Erode is min, dilate max, denoising median, a box blur mean.

enum WindowShape#

The shape of a neighbourhood: every cell within radius across and down (square), or within radius of the middle (disc).

fun <T : Number> Grid<T>.window(radius : int, reduce : Reduce, shape : WindowShape = WindowShape.square, into : Grid<T>? = null) : Grid<T>#

Each cell its neighbourhood within radius reduced (cells outside the grid left out): erode (Reduce.min), dilate (max), denoise (median), a box blur (mean), local totals (sum). Over a square the cost of a cell does not grow with the radius; over a disc it grows with it once.

fun <T : Number> Grid<T>.sample(xs : Grid<float>, ys : Grid<float>, interpolation : Interpolation = Interpolation.bilinear, outside : Number? = null) : Grid<T>#

A grid the size of xs, each cell read from the place (xs[x, y], ys[x, y]) of this one (whole numbers the middles of its cells), between cells by interpolation: rotation, any affine or perspective transform, lens correction, warps. Outside this grid, outside, or the nearest edge where it is null.
