fun Grid<int>.addAt(xs : List<int>, ys : List<int>, values : List<int> | int)#

Each of values added at its cell (xs[i], ys[i]), a cell given more than once adding each time: points splatted into a grid, a heat map.

fun Grid<float>.addAt(xs : List<int>, ys : List<int>, values : List<Number> | Number)#

The same for a grid of floats.

fun <T : Number> Grid<T>.histogram(bins : int = 256, from : Number? = null, to : Number? = null) : List<int>#

How many cells fall in each of bins equal parts of from to to (the least and the most cell when they are null).

fun <T : Number> Grid<T>.regions(tolerance : Number = 0, diagonal : bool = false) : Grid<int>#

Every connected area of cells numbered from 0, in the order their first cells come row by row: two neighbours (across and down, and corner to corner with diagonal) in one area where they differ by no more than tolerance. The magic wand, a selection's islands, counting shapes.

fun <T : Number> Grid<T>.floodRegion(x : int, y : int, tolerance : Number = 0, diagonal : bool = false) : Mask#

The cells joined to (x, y) through cells that differ from it by no more than tolerance.

fun Grid<bool>.distances() : Grid<float>#

Of a grid of bools: each cell's distance to the nearest true cell, exact (across, down and between), infinity when none is true: a feathered selection, a glow, an outline round a shape.

fun <T> Grid<T>.countDifferent(other : Grid<T>) : int#

Of any grid: how many cells differ from those of another of the same size: for tests, and for undo.

- Grids of colours -

fun Grid<Color>.mapChannels(red : List<int>? = null, green : List<int>? = null, blue : List<int>? = null, alpha : List<int>? = null, into : Grid<Color>? = null) : Grid<Color>#

Each part of each colour through a table of 256 values (0 to 255; a part kept as it is where its table is null): brightness, contrast, gamma, levels, inverting, together in one pass.

fun Grid<Color>.convolve(kernel : Grid<float>, into : Grid<Color>? = null) : Grid<Color>#

Each colour the sum of its neighbours' weighted by kernel, each part on its own, multiplied by alpha on the way so that a see-through edge does not darken; the edge cells repeated past the edge. into may be this grid itself.

fun Grid<Color>.scaled(width : int, height : int, interpolation : Interpolation = Interpolation.bilinear) : Grid<Color>#

The picture resampled to width by height: area-averaged each way it shrinks, read between pixels by interpolation each way it grows; colours weighed by alpha.

Colours convolved and scaled (2026-11-15; were C++'s, in single precision)

In doubles, each pixel's sums in the C++'s order: a row's taps one after another, then the rows. A colour is worked on as four floats, red, green and blue multiplied by its alpha out of 255, and alpha; a whole row of them at a time, each tap of a kernel one multiply-add over the row (__mulAdd, which native code does as a loop template), so that the work of each tap is a loop thousands long rather than four sums of a few taps for each pixel.

fun Grid<Color>.window(radius : int, reduce : Reduce, shape : WindowShape = WindowShape.square, into : Grid<Color>? = null) : Grid<Color>#

Each pixel its neighbourhood within radius reduced, each part of the colour on its own (a mean weighed by alpha, as a blur).

fun Grid<Color>.sample(xs : Grid<float>, ys : Grid<float>, interpolation : Interpolation = Interpolation.bilinear, outside : Color? = null) : Grid<Color>#

A picture the size of xs, each pixel read from the place (xs[x, y], ys[x, y]) of this one, between pixels by interpolation, weighed by alpha; outside it outside, or the nearest edge where it is null.

fun Grid<Color>.histogram(bins : int = 256, from : Number? = null, to : Number? = null) : List<List<int>>#

How the pixels' parts fall in each of bins equal parts of from to to (0 to 256, a bin a level, when they are null): four lists, red, green, blue, alpha. Auto-levels, equalising, counting colours.

fun Grid<Color>.regions(tolerance : int = 0, diagonal : bool = false) : Grid<int>#

Every connected area of pixels numbered from 0, in the order their first pixels come row by row, two neighbours in one area where no part of their colours (alpha too) differs by more than tolerance. (In WinterWolf since 2026-11-13, the numbers the C++ gave: neighbours joined as union-find, each area's root its first pixel.)

fun Grid<Color>.floodRegion(x : int, y : int, tolerance : int = 0, diagonal : bool = false) : Mask#

The pixels joined to (x, y) through pixels whose colour differs from its own by no more than tolerance in any part: the magic wand, a flood fill's area.

fun Grid<Color>.fill(color : Color, where : Mask)#

The pixels of where (every pixel when it is null) set to color.

enum BlendMode#

The ways a picture is laid over another, by their names in CSS's mix-blend-mode and the W3C's compositing: normal; multiply, screen and overlay; darken and lighten; colorDodge and colorBurn; hardLight and softLight; difference and exclusion; and hue, saturation, color and luminosity, which take one of those from the picture laid over and the rest from beneath.

fun Grid<Color>.blend(over : Grid<Color>, x : int = 0, y : int = 0, opacity : Number = 1.0, mode : BlendMode = BlendMode.normal)#

over laid over this picture with its top left corner at (x, y), by its alpha times opacity, in a blend mode: layers, markup, pasting. What falls outside this picture is left out.

class ColorLookup#

Any colour-to-colour function as a table of 33 by 33 by 33 colours, worked out once (the function called 35,937 times), for pixels.mapColors to put a picture through in one pass: saturation, hue, colour balance, sepia, grading, all at once, as photo editors do it. Between the table's colours the answer is mixed, so a function that bends sharply between them is off by about a level in 255; exact work on each part on its own is mapChannels'. A colour's own alpha is kept, times the alpha the function gives.

val sepia = ColorLookup { c -> Color(Math.min(255, (c.red * 0.39 + c.green * 0.77 + c.blue * 0.19).toInt()), ...) }
fun Grid<Color>.mapColors(lookup : ColorLookup, into : Grid<Color>? = null) : Grid<Color>#

Each colour put through a ColorLookup's table.

- Masks -

class Mask(val pixels : Grid<bool>)#

Which pixels of a picture something applies to (question 5): what floodRegion gives, and what grid.fill(color, where = mask) fills. A grid of bools, true for each pixel in it.

val width : int#

Its width, in pixels.

val height : int#

Its height, in pixels.

op [](x : int, y : int) : bool#

Whether the pixel (x, y) is in it; an IndexOutOfBoundsException outside it.

val count : int#

How many pixels are in it.

val bounds : Rect?#

The smallest rectangle holding every pixel in it; null when it holds none.

override fun toString() : string#

The mask as text: "Mask(30 x 20, 140 pixels)".

paint.ww

Paints, composites and pictures through a transform (N3)

interface Paint#

What shapes, outlines and text are filled with, as Java's Paint: a Color, a LinearGradient or a RadialGradient. A canvas's paint is one of them; setting its color sets its paint to that colour.

enum CycleMethod#

What a gradient does past its ends, as Java's MultipleGradientPaint.CycleMethod: keeps the end colours (pad), goes back the other way (reflect), or starts again (repeat).

value LinearGradient(val x1 : Number, val y1 : Number, val x2 : Number, val y2 : Number, val fractions : List<Number>, val colors : List<Color>, val cycle : CycleMethod = CycleMethod.pad) : Paint#

Colours changing along a line, as Java's LinearGradientPaint: colors[i] at fractions[i] of the way from (x1, y1) to (x2, y2), in between mixed, and lines across it of one colour. The fractions go up from 0 to 1, one for each colour, two or more. Its points go through the canvas's transform as what is drawn does.

canvas.paint = LinearGradient(0, 0, 100, 0, [0, 1], [Color.red, Color.blue])
canvas.fillRect(0, 0, 100, 40)
fun colorAt(x : Number, y : Number) : Color#

The colour at (x, y). An IllegalArgumentException for fractions and colours that do not go together.

value RadialGradient(val cx : Number, val cy : Number, val radius : Number, val fractions : List<Number>, val colors : List<Color>, val cycle : CycleMethod = CycleMethod.pad, val fx : Number? = null, val fy : Number? = null) : Paint#

Colours changing out from a point, as Java's RadialGradientPaint: colors[i] at fractions[i] of the way from the focus (fx, fy, the centre unless given) to the circle of radius round (cx, cy). A focus outside the circle is brought in, as Java brings it.

canvas.paint = RadialGradient(50, 50, 40, [0, 1], [Color.white, Color.black], fx = 35, fy = 35)
fun colorAt(x : Number, y : Number) : Color#

The colour at (x, y). An IllegalArgumentException for fractions and colours that do not go together, or a radius not above 0.

enum Composite#

How what is drawn is put together with what is there, as Java's AlphaComposite: laid over it (srcOver), or clearing it where it would have been drawn (clear), what is under the picture showing through.

enum Interpolation#

How a picture's pixels are read between them when it is drawn larger, smaller or turned, as Java's RenderingHints: the nearest one, a mix of the four round the place (bilinear), or of the sixteen (bicubic, sharper).

parallel.ww

Parallel sections

The items of a list worked on by several cores at once, on the objects themselves:

balls.parallelStream().forEach { it.move(width, height, steps) }
val totals = orders.parallelStream().map { priceOf(it) }.toList()

Threads take turns, and workers work on copies. Here the cores really run at the same time on the same objects, which is safe because of one rule that the runtime holds each core to:

- a core may change the item it was given, and anything it makes itself;
- everything else it may read and not change: the variables around the lambda, other
  lists and objects, and whatever an item holds that is an object of its own.

A lambda that keeps to that runs on every core. One that does not, { it.move(); moved.add(it) } say, gets an error the first time it runs: it never damages anything. Results come back by being returned: map, filter, sumOf and the rest put them together afterwards.

Nothing waits inside a section: no lock, no reading or writing, no setting a task aside. The section itself is an ordinary call, which returns when every core has finished. A section may start sections of its own, and parallel({ a }, { b }) runs functions at once; the cores share the work out between them as they come free. What every core may change is an Atomic, an atomic var field, a SharedMap or a SharedSet; gather gives each core a result of its own, merged at the end. An image is drawn on by several cores through the parts of its canvas, image.canvas().split(), a part to a piece.

class ParallelStream<T>#

A list's items for every core to work on at once, from parallelStream(): the items themselves, not copies. map, filter and the like give a new stream; toList() and the totals end one.

fun forEach(action : (T) -> unit)#

Runs the action on every item, the items dealt out to the cores.

fun replaceAll(transform : (T) -> T)#

Each item replaced by what the function gives for it, the items dealt out to the cores. An item of a list of values is where its result goes: balls.parallelStream().replaceAll { it.moved() } builds each new ball where the old one was, with nothing made on the side.

fun <R> map(transform : (T) -> R) : ParallelStream<R>#

What the function gives for each item, in the order of the items, as a stream to go on from.

fun <R> mapNotNull(transform : (T) -> R?) : ParallelStream<R>#

What the function gives for each item, the nulls left out, in the order of the items, as a stream to go on from.

fun filter(predicate : (T) -> bool) : ParallelStream<T>#

The items for which the condition holds.

fun count() : int#

How many items there are.

fun count(predicate : (T) -> bool) : int#

How many items predicate is true for, each tested on one of the cores.

fun any(predicate : (T) -> bool) : bool#

Whether predicate is true for any item; every item is tested, on the cores. False when there are none.

fun all(predicate : (T) -> bool) : bool#

Whether predicate is true for every item; every item is tested, on the cores. True when there are none.

fun none(predicate : (T) -> bool) : bool#

Whether predicate is true for no item; every item is tested, on the cores. True when there are none.

fun <R : Number> sumOf(selector : (T) -> R) : R#

The total of what selector gives for each item, each worked out on one of the cores; for none, a zero of the kind selector gives, 0 or 0.0.

fun <R> maxOf(selector : (T) -> R) : R#

The largest of what selector gives for each item, each worked out on one of the cores: NoSuchElementException when there are none.

fun <R> minOf(selector : (T) -> R) : R#

The smallest of what selector gives for each item, each worked out on one of the cores: NoSuchElementException when there are none.

fun sum() : any?#

The total of the items, which have to be numbers (ClassCastException otherwise); 0 for none.

fun max() : T#

The largest item, in natural order: NoSuchElementException when there are none.

fun min() : T#

The smallest item, in natural order: NoSuchElementException when there are none.

fun toList() : open List<T>#

The items as a list: after map or filter, what they came to.

fun toSet() : open Set<T>#

The items as a new set, each once, in the order they first come.

fun sorted() : open List<T>#

The items in their natural order, as a new list.

fun join(separator : string = ", ") : string#

The items as text, separator between each two: [3, 1].parallelStream().join() is "3, 1".

fun <R> flatMap(transform : (T) -> List<R>) : ParallelStream<R>#

What the function gives for each item, every list of them joined into one, in order.

fun reduce(operation : (T, T) -> T) : T#

One answer from every item, as on a list: the items combined two at a time, each core's share on its own, then the cores' answers. operation has to give the same whichever way its operands are grouped, as + and max do.

fun <R> gather(into : () -> R, each : (R, T) -> unit, merge : (R, R) -> unit) : R#

A result of its own for each core, made empty by into, every item added to one of them by each, and the results merged into the first by merge once every core is done. Nothing is shared while the cores work:

val counts = words.parallelStream().gather(
  into = { val counts : open Map<string, int> = {:}; counts },
  each = { counts, word -> counts[word] = (counts[word] ?: 0) + 1 },
  merge = { a, b -> for ((word, n) in b) { a[word] = (a[word] ?: 0) + n } })
fun <T> List<T>.parallelStream() : ParallelStream<T>#

A stream of the list's items for the cores to work on, the items themselves: what forEach does to them it does to the list's.

fun Range.parallelStream() : ParallelStream<int>#

A stream of the range's numbers, in order, for the cores to work on.

fun Bytes.parallelChunks(action : (Range) -> unit)#

The buffer in as many parts as there are cores, each core given the positions of one part: it may write those and no others.

pixels.parallelChunks { positions -> for i in positions { pixels[i] = shade(i) } }
fun <T> open List<T>.parallelChunks(action : (Range) -> unit)#

The same for a list of numbers or of values: each core writes the positions of its part.

fun <T> parallel(vararg work : () -> T) : List<T>#

Runs the functions at once, on whatever cores are free, and gives what they return when all are done, in order. What a function captures every one may read and none may change; a view of a list from splitAt that a function captures is that function's own to write.

val (left, right) = parallel({ count(a) }, { count(b) })
fun <T> open List<T>.parallelSort()#

The list sorted in place, its parts on every core: in their natural order, as sort() gives it, an object's by its compareTo.

Views of a list

class ListView<T>#

A stretch of a list, read and changed in place: what splitAt hands out. Two views from one splitAt never overlap, so each may go to a function of its own in a parallel(...), which writes through it whatever else is going on, and divide and conquer can be written in place:

fun increment(view : ListView<int>) {
  if (view.size < 1000) { for (i in view.indices) { view[i] += 1 } }
  else { view.splitAt(view.size / 2) { left, right -> parallel({ increment(left) }, { increment(right) }) } }
}
xs.splitAt(xs.size / 2) { left, right -> ... }
val size : int#

How many items the view has.

op [](index : int) : T#

The item at index, counted from the view's start: IndexOutOfBoundsException outside 0..<size. In a parallel section where views of the list are given out, each piece reads it through its own.

op []=(index : int, item : T)#

Sets the item at index, counted from the view's start, in the list itself: IndexOutOfBoundsException outside 0..<size. IllegalStateException while the view is split, once its splitAt's block has ended, or in a parallel section if the view is not this piece's own.

val indices : Range#

The view's positions, 0..<size.

fun isEmpty() : bool#

Whether the view has no items.

fun splitAt(mid : int, block : (ListView<T>, ListView<T>) -> unit)#

Its first mid items and the rest, as two views, to the block; meanwhile this one is lent to them, and nothing is written through it or split again. The two are for the block: once it ends they are written through no more.

fun sort()#

Its items sorted in place, in their natural order.

fun toList() : open List<T>#

The view's items, copied into a new list.

fun iterator() : List<T>#

For a for: the view's items, copied into a list first.

override fun toString() : string#

The view's items written as a list: [1, 2].

fun <T> open List<T>.splitAt(mid : int, block : (ListView<T>, ListView<T>) -> unit)#

The list's first mid items and the rest, as two views that never overlap, to the block. The list is lent to them while it runs: split again meanwhile, it is split through them (left.splitAt(...)), so that no two views of it in use overlap. In a parallel section given its views, each piece reads and writes the list through its own, and nothing else looks into it.

Shared collections

A map and a set any core of a parallel section may change, each change done whole:

val seen = SharedSet<string>()
pages.parallelStream().forEach { page -> for (link in page.links) { seen.add(link) } }

Keys and values are what every core may read: values, text, frozen data, or objects not changed in the section. There is no shared list: cores adding as they finish would give a different order each run; map, flatMap and gather collect results in order, with nothing shared.

class SharedMap<K, V>#

A map any core of a parallel section may change, each change done whole. In a section, a key or a value that is an object the core itself made, and may still change, is an IllegalStateException.

op [](key : K) : V?#

The key's value, or null if it has none.

op []=(key : K, value : V)#

Sets the key's value.

fun get(key : K) : V?#

The key's value, or null if it has none: the same as [key].

fun put(key : K, value : V) : V?#

Sets the key's value, giving the one it had.

fun remove(key : K) : V?#

Takes the key out, giving its value.

fun containsKey(key : K) : bool#

Whether the key has a value.

val size : int#

How many keys it holds now.

fun putIfAbsent(key : K, make : () -> V) : V#

The key's value, made by make and put in if there is none: make may run on two cores at once for one key, and only one value is kept.

fun update(key : K, change : (V?) -> V) : V#

Sets the key's value to what change gives for the one it has (null for none), in one step: change may run more than once when cores contend. Gives the new value.

fun toMap() : open Map<K, V>#

A copy of what it holds now, an ordinary map.

override fun toString() : string#

What it holds now, written as a map: {"x": 1}.

class SharedSet<T>#

A set any core of a parallel section may change, each change done whole. In a section, an item that is an object the core itself made, and may still change, is an IllegalStateException.

fun add(item : T) : bool#

Adds the item: whether it was not there.

fun remove(item : T) : bool#

Takes the item out: whether it was there.

fun contains(item : T) : bool#

Whether the item is in it.

val size : int#

How many items it holds now.

fun toSet() : open Set<T>#

A copy of what it holds now, an ordinary set.

override fun toString() : string#

What it holds now, written as a set: {1, 2}.

fun availableProcessors() : int#

How many cores a parallel section uses on this machine.

path.ww

Paths and transforms (N3)

enum FillRule#

Which parts of a path whose contours cross or lie inside each other are inside it, as Java's WIND_NON_ZERO and WIND_EVEN_ODD. With nonZero, a part the contours go round more times one way than the other: a contour inside another going the same way fills, one going the other way makes a hole. With evenOdd, a part inside an odd number of contours: one inside another makes a hole whichever way it goes.

value Transform(val scaleX : float = 1.0, val shearY : float = 0.0, val shearX : float = 0.0, val scaleY : float = 1.0, val translateX : float = 0.0, val translateY : float = 0.0)#

A change of place for every point of the plane: moved, turned, stretched, slanted, or several of these at once, as Java's AffineTransform. A point (x, y) goes to

x' = scaleX * x + shearX * y + translateX
y' = shearY * x + scaleY * y + translateY

It is a value: translate, scale, rotate and shear each give a new transform. As in Java, what each adds is done first, before what the transform already does, which is how a canvas's transform builds up: canvas.translate(50, 50) then canvas.rotate(a) turns what is drawn about the point (50, 50). t.then(u) is the other way: t first, then u.

val t = Transform.identity.translate(50, 50).rotate(Math.pi / 4)
val (x, y) = t.apply(10, 0)          where (10, 0) lands
val back = t.inverse()               the transform that undoes t

Angles are in radians, as Java's: from the x axis towards the y axis, which on a canvas, whose y grows downwards, is clockwise.

val identity#

The transform that leaves every point where it is.

fun translation(dx : Number, dy : Number) : Transform#

Moves every point dx across and dy down.

fun scaling(sx : Number, sy : Number? = null) : Transform#

Stretches by sx across and sy down (sx both ways when sy is not given), away from (0, 0).

fun rotation(angle : Number, cx : Number = 0, cy : Number = 0) : Transform#

Turns by angle radians about (cx, cy): clockwise on a canvas. Quarter turns are exact, as Java makes them.

fun shearing(shx : Number, shy : Number) : Transform#

Slants: each point moves across by shx times its y, and down by shy times its x.

val isIdentity : bool#

Whether it leaves every point where it is.

val determinant : float#

How many times it makes an area larger: below 0 when it also mirrors, 0 when it flattens everything onto a line (and cannot be undone).

fun translate(dx : Number, dy : Number) : Transform#

This transform after a move of dx across and dy down.

fun scale(sx : Number, sy : Number? = null) : Transform#

This transform after stretching by sx across and sy down (sx both ways when sy is not given).

fun rotate(angle : Number, cx : Number = 0, cy : Number = 0) : Transform#

This transform after turning by angle radians about (cx, cy).

fun shear(shx : Number, shy : Number) : Transform#

This transform after slanting by shx and shy, as Transform.shearing.

fun then(next : Transform) : Transform#

This transform, then next.

fun inverse() : Transform#

The transform that puts every point back where this one found it. An IllegalStateException for one that cannot be undone, its determinant 0: it flattens everything onto a line.

fun apply(x : Number, y : Number) : Pair<float, float>#

Where the point (x, y) goes: its x and y, to take apart, val (px, py) = t.apply(x, y).

override fun toString() : string#

The transform as text, its two rows: "Transform([1.0, 0.0, 5.0], [0.0, 1.0, 0.0])".

class Path(var fillRule : FillRule = FillRule.nonZero) : Shape#

A shape of straight lines and curves, as Java's Path2D: made a piece at a time, or read from the path data of an SVG picture.

val arrow = Path().moveTo(0, 10).lineTo(20, 10).lineTo(20, 0).lineTo(35, 15).lineTo(20, 30).lineTo(20, 20).lineTo(0, 20).close()
val drop = Path.parse("M20 0 C20 20 40 30 40 40 A20 20 0 0 1 0 40 C0 30 20 20 20 0 Z")
canvas.fill(arrow)

It is made of contours: each starts with moveTo and goes on with lines and curves; close() draws a straight line back to where it started. A filled path fills every contour as if it were closed, and where contours cross or lie inside each other, fillRule says what is inside. Its numbers are in units, as everything else drawn on a canvas.

fun parse(d : string) : Path#

A path read from SVG path data, the d of a <path>: M, L, H, V, C, S, Q, T, A and Z, in capitals for places and in small letters for steps from the last point, the numbers apart by spaces or commas or by nothing where they cannot run together ("M10-5.5.5"). An IllegalArgumentException where the data is not right, saying at which character (from 0) and what was expected there.

val isEmpty : bool#

Whether it has nothing in it yet.

fun moveTo(x : Number, y : Number) : Path#

Starts a contour at (x, y). A moveTo right after another takes its place.

fun lineTo(x : Number, y : Number) : Path#

A straight line on to (x, y).

fun quadTo(cx : Number, cy : Number, x : Number, y : Number) : Path#

A curve on to (x, y) that bends towards (cx, cy): a quadratic Bézier curve.

fun curveTo(c1x : Number, c1y : Number, c2x : Number, c2y : Number, x : Number, y : Number) : Path#

A curve on to (x, y) that leaves towards (c1x, c1y) and arrives from (c2x, c2y): a cubic Bézier curve.

fun arcTo(rx : Number, ry : Number, rotation : Number, largeArc : bool, sweep : bool, x : Number, y : Number) : Path#

A part of an ellipse on to (x, y), as SVG's A: the ellipse rx across and ry down, turned by rotation degrees; of the four arcs that join the points, the larger or the smaller one (largeArc), going clockwise on a canvas or not (sweep). Made of cubic curves. An ellipse too small to reach is made larger until it does, as SVG says; with rx or ry 0 it is a straight line.

fun close() : Path#

Closes the contour with a straight line back to where it began. A line or a curve after it starts a new contour there.

fun transformed(t : Transform) : Path#

The path with every point moved by t, its fill rule the same. Curves stay curves.

override val bounds : Rect#

The smallest rectangle of whole units that holds the whole path: the curves' own extremes, not their control points'. An empty path's is Rect(0, 0, 0 x 0).

override fun contains(px : float, py : float) : bool#

Whether the point (px, py) is inside the path filled by its fill rule, each contour closed; on an edge it may be either.

fun flatten(pixels : float) : List<float>#

The path in straight lines, as a canvas fills it: for each contour, how many points it has and then their x and y, one after another. Curves are cut into as many pieces as their bend needs at pixels pixels a unit, to within about a hundredth of a pixel. A contour of one point is left out.

override fun toString() : string#

The path as SVG path data, in places: "M0 10 L20 10 Q30 0 40 10 Z".

The canvas's shapes as paths, for filling them through a transform that turns them

picture.ww

Palettes and dithering

General picture operations over a picture's pixels, a Grid<Color> (image.pixels, or a grid of one's own): the colours that stand best for a picture, and the picture put into them. What GIF's 256 colours need, and posterising. The palette in WinterWolf (since 2026-11-13); the nearest colours and dithering in C++ (each pixel's index, paletteIndices), until native code carries the WinterWolf written for them at C++'s speed (bench/spike/gif_nearest.ww, 2026-11-17).

enum Dither#

How a picture put into a few colours spreads each pixel's error to the pixels after it: not at all, each pixel its nearest colour; Floyd and Steinberg's way, 7/16 of it to the right and 3/16, 5/16 and 1/16 to the three below; or Atkinson's, 1/8 to each of six neighbours (three quarters in all, so lighter and of more contrast, as the first Macintosh had it).

fun Grid<Color>.palette(count : int = 256) : List<Color>#

The colours that stand best for the picture, at most count (1 to 256): its own colours where it has no more than that, in the order first met, or else an octree's (its colours to 5 bits a channel, the branches with fewest pixels joined until few enough are left), each the average of the pixels it stands for. See-through pixels (alpha below 128) are left out; every colour given is solid.

val colors = image.pixels.palette(16)
val poster = image.pixels.dither(colors, Dither.none)
fun Grid<Color>.dither(palette : List<Color>, method : Dither = Dither.floydSteinberg) : Grid<Color>#

The picture with each pixel the nearest of palette's colours (the channels weighted as the eye sees them), its error spread by method; a see-through pixel (alpha below 128) clear. The palette's alpha is not read: every colour is solid.

fun Grid<Color>.paletteIndices(palette : List<Color>, method : Dither = Dither.floydSteinberg) : Bytes#

Each pixel's index in palette as dither chooses it, a byte each, row after row: what a picture file of indexed colours holds (GIF's, PNG's of a palette). A see-through pixel's index is palette.size, so a palette of 256 colours leaves none for one (an IllegalArgumentException).

val colors = image.pixels.palette(16)
val indices = image.pixels.paletteIndices(colors, Dither.none)

The DCT of 8 by 8 blocks, and planes

object Dct#

The discrete cosine transform of a plane's 8 by 8 blocks, both ways, as JPEG has it (and video, and picture analysis): samples 0 to 255, 128 taken off, made 64 coefficients a block, and back. Coefficients are kept as 16-bit numbers, two bytes each (lowest first), 64 a block in natural order (across, then down), the blocks one after another across the plane, then down.

fun inverse8x8(coefficients : Bytes, blocksWide : int, blocksHigh : int, plane : Bytes, stride : int, scale : List<int>? = null)#

The samples of blocksWide by blocksHigh blocks of coefficients, into plane (rows stride bytes apart, from its start), each block's coefficients first multiplied by scale's 64 steps (natural order) where it is given: a JPEG's dequantization. In integers (libjpeg's slow integer method, as stb_image has it), the samples kept to 0 to 255.

fun forward8x8(plane : Bytes, stride : int, blocksWide : int, blocksHigh : int, divisors : List<int>? = null) : Bytes#

The coefficients of blocksWide by blocksHigh blocks of plane (rows stride bytes apart), each divided by divisors' 64 steps (natural order) and rounded where they are given: a JPEG's quantization. AAN's method in floats (libjpeg's float method).

fun Bytes.scaledPlane(width : int, height : int, toWidth : int, toHeight : int, stride : int = width) : Bytes#

The plane (one byte a sample, width by height, rows stride bytes apart) at another size, toWidth by toHeight: twice either way (or one less, for an odd size) by a triangle filter, 3 parts the nearest sample and 1 the next, as libjpeg's "fancy" upsampling; half either way by averaging; any other size by bilinear interpolation between the samples' centres.

range.ww

Ranges

A range can be looped over, and has the methods of a list that only read: each makes a list of its numbers where it needs one.

inline fun Range.forEach(action : (int) -> unit)#

Calls action with each number of the range, in order.

inline fun <R> Range.map(transform : (int) -> R) : open List<R>#

What transform gives for each number, in order, as a new list.

inline fun <R> Range.mapNotNull(transform : (int) -> R?) : open List<R>#

What transform gives for each number, the nulls left out, as a new list.

inline fun <R> Range.flatMap(transform : (int) -> List<R>) : open List<R>#

The items of the lists transform gives for each number, one after the other, as a new list.

inline fun Range.filter(predicate : (int) -> bool) : open List<int>#

The numbers for which predicate is true, in their order, as a new list.

fun Range.any() : bool#

Whether the range has any numbers.

inline fun Range.any(predicate : (int) -> bool) : bool#

Whether predicate is true of at least one number; it stops at the first that it is true of.

inline fun Range.all(predicate : (int) -> bool) : bool#

Whether predicate is true of every number; true for an empty range.

fun Range.none() : bool#

Whether the range is empty, as 1..0 is.

inline fun Range.none(predicate : (int) -> bool) : bool#

Whether predicate is true of no number; true for an empty range.

fun Range.count() : int#

How many numbers the range has: its size.

inline fun Range.count(predicate : (int) -> bool) : int#

How many numbers predicate is true of.

fun Range.firstOrNull() : int?#

The first number, its start; null for an empty range.

inline fun Range.firstOrNull(predicate : (int) -> bool) : int?#

The first number, in the range's order, for which predicate is true; null where there is none.

inline fun Range.find(predicate : (int) -> bool) : int?#

The same as firstOrNull(predicate).

fun Range.sum() : int#

The sum of the numbers; 0 for an empty range.

inline fun <R : Number> Range.sumOf(selector : (int) -> R) : R#

The sum of what selector gives for each number; for an empty range a zero of the kind selector gives, 0 or 0.0.

inline fun <V> Range.associateWith(valueSelector : (int) -> V) : open Map<int, V>#

A new map from each number to what valueSelector gives for it, in the range's order.

(1..3).associateWith { it * it }   // {1: 1, 2: 4, 3: 9}
fun Range.isNotEmpty() : bool#

Whether the range has any numbers.

fun Range.reversed() : open List<int>#

The numbers last to first, as a new list: (1..3).reversed() is [3, 2, 1].

fun Range.toOpenList() : open List<int>#

The numbers in order, as a new list that can be changed.

fun Range.toSet() : open Set<int>#

The numbers as a new set, in the range's order.

fun Range.random() : int#

One of the numbers, picked at random, its step kept: (1..10 step 3).random() is 1, 4, 7 or 10. A NoSuchElementException for an empty range.

fun Range.join(separator : string = ", ", prefix : string = "", postfix : string = "", limit : int = -1, truncated : string = "...", transform : ((int) -> any?)? = null) : string#

The numbers as text, as a list's join() gives them: separator between them, prefix and postfix around them; with a limit of 0 or more, only the first limit, then truncated where there are more. transform, when given, makes what is shown of each.

(1..5).join(limit = 2)   // "1, 2, ..."

reflect.ww

Reflection

Image# is the class named Image and x# the class of the object x: a Class. It tells the class's name, what it extends and implements and the type arguments it was given, and lists its fields, methods and constructors, as far as the code where the # is written may use them: what a class keeps private is listed only to that class's own code. A member listed is read, set or called by its name, through what lists it.

In a generic function or class, T# is the class of the type argument T was given:

fun <T> make() : T => T#.constructors[0].call() as T

What a method or constructor called this way throws arrives wrapped in a checked InvocationTargetException, the original in cause. A call the member cannot take, as one with too few arguments or of the wrong types, or on an object of another class, is an IllegalArgumentException of its own.

class Class(internal val type : Type, internal val asker : any? = null)#

A class, or a built-in type, as Image# or x# gives it, with its type arguments (the class of a Box<int> is Box<int>), and as items.itemType gives what a list was made to hold: a type as the program runs is always a Class. What it lists is what the code where the # is written may use. type is the type it stands for, which only the standard library sees.

val name : string#

The class's name, or a built-in type's: "Circle", "int", "List".

val origin : string#

Where it comes from: "ww" for a built-in type and the standard library's classes, a library's name for its classes ("Jade"), and the program's name (Program.name) for the program's own.

val superclass : Class?#

The class it extends, with the type arguments it gives it; null where it extends none.

val interfaces : List<Class>#

Every interface it implements: those of the classes above it, those the interfaces extend, and those an extend fitted it to.

val typeArguments : List<Class>#

What it was given, as classes: [int#] for the class of a Box<int>; none for Box#.

val kinds : List<Class>#

The kinds of an enum class, in the order they are written: none for any other.

val isData : bool#

Whether it is a data class: compared, printed and taken apart by the fields in its header.

val entries : List<any>#

An enum's entries, in the order they are written, as Name.entries gives them: none for any other class.

val fields : List<Field>#

Its fields and properties, those of the classes above it first: each one the code that wrote the # may use.

val methods : List<Method>#

Its methods, those it inherits included, the same way.

val constructors : List<Constructor>#

Its constructors: none for an interface, an abstract class, an object or an enum.

val staticFields : List<Field>#

Its own static fields, each one the code that wrote the # may use: read and set with no object.

val staticMethods : List<Method>#

Its own static methods, the same way: called with null for the object.

val annotations : List<any>#

The annotations written on it.

fun <A> annotation() : A?#

The first annotation written on it that is an A, as Shape#.annotation<Tag>(); null where there is none.

fun field(name : string) : Field?#

The field of that name; null where there is none the code may use.

fun method(name : string, vararg parameterTypes : Class) : Method?#

The method named name: with parameterTypes, the form whose parameters are of exactly those classes (method("scale", float#, float#), G44), and without, the first of its forms; null where there is none the code may use.

fun staticField(name : string) : Field?#

The static field of that name, as field finds one.

fun staticMethod(name : string, vararg parameterTypes : Class) : Method?#

The static method of that name, as method finds one.

fun isInstance(value : any?) : bool#

Whether a value is an object of the class, or of a class below it.

op ==(other : any?) : bool#

Whether other is a Class for the same type, type arguments included: Box(1)# == Box(2)#, but Box(1)# != Box("a")#.

fun hashCode() : int#

A number from its type, the same for two Classes that are ==.

fun toString() : string#

The type with its type arguments, as "Box<int>" or "string".

fun namedFrom(name : string, classes : List<Class>) : Class?#

The class among classes whose name is name, or null: a name read from a file finds only a class the program lists, as Class.namedFrom(kind, [Circle#, Square#]).

class Field(val owner : Class, entry : List<any?>)#

A field or a property of a class, or a static one. owner is the Class that lists it.

val name : string#

Its name, as written in the class.

val type : Class#

What it holds, as a class: any? where its type cannot be named as the program runs. It lists what the code that asked for owner may use, as owner does (8.6d).

val annotations : List<any>#

The annotations written on it, in their order.

val isMutable : bool#

Whether set may change it: a var, or a property with a setter, that the code may assign to.

val isStatic : bool#

Whether it is a static field of the class, read and set with no object.

fun get(obj : any? = null) : any? throws InvocationTargetException#

Its value in obj, an object of the class; for a static field, its value, obj left out. A property's getter runs.

fun set(obj : any?, value : any?) throws InvocationTargetException#

Gives it a new value in obj, which has to fit its type; for a static field, obj is null. A property's setter runs.

fun <A> annotation() : A?#

The first annotation written on it that is an A; null where there is none.

fun toString() : string#

Its name and type, as "count : int".

class Parameter(entry : List<any?>, asker : any? = null)#

A parameter of a method or a constructor.

val name : string#

Its name, as written in the declaration.

val type : Class#

The type an argument for it has to be, as a class, listing what the code that asked for the method's class may use (8.6d).

val isOptional : bool#

Whether a call may leave it out, for its default.

val annotations : List<any>#

The annotations written on it, in their order.

fun <A> annotation() : A?#

The first annotation written on it that is an A; null where there is none.

fun toString() : string#

Its name and type, as "extra : string"; a default is not shown.

class Method(val owner : Class, entry : List<any?>, val isStatic : bool = false)#

A method of a class, or a static one (isStatic, G44), called with no object. owner is the Class that lists it.

val name : string#

Its name; the forms of a method with several share it.

val parameters : List<Parameter>#

Its parameters, in their order.

val result : Class?#

What it gives, as a class; null where it gives nothing. It lists what the code that asked for owner may use, as owner does (8.6d).

val annotations : List<any>#

The annotations written on it, in their order.

fun call(obj : any?, vararg args : any?) : any? throws InvocationTargetException#

Runs it on obj, an object of the class, with args; a static method with null for obj.

fun <A> annotation() : A?#

The first annotation written on it that is an A; null where there is none.

fun toString() : string#

Its name, parameters and result, as "twice(n : int, extra : string) : int".

class Constructor(val owner : Class, entry : List<any?>)#

A constructor of a class. owner is the Class it makes objects of.

val parameters : List<Parameter>#

Its parameters, in their order.

val annotations : List<any>#

The annotations written on it, in their order.

fun call(vararg args : any?) : any throws InvocationTargetException#

A new object of the class, with its type arguments, made from args.

fun callBy(arguments : Map<string, any?>) : any throws InvocationTargetException#

A new object of the class made from arguments by the parameters' names, each one left out given its default, as a call by name is: Point#.constructors[0].callBy({"y": 2}). An IllegalArgumentException for a name it has no parameter of, a value of the wrong type, or a parameter left out that has no default.

fun <A> annotation() : A?#

The first annotation written on it that is an A; null where there is none.

fun toString() : string#

The class's name and its parameters, as "Box(item : int, count : int)".

regex.ww

Regular expressions (N5, M8)

Patterns in Java's syntax, matched by character (code point), with Kotlin's Regex around them, its names and its meaning:

val date = Regex("(?<year>\\d{4})-(\\d\\d)-(\\d\\d)")
val m = date.find("due 2026-11-06, paid") ?: return
print(m.value, m.groupValues[2], m.groups["year"]?.value, m.range)   // 2026-11-06 11 2026 4..13
"a1b22c333".split(Regex("\\d+"))                                      // [a, b, c, ]
"John Smith".replace(Regex("(\\w+) (\\w+)"), "$2, $1")                // Smith, John
for (word in Regex("\\w+").findAll(text)) { ... }

The syntax is Java's: classes ([a-z&&[^aeiou]], \d, \w, \s, \p{Lu}, \p{IsAlphabetic}), groups (capturing, (?:...), named (?<name>...)), alternation, greedy, lazy and possessive repeats (*, *?, *+, {2,5}), anchors (^, $, \b, \A, \z, \Z, \G), look-ahead and look-behind, atomic groups, back references (\1, \k<name>), inline options ((?i), (?m:...)), \Q...\E. \d, \w, \s and \b are ASCII, as in Java and Kotlin; \p{L} and the other Unicode classes (the general categories, IsAlphabetic, IsWhite_Space, javaLowerCase and the rest) and case without regard to it are Java's, from WinterWolf's own Unicode data (16.0), as char.isLetter() and string.upper() are: the same on every system. A pattern that is no regular expression is a PatternSyntaxException.

enum RegexOption(internal val bit : int)#

How a pattern is read, as Kotlin's RegexOption.

class Regex#

A compiled pattern.

val pattern : string#

The pattern as it was given.

val options : Set<RegexOption>#

The options it was given.

fun matches(input : string) : bool#

Whether the whole of input matches.

fun matchesAt(input : string, index : int) : bool#

Whether a match starts at index in input.

fun containsMatchIn(input : string) : bool#

Whether the pattern matches somewhere in input.

fun find(input : string, startIndex : int = 0) : MatchResult?#

The first match at or after startIndex, or null.

fun findAll(input : string, startIndex : int = 0) : Sequence<MatchResult>#

Every match, one after another, from startIndex; after a match of nothing the next is looked for one character on.

fun matchEntire(input : string) : MatchResult?#

The match of the whole of input, or null.

fun matchAt(input : string, index : int) : MatchResult?#

The match that starts at index, or null.

fun spansAt(input : string, index : int) : List<int>?#

The match at index, as matchAt finds it, given as where it and each group start and end, the end left out: [start, end, group 1's start, group 1's end, ...], -1 for a group that took no part; null where there is no match. No MatchResult is made, for readers that match many times.

Regex("(\\w+)=(\\d+)").spansAt("x a=12", 2)   // [2, 6, 2, 3, 4, 6]
fun allSpans(input : string, startIndex : int = 0, endIndex : int = input.size) : List<int>#

Every match from startIndex, one after another as findAll finds them, up to the first that does not end by endIndex: their places one after another in one list, each as spansAt gives them (so 2 for the whole and 2 for each group).

Regex("(\\w)=(\\d)").allSpans("a=1 b=2")   // [0, 3, 0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 7, 4, 5, 6, 7]
fun replace(input : string, replacement : string) : string#

Every match replaced by replacement, in which $1 is a group's text, ${name} a named group's, and \$ a dollar sign (Regex.escapeReplacement makes text that means itself).

fun replace(input : string, transform : (MatchResult) -> string) : string#

Every match replaced by what transform makes of it.

fun replaceFirst(input : string, replacement : string) : string#

The first match replaced by replacement ($1 and the rest as in replace).

fun split(input : string, limit : int = 0) : List<string>#

The pieces of input between the matches, the empty ones kept; up to limit of them (0 for as many as there are), the last holding the rest.

fun splitToSequence(input : string, limit : int = 0) : Sequence<string>#

The pieces, as a sequence.

override fun toString() : string#

The pattern.

fun escape(literal : string) : string#

A pattern that matches literal as it is: \Q...\E around it, as Java's Pattern.quote.

fun escapeReplacement(literal : string) : string#

A replacement that puts literal in as it is: each \ and $ escaped.

fun fromLiteral(literal : string) : Regex#

A Regex that finds literal as it is.

class MatchResult#

A match: where it is, what it is, and each group's.

val range : Range#

Where it is in the input, its last character included (4..13); start..start-1 for a match of nothing.

val value : string#

What it matched.

val groupValues : List<string>#

The whole match, then each group's text, "" for a group that took no part.

val groups : MatchGroupCollection#

Each group, by number (0 the whole match) or by name: groups[1]?.value, groups["year"].

val destructured : List<string>#

The groups' texts without the whole match, to take apart: val (year, month) = m.destructured.

fun next() : MatchResult?#

The next match after this one, or null: looked for from where this one ends, or one character on after a match of nothing.

override fun toString() : string#

MatchResult(value=2026-11-06, range=4..13).

data MatchGroup(val value : string, val range : Range)#

A group of a match: what it matched and where.

class MatchGroupCollection#

The groups of a match, by number or by name.

val size : int#

How many groups there are, the whole match counted.

op [](index : int) : MatchGroup?#

Group index (0 for the whole match), or null where it took no part in the match.

op [](name : string) : MatchGroup?#

The group of that name, or null where it took no part; an IllegalArgumentException for a name the pattern does not have.

fun get(index : int) : MatchGroup?#

Group index, as [] gives it.

fun get(name : string) : MatchGroup?#

The group of that name, as [] gives it.

fun toList() : List<MatchGroup?>#

Every group, in order, null for one that took no part.

fun string.toRegex(options : (RegexOption | Set<RegexOption>)? = null) : Regex#

The text as a Regex: "\\d+".toRegex().

fun string.matches(regex : Regex) : bool#

Whether the whole text matches regex.

fun string.contains(regex : Regex) : bool#

Whether regex matches somewhere in the text.

fun string.replace(regex : Regex, replacement : string) : string#

The text with every match of regex replaced ($1 and the rest as in Regex.replace).

fun string.replace(regex : Regex, transform : (MatchResult) -> string) : string#

The text with every match of regex replaced by what transform makes of it.

fun string.replaceFirst(regex : Regex, replacement : string) : string#

The text with the first match of regex replaced.

fun string.split(regex : Regex, limit : int = 0) : List<string>#

The pieces of the text between the matches of regex, as Regex.split.

sandbox.ww

The sandbox

A world of files of its own, kept in memory, which the code in it reaches in place of the system's, so that nothing outside it is touched; and faults, which make the rare failures happen on purpose (the M6.6.2 plan, in docs/plans-and-gaps.html):

Sandbox.run {
  FilePath("/data").mkdir()
  FilePath("/data/a.txt").writeText("1")
  faults.add(Fault.list("/data", after = 0))
  FilePath("/data").list()            // an IOException: the device is not ready
}

What the code in it starts, threads, tasks, workers and the cores of a parallel section, is in it too. Its clock moves only when the program waits, a sleep or a timer's delay taking no real time, and its randomness starts from a fixed seed, so that a test runs the same every time. A program started in it is one the sandbox was given (processes.add), run on a thread of its own; a connection is to one of its hosts (network.host), and a ServerSocket listens on a port of its own (network.connect). Its installed fonts are the system's, which nothing can change, or those of a folder it was given (fontsFrom), so that text measures the same on every machine; it has a clipboard of its own. A wait for another thread with a time limit still takes real time.

enum PathStyle#

How a sandbox's paths are written, whatever the machine: Linux's, /data/a.txt, or Windows', C:\data\a.txt, names compared without case.

class Sandbox#

A sandbox: its files, the failures it makes happen, and what has changed in it.

val paths : PathStyle#

How its paths are written.

val faults : Faults#

The failures it makes happen.

val files : SandboxFiles#

Its files, as they have changed.

val processes : Programs#

The programs it runs in place of the system's.

val network : Network#

Its hosts and ports, in place of the network.

fun advance(time : Duration)#

Its clock moved on by time, as if that much had passed: the timers due by then run when the event loop next looks.

inline fun <T> run(paths : PathStyle = PathStyle.linux, copyFrom : FilePath? = null, diskSize : int? = null, seed : int = 1, fontsFrom : FilePath? = null, block : Sandbox.() -> T) : T#

Runs block in a new sandbox, and gives what it gives; the block reaches nothing outside it. With copyFrom, the sandbox starts with a copy of that directory of the real system, at its root under the directory's name (a dry run: files.changes() says what the block did). diskSize is the most its files may hold together, in bytes; past it a write fails as on a full disk. A copyFrom that cannot be copied is an IllegalArgumentException. Its randomness starts from seed. With fontsFrom, a directory of the real system, its installed fonts are the font files there (.ttf, .otf, .ttc) and no others.

val current : Sandbox? get()#

The sandbox the code running now is in; null on the real system.

class Faults(private val handle : any)#

What fails in a sandbox: each fault added, from then on, each time what it names is done.

fun add(fault : Fault)#

Makes fault happen from now on, each time what it names is done.

data Fault(val operation : string, val path : string, val after : int, val error : string)#

A failure a sandbox makes happen: whenever operation is done to path ("*" for any), with error as what the system would say. A read, a write and a list go after bytes or names first and fail then; the others fail at once.

fun list(path : FilePath | string, after : int = 0, error : string = "the device is not ready") : Fault#

Listing the directory fails after after names.

fun read(path : FilePath | string, after : int = 0, error : string = "the device is not ready") : Fault#

Reading the file fails after after bytes: a whole read at once, a stream's when it gets there.

fun write(path : FilePath | string, after : int = 0, error : string = "the device is not ready") : Fault#

Writing the file fails after after bytes, those written.

fun open(path : FilePath | string, error : string = "access is denied") : Fault#

Opening the file, to read or to write, fails.

fun delete(path : FilePath | string, error : string = "access is denied") : Fault#

Deleting it fails.

fun move(path : FilePath | string, error : string = "access is denied") : Fault#

Moving it fails.

fun copy(path : FilePath | string, error : string = "access is denied") : Fault#

Copying it fails.

fun mkdir(path : FilePath | string, error : string = "access is denied") : Fault#

Making the directory fails.

fun any(path : FilePath | string, error : string = "the device is not ready") : Fault#

Everything done to it fails.

fun connect(host : string, error : string = "the connection timed out") : Fault#

Connecting to the host fails.

class Network(private val handle : any)#

A sandbox's network: the hosts its code may connect to, each a far side written in WinterWolf, and the ports its ServerSockets listen on.

fun host(name : string, port : int, peer : (Peer) -> unit throws Exception)#

Connecting to name:port in the sandbox runs peer on a thread of its own, as the far side of the connection: what it reads is what the code sends, and what it writes the code reads.

fun connect(port : int) : Socket throws IOException#

Connects to a port a ServerSocket in the sandbox listens on, as another computer would.

class Peer#

The far side of a connection in a sandbox, as the host's lambda sees it.

val socket : Socket#

The connection, from the far side.

val reader : Reader#

What the code sent.

val writer : Writer#

What goes to the code.

fun send(text : string) throws IOException#

Sends text to the code.

fun reset()#

Resets the connection, as a far side that went away: once the code has read what was sent before, what it does with the connection next fails with "the connection was reset".

fun waitForever() : nothing#

Waits for ever, as a far side that stopped answering, until the connection is closed: a read of the code's with a readTimeout is up at once, by the sandbox's clock.

class Programs#

The programs a sandbox runs in place of the system's, by name.

fun add(name : string, program : (SandboxProcess) -> unit throws Exception)#

Starting name in the sandbox, with Process or Process.run, runs program on a thread of its own: given its arguments, environment and streams, ending with process.exitCode, or 1 where it throws, its error written to its errors.

class SandboxProcess#

A program of a sandbox's, as its lambda sees it.

val arguments : List<string>#

Its arguments, after its name.

val environment : Map<string, string?>#

The variables it was given (in a sandbox, only those).

val input : Reader#

What it reads.

val output : Writer#

What it writes.

val error : Writer#

What it writes as errors.

var exitCode#

What it ends with: 0 unless set.

fun waitForever() : nothing#

Waits for ever, as a program that hangs, until it is killed: a time limit on it, Process.run's timeout or waitFor's, is up at once by the sandbox's clock.

class SandboxFiles(private val handle : any)#

A sandbox's files, as they have changed.

fun changes() : List<FileChange>#

What has been made, changed and deleted since the sandbox was made (with what was copied in), by path.

enum ChangeKind#

What became of a file or directory in a sandbox.

data FileChange(val kind : ChangeKind, val path : FilePath)#

A file or directory made, changed or deleted in a sandbox.

script.ww

Scripts

class Script#

A script is WinterWolf source compiled while the program runs, from text or a file, and run in a VM of its own, on a thread of its own: it shares nothing with the program, as a worker does not. The program calls its functions by name; what goes in and what comes back is copied as a worker's messages are: numbers, text, booleans, null, bytes, and lists, maps and sets of those (frozen data shared, not copied).

val script = Script.compile("fun double(n : int) : int => n * 2", name = "double.ww")
val running = script.start()
print(running.call("double", [21]))   // 42
running.stop()

A script that does not compile is a ScriptError listing what is wrong and where; one that throws is a ScriptException in the program, with what it threw. A script compiled once can be started any number of times, each run with its own VM. A program built into an executable that uses Script keeps the compiler (and the standard library's source) in its file.

The program and its scripts talk through interfaces (the scripting plan, part 2), declared in a file of their own, the contract, which both compile: it imports nothing and holds only declarations. The script is given an object of one of them, its api, and may give the program one of another, made of its own top-level functions:

// door_contract.ww
interface DoorApi {
  fun open(door : int)
  fun isLocked(door : int) : bool
  fun onTimer(seconds : int, then : () -> unit)
}
interface DoorScript { fun onEnter(player : int) }

// the program, which imports door_contract.ww
val script = Script.compile(source, name = "door.ww", api = DoorApi#, gives = DoorScript#)
val running = script.start(api = game.doors)          // game.doors is a DoorApi
val door = running.gives as DoorScript
door.onEnter(7)                                        // runs the script's onEnter

// door.ww, the script: `api` is a DoorApi; it gives DoorScript with this onEnter
fun onEnter(player : int) {
  if (!api.isLocked(3)) { api.open(3) }
  api.onTimer(2) { api.open(4) }
}

A member with a body in the contract is a hook a script may leave out (fun onBoard(player : int, ship : int) {}): its own where it declares one, the contract's body where not, so each of a game's mods declares only the hooks it wants. One with no body, it has to declare.

A script that calls a member the contract does not have, or gives the interface with a function of another type, does not compile. Data (numbers, text, lists, maps, sets, the contract's data classes and value types) is copied; an object of an interface of the contract goes as a handle, an object of that interface on the other side whose calls run on the object itself; a function given goes so too, so a script's lambda is called back in the script. A handle that comes back is the object itself again.

A script is safe unless compiled trusted: it reaches nothing outside the program but through its api (each try a SecurityException), what it prints goes to a function of the program's or nowhere, and exit ends it alone.

What a script may take is given as it starts (Limits): each call's time, or a count of its steps for a game whose machines must agree; its heap; how deep its calls go. A call past one is a ScriptLimitException in the program, and the script goes on for the next:

val running = script.start(api = game, limits = Limits(time = 2.milliseconds, memory = 32 * 1024 * 1024))
val name : string#

The name it was compiled under, which its errors and stack traces give.

val api : Class?#

The interface its api is an object of, where it was compiled with api = ...

val gives : Class?#

The interface it gives, where it was compiled with gives = ...

fun compile(source : string, name : string = "script.ww", folder : FilePath? = null, api : Class? = null, gives : Class? = null, trusted : bool = false) : Script throws ScriptError#

source compiled, as a file named name in folder (by default where the program runs), which its imports are looked for from; a ScriptError listing each problem, with its line and column, where it does not compile. With api, an interface of a contract written here by its name (api = DoorApi#), the script has api, an object of it, and sees the contract's declarations; with gives, another of them, its top-level functions are made an object of that interface, which the program gets from RunningScript.gives. A script is safe unless trusted: it reaches nothing outside the program (files, the network, processes, native libraries, threads and workers, the system's fonts, standard input, the program's arguments and environment), each a SecurityException, only its api; and it imports only files under folder (none where no folder is given) and the libraries ww carries. A trusted one reaches what any program does.

fun load(path : FilePath, api : Class? = null, gives : Class? = null, trusted : bool = false) : Script throws IOException | ScriptError#

The file read and compiled, its imports looked for from its folder, as compile does; a ScriptError where it does not compile, an IOException where it cannot be read.

val names : List<string>#

What it declares at its top level, its functions among them, sorted.

fun start(api : any? = null, output : ((string) -> unit)? = null, limits : Limits? = null) : RunningScript#

Starts it: a VM of its own, on a thread of its own, runs its top level, and waits for calls. api is what the script's api stands for: an object of the interface it was compiled with, whose calls from the script run here. A ScriptException where the top level throws. output gets each line it prints (on the program's event loop); with none, a safe one's go nowhere and a trusted one's to the program's own output. limits are what it may take, its top level a call as any (one compiled in a ScriptVM has the ScriptVM's).

override fun toString() : string#

As "Script(door.ww)".

class RunningScript#

A script started (Script.start), until it is stopped.

val script : Script#

The script it runs.

fun call(function : string, arguments : List<any?> = []) : any?#

Calls the function the script declares under function, with arguments copied over, and gives what it returns, copied back. The program waits until it answers. A ScriptException where the script throws (with what it threw, and where), has no such function, or has ended; a ScriptLimitException where it ran past its limits.

fun stop()#

Stops the script, whatever it is doing (at its next call or loop round), and waits until it has ended. A call after that is a ScriptException.

val gives : any?#

The object of the interface the script gives (compiled with gives = DoorScript#), made of its own top-level functions: its calls run in the script. A ScriptException where it gives none.

val isRunning : bool#

True until it ends: stopped, or ended by itself (its top level called exit).

override fun toString() : string#

As "RunningScript(door.ww)".

class ScriptVM#

A VM that several scripts share (asked 2026-11-19: "so we can choose which vm a script runs in"): one copy of the standard library and of what they import, for all of them. Each is compiled into it as it runs, by a compiler of its own that lives on its thread (as the shell's does), so a script after the first compiles in a few milliseconds. Its scripts run one at a time, on its one thread, each with names of its own; but they share its fate: one that loops for ever, or calls exit, stops them all. So a game puts its own mods in one, and a script it does not trust in a VM of its own (Script.compile(...).start()).

val shared = ScriptVM()
val core = shared.compile(coreSource, name = "core.ww", api = ModApi#, gives = ModHooks#).start(api = game)
val earthlings = shared.compile(earthSource, name = "earthlings.ww", api = ModApi#, gives = ModHooks#).start(api = game)
fun compile(source : string, name : string = "script.ww", folder : FilePath? = null, api : Class? = null, gives : Class? = null) : Script throws ScriptError#

source compiled into this VM, as Script.compile compiles one of its own; it starts here, once (compile it again for another run).

fun load(path : FilePath, api : Class? = null, gives : Class? = null) : Script throws IOException | ScriptError#

The file read and compiled into this VM, as Script.load does.

fun stop()#

Stops it, and every script in it, whatever it is doing, and waits until it has ended.

val isRunning : bool#

True until it is stopped, or ends by itself (a script of it called exit).

override fun toString() : string#

As "ScriptVM".

value ScriptProblem(val file : string, val line : int, val column : int, val message : string)#

One thing wrong with a script that does not compile, and where.

override fun toString() : string#

As the compiler writes it: "door.ww:3:5: error: ...".

class ScriptError(val problems : List<ScriptProblem>) : Exception(problems.map { it.toString() }.join("\n"))#

A script that does not compile: every problem found, each with its place.

class ScriptException(message : string? = null) : RuntimeException(message)#

A script that threw, has no function of the name called, or is no longer running.

class ScriptLimitException(message : string? = null) : ScriptException(message)#

A call of a script's that ran past its limits (Limits): its time, its steps or its heap. The script goes on, for the next call.

value Limits(val time : Duration? = null, val steps : int? = null, val memory : int? = null, val depth : int? = null)#

What a script may take (the scripting plan, part 4), each null for no limit:

time    each call from the program, the script's own time in it (not the program's, while
        the script waits on its api): past it, the call ends
steps   each call from the program, a count of its steps (each time round a loop, each call),
        the same on every machine, for a game whose machines must agree (replays,
        multiplayer): a script counted runs with no native code, more slowly
memory  the script's heap, in bytes: a collection that finds more live ends the call, and
        one request for more than all of it (Bytes(n), a list's room) is refused at once,
        nothing taken
depth   how deep its calls go: deeper is a StackOverflowError in the script

The top level, as the script starts, is a call as any. A call that ends by a limit ends at once, no catch or finally of the script's run, and is a ScriptLimitException in the program; the script goes on for the next, what it was in the middle of left as it was. A program whose scripts keep passing their limits stops them (a game: a mod disabled after a few).

What the compiler and the contract's proxies call

Code compiled into the program itself

object Compiler#

Source compiled while the program runs into the program itself (asked 2026-11-21, for an emulator that translates the code it runs into WinterWolf): in the program's own VM, on the calling thread, trusted, with nothing copied. Unlike a Script, it is the program's own code: its imports are the program's files and their classes, the same ones, and the names of the program's main file are its own, so what it makes is an object like any (a class of the program's extended, an interface of the program's implemented, a function) and runs as fast as the program's own code.

// shape.ww, imported by the program
interface Shape { fun area() : float }

val made = Compiler.compile("import \"shape.ww\".*\nobject : Shape { override fun area() : float => 2.5 }") as Shape
print(made.area())   // 2.5

Each compile is one more file in folder (by default the folder of the program's own file), which its imports are looked for from: the names of that folder's files are its own, as for any file there, and what it declares at its top level joins them (a name the program already declares cannot be declared again). The compile needs the compiler's view of the program. Run from its source (ww program.ww), the program is compiled from it each time it starts (the units ww kept are put back, so that is quick), never read back whole from what ww kept, and that view is kept. Built (ww build, to a bytecode file or an executable), it carries the sources it was compiled from and keeps all of its code; its first compile compiles it again from those, not from the disk (once, taking about as long as compiling it did), so it works where they are not.

fun compile(source : string, name : string = "code.ww", folder : FilePath? = null) : any?#

source compiled into the program, as the file name in folder, and run: what its last expression gives (null where it ends with none). An IllegalArgumentException with the compiler's errors, each with its line and column, where it does not compile; an IllegalStateException in a program built without its sources (by a ww before 0.9.5050).

securerandom.ww

Secure random numbers (networking plan, part 1, 2026-11-20)

object SecureRandom#

Random numbers no one can guess or repeat, for keys, tokens, passwords and nonces: from the system's own generator (BCryptGenRandom on Windows, getrandom on Linux), which is seeded from the machine's sources of chance. Math.random and Math.randomInt are quicker and can be seeded to repeat, for games and tests; these can not, by design.

val key = SecureRandom.bytes(32)
val token = Base64.encode(SecureRandom.bytes(18))
val roll = SecureRandom.randomInt(1, 6)

Safe from every thread and worker at once; there is nothing to make or seed.

fun random() : float#

A float from 0.0 up to 1.0, not including 1.0.

fun randomInt(low : int, high : int) : int#

An int from low to high, both included, every one as likely; an IllegalArgumentException where low is above high.

fun bytes(size : int) : Bytes#

size new random bytes; an IllegalArgumentException for a negative size.

fun fill(into : Bytes, offset : int = 0, size : int = into.size - offset) : Bytes#

Makes size of the bytes from offset random, in place, and gives the bytes.

sequence.ww

Sequences

A sequence gives its items one at a time, as they are asked for: nothing is worked out ahead, and nothing is kept, so a sequence can be endless, or longer than would fit in memory.

val squares = generateSequence(1) { it + 1 }.map { it * it }
print(squares.filter { it % 3 == 0 }.take(5).toList())     // [9, 36, 81, 144, 225]

val fibonacci = sequence {
  var (a, b) = 0 to 1
  while (true) {
    yield(a)
    (a, b) = b to a + b
  }
}

map, filter, take and the others make a new sequence, which does its work as its items are asked for; toList, first, count, fold and the others ask for them. Each time a sequence is gone over, it starts again from the beginning.

class Sequence<T>#

Items given one at a time, as they are asked for, by an iterator made afresh each time the sequence is gone over. sequence { }, generateSequence, sequenceOf and asSequence() make one.

fun iterator() : Iterator<T>#

A new iterator over the items, from the beginning; for item in sequence uses it.

fun <R> map(transform : (T) -> R) : Sequence<R>#

What transform gives for each item, worked out as the items are asked for.

fun <R> mapIndexed(transform : (int, T) -> R) : Sequence<R>#

What transform gives for each item and its position (from 0), worked out as asked for.

fun <R> mapNotNull(transform : (T) -> R?) : Sequence<R>#

What transform gives for each item, leaving out the nulls, worked out as asked for.

fun filter(predicate : (T) -> bool) : Sequence<T>#

The items for which predicate is true, in their order, found as they are asked for.

fun filterNot(predicate : (T) -> bool) : Sequence<T>#

The items for which predicate is false, in their order, found as they are asked for.

fun <R> flatMap(transform : (T) -> Sequence<R>) : Sequence<R>#

Each item's items, one sequence after another.

fun take(count : int) : Sequence<T>#

The first count items, or all of them if there are fewer.

fun takeWhile(predicate : (T) -> bool) : Sequence<T>#

The items from the start for as long as predicate is true of them; the sequence ends at the first item for which it is false.

fun drop(count : int) : Sequence<T>#

All but the first count items.

fun dropWhile(predicate : (T) -> bool) : Sequence<T>#

The items from the first one for which predicate is false, that one included.

fun onEach(action : (T) -> unit) : Sequence<T>#

The same items, the action run on each as it goes by.

fun distinct() : Sequence<T>#

Each item once, the first time it comes; those seen are kept to tell.

fun distinctBy(selector : (T) -> any?) : Sequence<T>#

Each item whose selector key has not come before, the first time it comes; the keys seen are kept to tell.

op +(other : Sequence<T>) : Sequence<T>#

This sequence's items, then the other's.

fun toList() : open List<T>#

All the items, asked for now, as a new list. An endless sequence never gets to the end.

fun toSet() : open Set<T>#

All the items, each once, asked for now, as a new set.

fun forEach(action : (T) -> unit)#

Runs action on each item, in order.

fun count() : int#

The number of items; it asks for all of them.

fun count(predicate : (T) -> bool) : int#

The number of items for which predicate is true; it asks for all of them.

fun any() : bool#

Whether the sequence has at least one item; it asks for the first only.

fun any(predicate : (T) -> bool) : bool#

Whether predicate is true of at least one item; it stops at the first. False when empty.

fun all(predicate : (T) -> bool) : bool#

Whether predicate is true of every item; it stops at the first that fails. True when empty.

fun none() : bool#

Whether the sequence has no items; it asks for the first only.

fun none(predicate : (T) -> bool) : bool#

Whether predicate is true of no item; it stops at the first that it is true of.

fun first() : T#

The first item; a NoSuchElementException when the sequence is empty.

fun first(predicate : (T) -> bool) : T#

The first item for which predicate is true; a NoSuchElementException when there is none.

fun firstOrNull() : T?#

The first item, or null when the sequence is empty.

fun firstOrNull(predicate : (T) -> bool) : T?#

The first item for which predicate is true, or null when there is none.

fun find(predicate : (T) -> bool) : T?#

The first item for which predicate is true, or null when there is none; the same as firstOrNull(predicate).

fun last() : T#

The last item, which asks for all of them; a NoSuchElementException when the sequence is empty.

fun elementAt(index : int) : T#

The item at index, counted from 0, asking for the items up to it; an IndexOutOfBoundsException when there is none there or index is negative.

fun <R> fold(initial : R, operation : (R, T) -> R) : R#

initial put through operation with each item in turn, from the first: the result of each step and the item give the next.

fun reduce(operation : (T, T) -> T) : T#

The first item put through operation with each of the others in turn; an UnsupportedOperationException when the sequence is empty (fold takes a starting value).

fun <R : Number> sumOf(selector : (T) -> R) : R#

The sum of what selector gives for each item: an int if all are ints, a float if any is a float; for an empty sequence a zero of the kind selector gives, 0 or 0.0.

fun join(separator : string = ", ", prefix : string = "", postfix : string = "", limit : int = -1, truncated : string = "...", transform : ((T) -> any?)? = null) : string#

The items as text, as a list's join gives them; with a limit, only so many are asked for.

override fun toString() : string#

"Sequence": it asks for none of the items. join() gives them as text.

fun <T : Number> Sequence<T>.sum() : T#

The sum of the items, which asks for all of them: ints sum to an int, and with a float among them to a float; for an empty sequence a zero of its item type, 0 or 0.0.

Making a sequence

class SequenceScope<T>#

What a sequence { } block gives its values with.

suspend fun yield(value : T)#

Gives a value. The block waits here until the next one is asked for, and goes no further if none is.

suspend fun yieldAll(items : Sequence<T>)#

Gives each item of items in turn, as yield does.

suspend fun yieldAll(items : List<T>)#

Gives each item of items in turn, as yield does.

suspend fun yieldAll(items : Set<T>)#

Gives each item of a set in turn, in its order, as yield does.

suspend fun yieldAll(items : Iterable<T>)#

Gives each item of what an Iterable goes through in turn, as yield does.

suspend fun yieldAll(items : Iterator<T>)#

Gives each item an Iterator has left in turn, as yield does.

fun <T> sequence(block : suspend SequenceScope<T>.() -> unit) : Sequence<T>#

A sequence of what the block gives with yield. The block runs as its values are asked for, up to the yield of the next one each time, on a stack of its own that starts small; it waits nowhere else.

fun <T> generateSequence(seed : T?, next : (T) -> T?) : Sequence<T>#

seed, then what next makes of each item, until it gives null.

fun <T> generateSequence(next : () -> T?) : Sequence<T>#

What next gives, each time, until it gives null.

fun <T> sequenceOf(vararg items : T) : Sequence<T>#

A sequence of the items given, in their order.

fun <T> emptySequence() : Sequence<T>#

A sequence with no items.

fun <T> List<T>.asSequence() : Sequence<T>#

A list's items, read as they are asked for: a change to the list shows in what is still to come.

fun Range.asSequence() : Sequence<int>#

A range's numbers, worked out as they are asked for: (1..1_000_000_000).asSequence() keeps none.

fun <T> Set<T>.asSequence() : Sequence<T>#

The set's items, in its order, as a sequence. They are copied when this is called, so a later change to the set does not show.

fun string.asSequence() : Sequence<char>#

The characters, as a sequence. They are copied into a list when this is called.

fun <K, V> Map<K, V>.asSequence() : Sequence<MapEntry<K, V>>#

The map's entries, in its order, as a sequence. They are copied when this is called, so a later change to the map does not show.

fun <T> Sequence<T>.asSequence() : Sequence<T>#

The sequence itself.

How they are made

set.ww

Sets and decks

A set holds each value once, in the order the values were first added; two values are the same one if a map would take them as one key. It is built in (Set(), add, remove, contains, addAll, removeAll, retainAll, clear, toList, first, last, set + x and set - x); what it does besides is here, each reading its items in order, and those that make a collection making a list, as a list's do. A map's keys and entries are sets too, live views of it.

val seen = Set<string>()        setOf(1, 2, 3)        [1, 2, 2].toSet()
fun <T> Set<T>.containsAll(from : List<T>) : bool#

True if every item of from is in the set; true for an empty from.

fun <T> Set<T>.containsAll(from : Set<T>) : bool#
