True if every item of from is in the set; true for an empty from.

fun <T> open Set<T>.removeIf(predicate : (T) -> bool) : bool#

Takes out the items for which predicate is true; true if it took any out.

fun <T> Set<T>.toOpenList() : open List<T>#

The items in order as a new list, which can be changed; the same as toList().

fun <T> Set<T>.toSet() : open Set<T>#

A new set of the same items in the same order, which can be changed without changing this one.

fun <T> Set<T>.toOpenSet() : open Set<T>#

A new set of the same items in the same order, which can be changed without changing this one.

fun <T> Set<T>.union(other : any) : open Set<T>#

A new set of this set's items followed by those of other (a list, a set or another collection) that it does not have.

fun <T> Set<T>.intersect(other : any) : open Set<T>#

A new set of the items of other (a list, a set or another collection) that are also in this set, in the order they have in other.

setOf(3, 1, 2).intersect([2, 3])   // {2, 3}
fun <T> Set<T>.subtract(other : any) : open Set<T>#

A new set of this set's items that are not in other (a list, a set or another collection), in this set's order.

fun <T> Set<T>.plus(item : T) : open Set<T>#

A new set of these items and item at the end, unless it is one of them already; the set itself is left as it is. The same as set + item.

fun <T> Set<T>.plus(other : List<T>) : open Set<T>#

A new set of these items followed by those of other that are not among them; as union.

fun <T> Set<T>.plus(other : Set<T>) : open Set<T>#

A new set of these items followed by those of other that are not among them; as union.

fun <T> Set<T>.minus(item : T) : open Set<T>#

A new set of these items without item; the set itself is left as it is. The same as set - item.

fun <T> Set<T>.minus(other : List<T>) : open Set<T>#

A new set of these items without those in other; as subtract.

fun <T> Set<T>.minus(other : Set<T>) : open Set<T>#

A new set of these items without those in other; as subtract.

fun <T> Set<T>.firstOrNull() : T?#

The first item, or null when the set is empty.

inline fun <T> Set<T>.firstOrNull(predicate : (T) -> bool) : T?#

The first item for which predicate is true, or null when there is none.

The last item, or null when the set is empty.

inline fun <T> Set<T>.lastOrNull(predicate : (T) -> bool) : T?#

The last item for which predicate is true, or null when there is none.

inline fun <T> Set<T>.find(predicate : (T) -> bool) : T?#

The first item for which predicate is true, or null when there is none; as firstOrNull.

fun <T> Set<T>.single() : T#

The only item: a NoSuchElementException when the set is empty, an IllegalArgumentException when it has more than one, as in Kotlin.

inline fun <T> Set<T>.single(predicate : (T) -> bool) : T#

The only item for which predicate is true: a NoSuchElementException when there is none, an IllegalArgumentException when there are more. predicate is called for every item.

The only item, or null when the set is empty or has more than one.

inline fun <T> Set<T>.singleOrNull(predicate : (T) -> bool) : T?#

The only item for which predicate is true, or null when there is none or more than one.

inline fun <T> Set<T>.forEach(action : (T) -> unit)#

Calls action with each item, in order.

inline fun <T, R> Set<T>.map(transform : (T) -> R) : open List<R>#

What transform gives for each item, in order, as a new list.

inline fun <T, R> Set<T>.mapNotNull(transform : (T) -> R?) : open List<R>#

What transform gives for each item where that is not null, in order, as a new list.

inline fun <T, R> Set<T>.flatMap(transform : (T) -> List<R>) : open List<R>#

The items of the lists transform gives for each item, one list after another, as a new list.

inline fun <T> Set<T>.filter(predicate : (T) -> bool) : open List<T>#

The items for which predicate is true, in their order, as a new list.

inline fun <T> Set<T>.filterNot(predicate : (T) -> bool) : open List<T>#

The items for which predicate is false, in their order, as a new list.

fun <T> Set<T>.any() : bool#

True if the set has any items.

inline fun <T> Set<T>.any(predicate : (T) -> bool) : bool#

True if predicate is true for any item; it stops at the first for which it is.

inline fun <T> Set<T>.all(predicate : (T) -> bool) : bool#

True if predicate is true for every item, stopping at the first for which it is not; true for an empty set.

fun <T> Set<T>.none() : bool#

True if the set is empty.

inline fun <T> Set<T>.none(predicate : (T) -> bool) : bool#

True if predicate is true for no item; true for an empty set.

The number of items, as size.

inline fun <T> Set<T>.count(predicate : (T) -> bool) : int#

The number of items for which predicate is true.

inline fun <T, R> Set<T>.fold(initial : R, operation : (R, T) -> R) : R#

initial combined with each item in turn by operation(soFar, item); initial for an empty set.

setOf(1, 2, 3).fold(10) { acc, x -> acc - x }   // 4
fun <T> Set<T>.sum() : any?#

The sum of the items, which have to be numbers (a ClassCastException where they are not); 0 for an empty set.

inline fun <T, R : Number> Set<T>.sumOf(selector : (T) -> R) : R#

The sum of what selector gives for each item; 0 for an empty set.

The largest item; a NoSuchElementException when the set is empty.

The smallest item; a NoSuchElementException when the set is empty.

The largest item, or null when the set is empty.

The smallest item, or null when the set is empty.

The items in ascending order, as a new list.

The items in descending order, as a new list.

The items in the ascending order of what selector gives for them, as a new list; those for which it gives the same keep their order.

fun <T> Set<T>.sortedByDescending(selector : (T) -> any?) : open List<T>#

The items in the descending order of what selector gives for them, as a new list; those for which it gives the same keep their order.

fun <T> Set<T>.sortedWith(comparator : (T, T) -> int) : open List<T>#

The items in the order comparator sets, as a new list: it gives a negative number, 0 or a positive number as its first argument goes before, with or after its second.

inline fun <T, V> Set<T>.associateWith(valueSelector : (T) -> V) : open Map<T, V>#

A new map from each item to what valueSelector gives for it.

A new map to each item from what keySelector gives for it; where two items give the same key, the later one is kept.

inline fun <T, K> Set<T>.groupBy(keySelector : (T) -> K) : open Map<K, open List<T>>#

A new map from each key keySelector gives to the list of the items that give it, in their order.

inline fun <T> Set<T>.partition(predicate : (T) -> bool) : Pair<List<T>, List<T>>#

A pair of new lists: the items for which predicate is true, and those for which it is false.

fun <T> Set<T>.join(separator : string = ", ", prefix : string = "", postfix : string = "", limit : int = -1, truncated : string = "...", transform : ((T) -> any?)? = null) : string#

The items as text, each as transform makes it when it is given, with separator between them and prefix and postfix around; with a limit of 0 or more, only that many items, then truncated where there were more.

setOf(1, 2, 3).join(limit = 2)   // "1, 2, ..."
class Deck<T>#

A queue open at both ends: adding and removing are quick at the front as well as the back, where a list is quick only at the back.

val queue = Deck<Job>()        queue.addLast(job)        val next = queue.removeFirst()
val size : int#

The number of items.

val lastIndex : int#

The index of the back item, size - 1; -1 when the deck is empty.

val indices : Range#

The range of the indexes, 0 ..< size.

fun isEmpty() : bool#

True if the deck has no items.

fun isNotEmpty() : bool#

True if the deck has any items.

fun addLast(item : T)#

Adds item at the back.

fun add(item : T) : bool#

Adds item at the back, as addLast; always true.

fun addAll(from : any) : bool#

Adds the items of from (a list, a set or another collection) at the back, in their order; true if there were any.

fun addFirst(item : T)#

Adds item at the front, so that it is deck[0].

fun removeFirst() : T#

Takes out the front item and gives it; a NoSuchElementException when the deck is empty.

fun removeLast() : T#

Takes out the back item and gives it; a NoSuchElementException when the deck is empty.

fun removeFirstOrNull() : T?#

Takes out the front item and gives it, or gives null when the deck is empty.

fun removeLastOrNull() : T?#

Takes out the back item and gives it, or gives null when the deck is empty.

fun first() : T#

The front item, left in; a NoSuchElementException when the deck is empty.

fun last() : T#

The back item, left in; a NoSuchElementException when the deck is empty.

fun firstOrNull() : T?#

The front item, or null when the deck is empty.

fun lastOrNull() : T?#

The back item, or null when the deck is empty.

fun get(index : int) : T#

deck[0] is the front.

fun getOrNull(index : int) : T?#

The item at index from the front, or null when there is none there.

fun set(index : int, item : T)#

Puts item at index from the front, in place of the one there; an IndexOutOfBoundsException when there is none there.

op [](index : int) : T#

deck[index]: the item at index from the front, as get.

op []=(index : int, item : T)#

deck[index] = item: puts item at index from the front, as set.

fun indexOf(item : T) : int#

The index from the front of the first item equal to item, or -1 when there is none.

fun contains(item : T) : bool#

True if an item is equal to item.

fun remove(item : T) : bool#

Takes out the first item equal to this one. True if there was one.

fun removeAt(index : int) : T#

Takes out the item at index from the front and gives it; an IndexOutOfBoundsException when there is none there.

fun clear()#

Takes out all the items.

fun iterator() : List<T>#

A for loop goes through the items as they were when it began, front to back.

fun toList() : open List<T>#

A list of T made of them: the items are kept as T? (what is before head is null), and a List<T?> is no List<T>.

fun toOpenList() : open List<T>#

The items front to back as a new list, which can be changed; the same as toList().

fun forEach(action : (T) -> unit)#

Calls action with each item, front to back, the items being those there when it began.

fun <R> map(transform : (T) -> R) : open List<R>#

What transform gives for each item, front to back, as a new list.

fun filter(predicate : (T) -> bool) : open List<T>#

The items for which predicate is true, front to back, as a new list.

fun any() : bool#

True if the deck has any items.

fun any(predicate : (T) -> bool) : bool#

True if predicate is true for any item.

fun all(predicate : (T) -> bool) : bool#

True if predicate is true for every item; true for an empty deck.

fun none() : bool#

True if the deck is empty.

fun none(predicate : (T) -> bool) : bool#

True if predicate is true for no item; true for an empty deck.

fun count() : int#

The number of items, as size.

fun count(predicate : (T) -> bool) : int#

The number of items for which predicate is true.

fun find(predicate : (T) -> bool) : T?#

The first item from the front for which predicate is true, or null when there is none.

fun reversed() : open List<T>#

The items back to front, as a new list.

fun join(separator : string = ", ", prefix : string = "", postfix : string = "", limit : int = -1, truncated : string = "...", transform : ((T) -> any?)? = null) : string#

The items front to back as text, as a list's join makes it: separator between them, prefix and postfix around, at most limit of them when that is 0 or more, then truncated where there were more.

op ==(other : any?) : bool#

True if other is a deck with equal items in the same order; a list is never equal to a deck.

fun hashCode() : int#

A hash of the items in order, the same as a list of them gives, so equal decks give equal ones.

fun toString() : string#

As a list shows itself: [1, 2, 3]

string.ww

Strings

The methods a string has besides the built-in ones. Its characters are chars, as s[i] gives them, and so is what each of these functions passes on or gives for one.

Parts of the text

fun string.take(count : int) : string#

The first count characters, or the whole string when it has fewer. A negative count is an IllegalArgumentException.

fun string.takeLast(count : int) : string#

The last count characters, or the whole string when it has fewer. A negative count is an IllegalArgumentException.

fun string.drop(count : int) : string#

All but the first count characters: "" when the string has no more than count. A negative count is an IllegalArgumentException.

fun string.dropLast(count : int) : string#

All but the last count characters: "" when the string has no more than count. A negative count is an IllegalArgumentException.

inline fun string.takeWhile(predicate : (char) -> bool) : string#

The characters from the start for as long as predicate is true of them.

"123abc".takeWhile { it.isDigit() }   // "123"
inline fun string.dropWhile(predicate : (char) -> bool) : string#

The string from the first character for which predicate is false; "" when it is true of all.

fun string.substringBefore(delimiter : string, missingDelimiterValue : string? = null) : string#

What comes before or after a delimiter. If the delimiter is not there, the whole string, or missingDelimiterValue when one is given.

fun string.substringAfter(delimiter : string, missingDelimiterValue : string? = null) : string#

What comes after the first delimiter; the whole string, or missingDelimiterValue when one is given, if delimiter is not there.

fun string.substringBeforeLast(delimiter : string, missingDelimiterValue : string? = null) : string#

What comes before the last delimiter; the whole string, or missingDelimiterValue when one is given, if delimiter is not there.

fun string.substringAfterLast(delimiter : string, missingDelimiterValue : string? = null) : string#

What comes after the last delimiter; the whole string, or missingDelimiterValue when one is given, if delimiter is not there.

"a/b/c.txt".substringAfterLast("/")   // "c.txt"
fun string.removePrefix(prefix : string) : string#

The string without prefix at its start; the string as it is when it does not start with it.

fun string.removeSuffix(suffix : string) : string#

The string without suffix at its end; the string as it is when it does not end with it.

fun string.removeSurrounding(prefix : string, suffix : string = prefix) : string#

The string without prefix at its start and suffix at its end, when it has both and is long enough for them not to overlap; otherwise the string as it is. suffix is prefix if not given.

"\"quoted\"".removeSurrounding("\"")   // "quoted"
fun string.chunked(size : int) : open List<string>#

The text in pieces of size characters; the last may be shorter.

fun string.replaceFirst(oldValue : string, newValue : string) : string#

The string with the first oldValue in it put back as newValue; the string as it is when oldValue is not there. An empty oldValue puts newValue at the start.

inline fun string.replaceFirstChar(transform : (char) -> any?) : string#

The text with its first character put through a function: name.replaceFirstChar { it.upper() }

fun string.commonPrefixWith(other : string) : string#

The longest start that this string and other have in common; "" when none.

Characters

fun string.first() : char#

The first character; a NoSuchElementException when the string is empty.

The character at index, counted from 0; an IndexOutOfBoundsException when there is none. Unlike s[i], it takes no position from the end (^1).

fun string.getOrNull(index : int) : char?#

The character at index, counted from 0, or null when there is none.

The position of the first character for which predicate is true, or -1 when there is none.

inline fun string.indexOfLast(predicate : (char) -> bool) : int#

inline fun string.forEach(action : (char) -> unit)#

Whether the string has any characters at all.

The characters, as a new list; the same as toList().

The characters, each once, as a new set.

Empty and missing text

Whether the string is null, empty, or only spaces, tabs and line breaks.

The string, or "" when it is null.

inline fun string.ifEmpty(defaultValue : () -> string) : string#

The string, or what defaultValue gives when the string is empty. defaultValue runs only then.

The string, or what defaultValue gives when the string is blank (empty, or only spaces, tabs and line breaks). defaultValue runs only then.

To other types

toInt, toIntOrNull, toFloat and toFloatOrNull are built in. WinterWolf has one type for whole numbers, 64 bits, and one for fractions, so Kotlin's toLong and toDouble are written so.

fun string.toBoolean() : bool#

Whether the string is "true", in any mix of capitals and small letters; false for anything else.

fun string.encodeToByteArray(charset : Charset = Charset.utf8) : Bytes#

The string's bytes in charset, UTF-8 if none is given; the same as toBytes(charset).

fun string.plus(other : any?) : string#

The string followed by other as print would show it: "a".plus(null) is "anull".

stroke.ww

Strokes (N3)

enum LineCap#

How the open ends of a stroked line look, as Java's BasicStroke: cut off square at the end (butt), a half circle round it (round), or a half square past it (square).

enum LineJoin#

How a stroked line turns a corner, as Java's BasicStroke: out to a point (miter), round it (round), or cut across it (bevel).

value Stroke(val width : Number = 1, val cap : LineCap = LineCap.square, val join : LineJoin = LineJoin.miter, val miterLimit : Number = 10, val dashes : List<Number>? = null, val dashPhase : Number = 0)#

How lines are drawn, as Java's BasicStroke: width units across, centred on the line; its ends by cap and its corners by join; a miter that would reach out more than miterLimit times the width is cut across as a bevel. With dashes, the line is drawn in pieces: a piece that long, a gap that long, and so on round the list again, starting dashPhase units into it, afresh on each contour (a list with an odd count is gone round twice, as SVG does).

canvas.stroke = Stroke(3, LineCap.round, LineJoin.round)
canvas.draw(path)
canvas.stroke = Stroke(1, dashes = [4, 2])

A canvas's lineWidth is its stroke's width. Java's defaults: 1 unit, square ends, miter joins, a miter limit of 10, no dashes.

fun outline(path : Path, pixels : Number = 4) : Path#

The shape the stroke makes of path: what is filled to draw its outline, by nonZero, as Java's createStrokedShape. Curves are cut into straight pieces fine enough for pixels pixels a unit, to within about a hundredth of a pixel, and so are round ends and corners. An IllegalArgumentException for a width or a miter limit below 0 or below 1, or dashes that are not all 0 or more and add up to more than 0. A width of 0 makes an empty path.

override fun toString() : string#

The stroke as text: "Stroke(2, round, miter, 10)", with its dashes if it has them.

task.ww

The event loop

object EventLoop#

One per program, on the main thread. It runs the jobs posted to it and the timers, one at a time, after the script itself has finished, and the program ends when there is nothing left for it to wait for. A window's events arrive through the same loop.

EventLoop.post { refresh() }                       // from any thread: run this on the main thread
val tick = EventLoop.every(1.seconds) { print("tick") }
EventLoop.after(5.5.seconds) { tick.cancel() }
fun post(job : () -> unit)#

Runs the job on the main thread, soon. Safe to call from any thread.

fun after(delay : Duration, job : () -> unit) : Timer#

Runs the job once, delay from now, or again and again period apart. Each gives a Timer, whose cancel() calls it off.

fun every(period : Duration, job : () -> unit) : Timer#

Runs the job every period, the first time one period from now, until the Timer is cancelled; a running timer keeps the program going.

val isLoopThread : bool#

Whether the code asking is running on the loop's thread.

fun run()#

Runs the loop here until there is nothing left for it to wait for. A program need not call this: the loop runs by itself when the script ends. It is for a script that has something to do after that.

class Timer(private val id : int)#

A job EventLoop.after or EventLoop.every has set to run later.

fun cancel()#

Calls the job off: it does not run again. Nothing happens when it has already run or been cancelled.

Tasks

A task is a function that can set itself aside in the middle of a call and be taken up again later, where it was, without holding a thread meanwhile (Kotlin's coroutine). Code that asks one thing and then another reads top to bottom:

suspend fun ask(question : string) : string => suspendTask { resume -> dialog.prompt(question) { answer -> resume(answer) } }

task {
  val name = ask("Name?")
  val colour = ask("Favourite colour, $name?")
  save(name, colour)
}

task { } gives a Job of what the block returns (Kotlin's async): then { } runs with it on the event loop, get() waits for it. A function that may wait says so with suspend, and the checker holds callers to it. Waiting in a task on the main thread sets the task aside and lets the event loop go on; anywhere else it blocks the thread.

class Result<T>#

How something turned out: a value, or the error that was thrown instead.

fun <T> success(value : T) : Result<T>#

A Result that holds value.

fun <T> failure(error : Throwable) : Result<T>#

A Result that holds error, as if it had been thrown.

val isSuccess : bool#

Whether it holds a value.

val isFailure : bool#

Whether it holds an error.

fun getOrNull() : T?#

The value, or null when it holds an error.

fun exceptionOrNull() : Throwable?#

The error, or null when it holds a value.

fun getOrDefault(defaultValue : T) : T#

The value, or defaultValue when it holds an error.

fun getOrElse(onFailure : (Throwable) -> T) : T#

The value, or what onFailure gives for the error when it holds one.

fun getOrThrow() : T#

The value; when it holds an error, that error is thrown.

fun onSuccess(action : (T) -> unit) : Result<T>#

Runs action with the value when it holds one. Gives the Result itself, so calls can be chained.

fun onFailure(action : (Throwable) -> unit) : Result<T>#

Runs action with the error when it holds one. Gives the Result itself, so calls can be chained.

override fun toString() : string#

"Success(value)" or "Failure(error)".

inline fun <T> runCatching(block : () -> T) : Result<T>#

Runs the block and gives its value, or the error it threw, as a Result.

fun <T> task(block : suspend () -> T throws Exception) : Job<T>#

Starts a task running the block, and gives a Job of what it returns, or of the Exception it throws. Returns once the task has finished or has first set itself aside. The job's cancel(interrupt = true) stops the task where it next waits, with a CancellationException.

Task scopes

The tasks and threads started in a scope are its own (Kotlin's structured concurrency): the scope ends only once all of them have, and where one fails, the scope cancels the rest and throws that failure where it ends, so a try around it takes it.

val both = taskScope {
  val page = task { fetch(pageAddress) }
  val picture = task { fetch(pictureAddress) }
  page.get() + picture.get()
}

task { } and thread { } in the scope's block start its own; anything else that gives a Job, a pool's submit { }, joins it with add. The checker counts what their blocks may throw as thrown by the scope, where it ends.

suspend inline fun <T> taskScope(block : TaskScope.() -> T) : T#

Runs block with a new scope, and gives what it gives, once every job of the scope has ended. Where a job fails, or the block throws, the scope cancels its other jobs (cancel(interrupt = true): a task stops where it next waits, a thread is interrupted), waits for them to end, and throws the first failure, any later ones set aside in it (suppressed). The block itself goes on until it ends. A cancelled job is no failure. Waiting, a task is set aside, a thread blocks, and the main thread runs the event loop meanwhile, so that its tasks go on.

class TaskScope#

The jobs of a scope taskScope { } is running, and what has gone wrong with them.

fun <T> task(block : suspend () -> T) : Job<T>#

Starts a task of the scope, as task { } does. In a scope that is cancelling, nothing is started: the job is cancelled at once.

fun <T> thread(name : string? = null, daemon : bool = false, block : suspend () -> T) : Job<T>#

Starts a thread of the scope, as thread { } does; in a scope that is cancelling, none.

fun <T> add(job : Job<T>) : Job<T>#

Makes job, started some other way, one of the scope's: it is waited for, and cancelled with the rest, at once in a scope that is cancelling. Gives the job.

fun cancel()#

Cancels every job of the scope, which then throws CancellationException where it ends, unless a job failed.

val isCancelled : bool#

Whether the scope is cancelling its jobs: one failed, or cancel() was called.

suspend fun <T> suspendTask(block : ((T) -> unit) -> unit) : T#

Sets the running task aside until resume is called, and gives what it is called with:

val answer = suspendTask<string> { resume -> dialog.prompt(question) { resume(it) } }

resume.fail(e) makes it throw e instead. resume may be called from any thread, once, and the task goes on there and then. On a thread that is not running a task, this waits on that thread.

fun Function.fail(error : Throwable)#

resume.fail(e): the task suspendTask set aside goes on by throwing e.

suspend fun delay(time : Duration)#

Waits for the time given. In a task on the main thread the task is set aside meanwhile and the event loop goes on; anywhere else the thread sleeps.

tensor.ww

Tensors (M9/M10, question 16)

An array of any number of axes, for what lists and grids do not cover: a volume, a picture's colour channels as an axis of their own, the frames of an animation, a batch; parts worked on in place, arrays of one shape combined with another, totals along an axis, matrices. Its T is int, float, a narrow width (int8 to uint32, float32, byte) or bool, its items one packed block.

val a = Tensor<float>(3, 4)                     3 rows of 4, all 0.0
val b = Tensor.arange(0, 12).reshaped(3, 4)      0 to 11 in 3 rows
val c = (b.toFloat() - 5.5) / 2.0                item by item; numbers stand for every item
val rows = b.sum(axis = 1)                       each row's total
val first = b[0, all]                            a view of row 0: changing it changes b
val m = Tensor.random(3, 3, seed = 7)
val x = m.solve(Tensor.ones<float>(3))           m.dot(x) is all ones

Where it differs from lists and grids: +, -, *, / and % work item by item, between tensors and with a number on the right (an int's overflow an ArithmeticException, the ...Wrapped forms wrapping); shapes are broadcast as numpy's are, matched from the last axis, an axis of 1 standing for any length; indexing with ranges gives a view of the same items, not a copy; and totals are taken over the whole or along one axis. An operation on two tensors gives a tensor of their item type: a tensor of ints with floats in it is made floats first, with toFloat().

val all : Range#

The whole of an axis, in a tensor's index: t[all, 0] is its first column.

class Tensor<T>#

An array of any number of axes, its items one packed block seen through a shape, as the notes above say.

var shape : List<int>#

The length of each axis.

fun <R> zeros(vararg shape : int) : Tensor<R>#

A tensor of shape, every item 0 (numpy's zeros).

fun <R> ones(vararg shape : int) : Tensor<R>#

A tensor of shape, every item 1 (numpy's ones): Tensor.ones<float>(3).

fun <R> full(shape : List<int>, value : R) : Tensor<R>#

A tensor of shape, every item value.

fun identity(n : int) : Tensor<float>#

The n by n identity matrix: 1.0 on its diagonal, 0.0 elsewhere.

fun arange(from : int, until : int, step : int = 1) : Tensor<int>#

The ints from from up to until, step apart: Tensor.arange(0, 5) is 0 to 4.

fun arange(from : float, until : float, step : float = 1.0) : Tensor<float>#

The floats from from up to until, step apart.

fun linspace(from : Number, to : Number, count : int) : Tensor<float>#

count floats evenly from from to to, both of them among them.

fun random(vararg shape : int, seed : int? = null) : Tensor<float>#

Floats from 0 up to 1 of shape, the same ones for the same seed, others each time when it is null.

fun <R> stack(arrays : List<Tensor<R>>, axis : int = 0) : Tensor<R>#

Tensors of one shape put together along a new axis, at axis.

fun <R> concat(arrays : List<Tensor<R>>, axis : int = 0) : Tensor<R>#

Tensors put together along an axis they have, their other axes the same.

fun <R> where(condition : Tensor<bool>, then : Tensor<R> | Number, otherwise : Tensor<R> | Number) : Tensor<R>#

Each item then's where condition is true, else otherwise's, the three broadcast; either may be a number.

val rank : int#

How many axes it has (numpy's ndim).

val size : int#

How many items it has.

op [](i : int) : T#

The items by index, worked out in place where the index fits; anything else goes through position(), which gives the error. A list for the index each time cost about 250 ns an item. The item at i of a tensor of one axis.

op [](i : int, j : int) : T#

The item at (i, j) of a tensor of two axes.

op [](i : int, j : int, k : int) : T#

The item at (i, j, k) of a tensor of three axes.

op [](i : int, j : int, k : int, l : int) : T#

The item at (i, j, k, l) of a tensor of four axes.

fun get(index : List<int>) : T#

The item at index, one int for each axis, whatever its rank.

op []=(i : int, value : T)#

Sets the item at i of a tensor of one axis.

op []=(i : int, j : int, value : T)#

Sets the item at (i, j).

op []=(i : int, j : int, k : int, value : T)#

Sets the item at (i, j, k).

op []=(i : int, j : int, k : int, l : int, value : T)#

Sets the item at (i, j, k, l).

fun set(index : List<int>, value : T)#

Sets the item at index, one int for each axis.

fun item() : T#

The one item of a tensor of one item: a dot product of two vectors, a total kept as a tensor.

op [](i : Range) : Tensor<T>#

A view: along each axis a range of it, the whole of it (all), or one place, which drops the axis; axes not given whole. Changing the view changes this tensor.

op [](i : Range | int, j : Range | int) : Tensor<T>#

A view of two axes' parts: t[0 ..< 10, all], t[all, 2].

op [](i : Range | int, j : Range | int, k : Range | int) : Tensor<T>#

A view of three axes' parts.

op [](i : Range | int, j : Range | int, k : Range | int, l : Range | int) : Tensor<T>#

A view of four axes' parts.

op [](mask : Tensor<bool>) : Tensor<T>#

The items where mask (of this tensor's shape) is true, in order, as a new tensor of one axis.

op []=(mask : Tensor<bool>, value : T)#

Sets the items where mask is true to value.

fun copy() : Tensor<T>#

A tensor of its own with these items.

fun fill(value : T)#

Every item set to value (through a view, every item of that part).

fun copyInto(destination : Tensor<T>)#

Its items written into destination, of the same shape.

fun reshaped(vararg shape : int) : Tensor<T>#

The same items in another shape, the same number of them (one axis given as -1 worked out): a view where the items lie in order, a copy otherwise.

fun flattened() : Tensor<T>#

The same items along one axis.

fun transposed(vararg axes : int) : Tensor<T>#

A view with its axes in the order axes gives, the last two swapped when none are given: the transpose of a matrix.

op +(other : Tensor<T> | Number) : Tensor<T>#

Item by item, broadcast: this plus other, a tensor or a number.

op -(other : Tensor<T> | Number) : Tensor<T>#

Item by item: this minus other.

op *(other : Tensor<T> | Number) : Tensor<T>#

Item by item: this times other.

op /(other : Tensor<T> | Number) : Tensor<T>#

Item by item: this divided by other; whole numbers for ints.

op %(other : Tensor<T> | Number) : Tensor<T>#

Item by item: what is left of this divided by other.

op -() : Tensor<T>#

Each item negated.

fun plusWrapped(other : Tensor<T> | Number) : Tensor<T>#

Item by item, this plus other, an int's overflow wrapping round as &+ does.

fun minusWrapped(other : Tensor<T> | Number) : Tensor<T>#

Item by item, this minus other, wrapping round as &- does.

fun timesWrapped(other : Tensor<T> | Number) : Tensor<T>#

Item by item, this times other, wrapping round as &* does.

fun lessThan(other : Tensor<T> | Number) : Tensor<bool>#

Item by item, whether this is less than other.

fun lessOrEqual(other : Tensor<T> | Number) : Tensor<bool>#

Item by item, whether this is less than or equal to other.

fun greaterThan(other : Tensor<T> | Number) : Tensor<bool>#

Item by item, whether this is greater than other.

fun greaterOrEqual(other : Tensor<T> | Number) : Tensor<bool>#

Item by item, whether this is greater than or equal to other.

fun equalTo(other : Tensor<T> | Number) : Tensor<bool>#

Item by item, whether this equals other.

fun notEqualTo(other : Tensor<T> | Number) : Tensor<bool>#

Item by item, whether this differs from other.

fun and(other : Tensor<bool>) : Tensor<bool>#

Of bools, item by item: both true.

fun or(other : Tensor<bool>) : Tensor<bool>#

Of bools, item by item: either true.

fun not() : Tensor<bool>#

Of bools, item by item: the other way.

fun abs(into : Tensor<T>? = null) : Tensor<T>#

Each item made positive.

fun sqrt(into : Tensor<float>? = null) : Tensor<float>#

Each item's square root, as floats.

fun exp(into : Tensor<float>? = null) : Tensor<float>#

e to the power of each item.

fun ln(into : Tensor<float>? = null) : Tensor<float>#

Each item's natural logarithm, as Math.ln.

fun log(base : Number, into : Tensor<float>? = null) : Tensor<float>#

Each item's logarithm in base, as Math.log.

fun log10(into : Tensor<float>? = null) : Tensor<float>#

Each item's logarithm in base 10.

fun pow(x : Number, into : Tensor<float>? = null) : Tensor<float>#

Each item to the power x.

fun sin(into : Tensor<float>? = null) : Tensor<float>#

Each item's sine, in radians.

fun cos(into : Tensor<float>? = null) : Tensor<float>#

Each item's cosine.

fun tan(into : Tensor<float>? = null) : Tensor<float>#

Each item's tangent.

fun floor(into : Tensor<T>? = null) : Tensor<T>#

Each item rounded down to a whole number.

fun ceil(into : Tensor<T>? = null) : Tensor<T>#

Each item rounded up to a whole number.

fun round(into : Tensor<T>? = null) : Tensor<T>#

Each item rounded to the nearest whole number, halves away from 0.

fun clamp(low : Number, high : Number, into : Tensor<T>? = null) : Tensor<T>#

Each item held from low to high.

fun toInt() : Tensor<int>#

As ints: a float cut to its whole part; a NaN, or a float too large for an int, an ArithmeticException.

fun toFloat() : Tensor<float>#

As floats.

fun toFloat32() : Tensor<float32>#

As float32s.

fun toByte() : Tensor<byte>#

As bytes: an ArithmeticException for an item outside 0 to 255.

fun toInt16() : Tensor<int16>#

As int16s, checked.

fun toInt32() : Tensor<int32>#

As int32s, checked.

fun toUint16() : Tensor<uint16>#

As uint16s, checked.

fun toUint32() : Tensor<uint32>#

As uint32s, checked.

fun toByteWrapped() : Tensor<byte>#

As bytes, each keeping its low 8 bits, as toByteWrapped() does.

fun toInt16Wrapped() : Tensor<int16>#

As int16s, each keeping its low 16 bits.

fun toInt32Wrapped() : Tensor<int32>#

As int32s, each keeping its low 32 bits.

fun toUint16Wrapped() : Tensor<uint16>#

As uint16s, each keeping its low 16 bits.

fun toUint32Wrapped() : Tensor<uint32>#

As uint32s, each keeping its low 32 bits.

fun sum() : Number#

The total of every item: an int for ints (an overflow an ArithmeticException), a float otherwise.

fun sum(axis : int, into : Tensor<T>? = null) : Tensor<T>#

The totals along axis: a tensor of one axis fewer.

fun average() : float#

The mean of every item, as a float; NaN for no items.

fun average(axis : int, into : Tensor<float>? = null) : Tensor<float>#

The means along axis.

fun min() : Number#

The least item; a NoSuchElementException for no items.

fun min(axis : int, into : Tensor<T>? = null) : Tensor<T>#

The least items along axis.

fun max() : Number#

The greatest item; a NoSuchElementException for no items.

fun max(axis : int, into : Tensor<T>? = null) : Tensor<T>#

The greatest items along axis.

fun product() : Number#

The product of every item.

fun product(axis : int, into : Tensor<T>? = null) : Tensor<T>#

The products along axis.

fun indexOfMin() : int#

Where the least item is, counted through the items in order (the last axis fastest), as Kotlin's lists have it; the first where several are least.

fun indexOfMin(axis : int, into : Tensor<int>? = null) : Tensor<int>#

Where the least item is along axis.

fun indexOfMax() : int#

Where the greatest item is, counted through the items in order.

fun indexOfMax(axis : int, into : Tensor<int>? = null) : Tensor<int>#

Where the greatest item is along axis.

fun any() : bool#

Of bools: whether any is true.

fun any(axis : int, into : Tensor<bool>? = null) : Tensor<bool>#

Of bools: whether any is true along axis.

fun all() : bool#

Of bools: whether all are true.

fun all(axis : int, into : Tensor<bool>? = null) : Tensor<bool>#

Of bools: whether all are true along axis.

fun count() : int#

Of bools: how many are true.

fun count(axis : int, into : Tensor<int>? = null) : Tensor<int>#

Of bools: how many are true along axis.

fun runningSum(axis : int? = null) : Tensor<T>#

The prefix sums: along axis, or of all the items in order as a tensor of one axis.

fun dot(other : Tensor<T>, into : Tensor<T>? = null) : Tensor<T>#

The matrix product, as numpy's dot: of two vectors a tensor of one item (item() gives it), of a matrix and a vector a vector, of two matrices a matrix. An IllegalArgumentException where the lengths summed over differ.

fun inverse() : Tensor<float>#

Of a square matrix: its inverse, as floats. An ArithmeticException for one that has none.

fun solve(b : Tensor<T>) : Tensor<float>#

Of a square matrix a: the x for which a.dot(x) is b (a vector, or a matrix of several), as floats. An ArithmeticException for a matrix that has no inverse.

fun determinant() : float#

Of a square matrix: its determinant.

fun toList() : open List<T>#

Its items in order (the last axis fastest), as a new list.

fun toGrid() : Grid<T>#

A tensor of two axes as a grid: its rows the grid's rows.

fun toImage() : Image#

A tensor of height by width by 3 or 4 numbers from 0 to 255 (red, green, blue, and alpha) as a picture.

op ==(other : any?) : bool#

Whether other is a tensor of the same shape and items.

fun hashCode() : int#

A hash code, the same for equal tensors.

override fun toString() : string#

Its items in nested brackets, as numpy shows them, the middle of a long axis left out: [[1, 2], [3, 4]].

A number on the left

op <T> Number.+(tensor : Tensor<T>) : Tensor<T>#

Item by item: the number plus each item (1 + tensor), as tensor + number is.

op <T> Number.-(tensor : Tensor<T>) : Tensor<T>#

Item by item: the number minus each item (1.0 - tensor).

op <T> Number.*(tensor : Tensor<T>) : Tensor<T>#

Item by item: the number times each item (2.0 * tensor).

op <T> Number./(tensor : Tensor<T>) : Tensor<T>#

Item by item: the number divided by each item (1.0 / tensor); whole numbers for ints.

op <T> Number.%(tensor : Tensor<T>) : Tensor<T>#

Item by item: what is left of the number divided by each item.

The list's items as a tensor of one axis, a copy.

The grid as a tensor of its rows by its columns, a copy.

The bytes as a tensor of bytes of one axis, a copy.

A picture's pixels as a tensor of bytes, height by width by 4: red, green, blue, alpha.

test.ww

Tests: what ww test runs

A function written @Test is a test: ww test file.ww runs it, and it passes unless it throws. Around each test run the functions written @BeforeTest and @AfterTest, and around all of a file's tests those written @BeforeAll and @AfterAll. A test may be a method of a class, which is made anew for each of its tests, with no arguments; its own @BeforeTest and @AfterTest methods run on that object, and its static @BeforeAll and @AfterAll methods once.

@Test fun adds() { assertEquals(4, 2 + 2) }
@Test @Timeout(500.milliseconds) fun quick() { ... }
@Test fun writes(@TempDir dir : FilePath) { FilePath(dir, "a.txt").writeText("hi") }

Each test runs in a sandbox of its own, made empty for it, with what runs around it and the object made for it: nothing it does reaches the system's files, programs or network, and Sandbox.current is its sandbox, for adding faults, programs and hosts. @BeforeAll and @AfterAll run in a sandbox of the file's, and the file's own code on the real system, as a program's does. @RealHost runs a test, or a class's tests, on the real system instead.

@Test @RealHost fun realNetwork() { ... }
@Test @Sandboxed(paths = PathStyle.windows) fun windowsPaths() { ... }
annotation Test#

Makes a function, or a method of a class, a test that ww test runs; it passes unless it throws. It takes no parameters but those written @TempDir.

annotation BeforeTest#

Makes a function one that runs before each test of the file, or a method one that runs before each test of its class, on the object made for it; where it throws, the test fails unrun.

annotation AfterTest#

Makes a function one that runs after each test of the file, or a method one that runs after each test of its class, on its object; it runs where the test failed too, and where it throws, the test fails.

annotation BeforeAll#

Makes a function one that runs once before all of the file's tests, or a static method one that runs once before its class's tests; where it throws, that is a failure and those tests do not run.

annotation AfterAll#

Makes a function one that runs once after all of the file's tests, or a static method one that runs once after its class's tests; where it throws, that is a failure.

annotation Timeout(val time : Duration)#

The longest the test may run, written as a constant (2.seconds, G52): once that is past it fails, stopped where it is, even in a wait.

annotation TempDir#

A test's parameter that is given a FilePath naming a new, empty directory of its own, deleted with what it holds once the test is over.

annotation RealHost#

Runs a test, or each test of a class with what runs around them, on the real system rather than in a sandbox: for what only the real system shows, such as a real network or programs.

annotation Sandboxed(val paths : PathStyle = PathStyle.linux, val fontsFrom : string = "")#

What the sandbox of a test, or of each test of a class, is to be like: its paths, and a folder of fonts, relative to the test's file, to be its installed fonts (Sandbox.run's fontsFrom); "" for the system's.

What a test checks, as Kotlin's: each throws an AssertionError where it does not hold

Each takes a message too, which when it is given begins the error's text.

fun fail(message : string? = null) : nothing#

Fails at once: throws an AssertionError with message, or with none.

fun assertEquals(expected : any?, actual : any?, message : string? = null)#

Checks that actual is equal to expected by ==, so that lists, sets and maps are compared by what they hold; the error shows both.

fun assertNotEquals(illegal : any?, actual : any?, message : string? = null)#

Checks that actual is not equal to illegal by ==.

fun assertEquals(expected : float, actual : float, absoluteTolerance : float, message : string? = null)#

A float near enough to what was expected.

fun assertSame(expected : any?, actual : any?, message : string? = null)#

Checks that actual and expected are the same by ===, not merely equal.

fun assertNotSame(illegal : any?, actual : any?, message : string? = null)#

Checks that actual and illegal are not the same by ===.

fun assertTrue(actual : bool, message : string? = null)#

Checks that actual is true.

fun assertFalse(actual : bool, message : string? = null)#

Checks that actual is false.

fun assertNull(actual : any?, message : string? = null)#

Checks that actual is null; the error shows what it was.

fun <T> assertNotNull(actual : T?, message : string? = null) : T#

Checks that actual is not null, and gives it back, typed as not null.

val user = assertNotNull(findUser("ann"))
fun <T> assertContains(items : List<T>, element : T, message : string? = null)#

Checks that items has an item equal to element.

fun assertContains(text : string, other : string, message : string? = null)#

Checks that text has other in it.

fun assertFails(block : () -> unit) : Throwable#

What the block throws, which it has to: an Exception or an Error.

fun <T> assertFailsWith(message : string? = null, block : () -> unit) : T#

What the block throws, which has to be a T.

text.ww

StringBuilder

class StringBuilder#

Text put together a piece at a time. Adding to a string with += copies the whole string each time, which is slow for a long text built in a loop; this keeps the pieces and joins them once.

val out = StringBuilder()
for line in lines { out.append(line).append("\n") }
print(out.toString())

buildString { append("a"); append(1) } does the same in one expression.

val size : int#

In characters.

val lastIndex : int#

The position of the last character: one less than size, and -1 when the builder is empty.

fun isEmpty() : bool#

Whether the builder has no text.

fun isNotEmpty() : bool#

Whether the builder has some text.

fun append(value : any?) : StringBuilder#

Adds a value as print would show it. Gives the builder, so that calls can be chained.

fun appendLine(value : any? = "") : StringBuilder#

Adds a value as print would show it, then a line break; with no value, only the line break. Gives the builder.

fun toString() : string#

The text put together so far.

fun clear() : StringBuilder#

Takes all the text out, leaving the builder empty. Gives the builder.

fun insert(index : int, value : any?) : StringBuilder#

Positions count characters, as in a string.

fun deleteRange(startIndex : int, endIndex : int) : StringBuilder#

Takes out the characters from startIndex up to, not including, endIndex; an endIndex past the end stops at the end. Gives the builder. A startIndex that is negative, past the end or after endIndex is an IndexOutOfBoundsException.

fun delete(startIndex : int, endIndex : int) : StringBuilder#

The same as deleteRange(startIndex, endIndex).

fun deleteAt(index : int) : StringBuilder#

Takes out the character at index. Gives the builder. An index that is negative, or not below the size, is an IndexOutOfBoundsException.

fun deleteCharAt(index : int) : StringBuilder#

Takes out the character at index; the same as deleteAt(index).

fun setLength(newLength : int)#

Cuts the text down to newLength characters; a longer length leaves it as it is.

fun reverse() : StringBuilder#

Turns the text back to front. Gives the builder.

fun get(index : int) : char#

The character at index, counted from 0; an IndexOutOfBoundsException when there is none.

op [](index : Index) : char#

The character at index, as on a string: sb[^1] counts from the end. An IndexOutOfBoundsException when there is none.

op [](range : Range) : string#

The part of the text in range, as a string, as on a string: sb[2..^1] is from the third character to the last.

fun indexOf(text : string, startIndex : int = 0) : int#

The position of the first text at or after startIndex, or -1 when it is not there.

fun lastIndexOf(text : string) : int#

The position of the last text, or -1 when it is not there.

fun contains(text : string) : bool#

Whether text is in the text so far.

fun startsWith(prefix : string) : bool#

Whether the text so far starts with prefix.

fun endsWith(suffix : string) : bool#

Whether the text so far ends with suffix.

thread.ww

Threads

Threads share everything: a variable or an object is the same one on every thread. One thread runs WinterWolf code at a time, and they change places at calls and at the ends of loop bodies, so anything longer than that which must not be interrupted holds a Lock's key. They are for blocking work and ported code; workers and parallel sections use the cores.

val loading = thread(name = "loader") { load(file) }    // a Job of what the block gives
loading.then { shown = it }                              // on the event loop, when it is done

A program ends when its script has finished, every thread that is not a daemon has too, and the event loop has nothing left.

class Thread#

A thread of its own, running task or, in a class that extends Thread, its own run(), once start() is called. thread { } is the shorter way, giving a Job of what the block returns.

var made#

How many threads have been given no name of their own: the number in the name Thread-N of the last. The main thread, and one given a name, are not counted.

fun currentThread() : Thread#

The thread running this code. On the main thread, a Thread named "main", made the first time it is asked for.

suspend fun sleep(time : Duration)#

Waits for the time given: InterruptedException if the thread is interrupted meanwhile.

fun interrupted() : bool#

Whether the running thread has been interrupted. Asking clears the mark.

fun yield()#

Lets another thread run, if one is waiting to.

var name : string#

Its name: the one given, or Thread-N. The name it has at start() is the one the running thread is known by, as in the report of an error it does not catch.

var isDaemon#

Whether the thread is a daemon, which does not keep the program alive: false unless set before start().

suspend fun run()#

What the thread does.

fun start() : Thread#

Starts the thread running run(), and gives the thread itself. Starting it a second time is an IllegalStateException.

suspend fun join(timeout : Duration? = null) : bool#

Waits for the thread to finish, timeout at most if that is given: whether it finished.

val isAlive : bool#

Whether the thread has started and not yet ended. The main thread's is true.

fun interrupt()#

Marks the thread as interrupted, and wakes it if it is waiting: the wait then throws InterruptedException. A thread that is working sees the mark only by asking for it.

val isInterrupted : bool#

Whether the thread has been interrupted and has not yet seen it: asking here does not clear the mark. False before it starts and after it ends.

override fun toString() : string#

The thread written with its name: Thread(main).

fun <T> thread(name : string? = null, daemon : bool = false, block : suspend () -> T throws Exception) : Job<T>#

Starts a thread running the block and gives a Job of what it returns, as task { } and a pool's submit { } do: then { } runs on the event loop, get() waits, cancel(interrupt = true) interrupts the thread. An error the block throws fails the job and ends the thread; it is the job's, as a task's is: an error nobody caught only if nothing asks for it (8.7a), and then reported with where it was thrown.

Jobs

A result to come: from task { }, thread { }, a pool's submit { }, or made by hand, Job<int>(), and given its value with complete(42) or its error with fail(e).

class Job<T>#

A result of type T to come, finished once: with a value, with an error, or cancelled. Threads and tasks may wait for it with get(); then { } and onError { } run on the event loop.

val isDone : bool#

Whether it has finished: with a value, with an error, or cancelled.

val isCancelled : bool#

Whether it was cancelled.

val failed : bool#

Whether it failed, or was cancelled.

fun complete(value : T) : bool#

Each gives whether this is what finished it: false if it was finished already.

fun fail(error : Throwable) : bool#

Finishes it with error: get() then throws ExecutionException with error as its cause. False if it was finished already.

fun cancel(interrupt : bool = false) : bool#

With interrupt, the thread working on it is interrupted as well.

fun getOrNull() : T?#

Its value if it has one; null if it has not, or failed.

suspend fun get() : T throws ExecutionException#

Its value, waited for if it is not done: a thread blocks, a task on the main thread is set aside, and the main thread outside a task runs the event loop meanwhile, so that the tasks and timers the job may be waiting on go on. A job that failed throws ExecutionException, its error in cause; one that was cancelled throws CancellationException.

suspend fun get(timeout : Duration) : T throws ExecutionException | TimeoutException#

The same, waiting timeout at most: TimeoutException after that.

fun then(action : (T) -> unit) : Job<T>#

Runs action with its value once it is done, on the event loop: normally the main thread, so a window may be changed there. Not if it fails; then onError's runs.

fun onError(action : (Throwable) -> unit) : Job<T>#

Runs action with its error if it fails or is cancelled, on the event loop.

override fun toString() : string#

Job(running), Job(42) with its value, Job(failed: <the error>) or Job(cancelled).

fun <T> all(jobs : List<Job<T>>) : Job<List<T>>#

A job of every job's value, in order, once all are done; it fails with the first error.

fun <T> any(jobs : List<Job<T>>) : Job<T>#

A job of the value of the first job to succeed; it fails with the last error if all fail.

Locks

One key: whoever has it opens the lock, everyone else waits until it is given back.

val drawing = Lock()
drawing.useKey { paint() }

val counts = LockBox<open Map<string, int>>({:})    // reached only with the key
counts.useKey { it["clicks"] = (it["clicks"] ?: 0) + 1 }

Inside either, waitUntil { condition } gives the key back until the condition holds, checked again whenever another thread gives a key back: Java's wait and notify, with no notify to forget. A thread that has the key may take it again. Nothing waits in a parallel section.

class Lock#

A lock with one key: useKey takes it, waiting while another thread holds it. A thread that holds the key may take it again.

inline fun <T> useKey(block : () -> T) : T#

Runs block holding the key, waiting for it if another thread holds it, and gives it back when the block ends, even by an error: gives what the block gives. Waiting threads and tasks look at their conditions again then. In a parallel section it is an IllegalStateException.

class LockBox<T>(private val value : T)#

Data reached only with the key: useKey gives the block the value.

inline fun <R> useKey(block : (T) -> R) : R#

Runs block with the value, holding the box's key: gives what the block gives.

suspend fun waitUntil(timeout : Duration? = null, condition : () -> bool) : bool#

Inside useKey: gives the key back until condition holds, checked again each time a key is given back, then goes on with it held. With a time limit, false if it ran out first.

Channels

Items handed from one thread or task to another, in order, each to one receiver:

val frames = Channel<Frame>(capacity = 2)    // a send waits while it holds 2
val jobs = Channel<Job>.unbounded()          // a send never waits
val handoff = Channel<int>(capacity = 0)     // each send waits for a receive

receive() waits for an item, and a for over a channel takes its items until it is closed and empty. Waiting blocks a thread, and sets a task on the main thread aside.

class Channel<T>#

Items handed from one thread or task to another, in order, each to one receiver: made with a capacity, Channel<T>(2), or with none, Channel<T>.unbounded().

fun <T> unbounded() : Channel<T>#

A channel that holds any number of items: a send never waits.

suspend fun send(item : T)#

Hands over an item, waiting while the channel is full; with capacity 0, until it is received. Sending to a closed channel is an IllegalStateException.

fun trySend(item : T) : bool#

Hands over an item if that can be done without waiting: whether it was.

suspend fun receive() : T#

Takes the next item, waiting for one; a closed channel that is empty throws IllegalStateException.

suspend fun receive(timeout : Duration) : T?#

The same, waiting timeout at most: null if nothing came.

fun tryReceive() : T?#

Takes the next item if there is one: null if there is not.

fun close()#

Takes no more items: what it holds can still be received, then a receive throws, and a for over it ends.

val isClosed : bool#

Whether close() has been called; it may still hold items to receive.

fun iterator() : __ChannelItems<T>#

For a for: each item as it comes, waited for, until the channel is closed and empty.

Thread pools

Threads that run the jobs given to them, each submit { } giving a Job of the job's result:

val pool = ThreadPool(threads = 4)
val total = pool.submit { sum(numbers) }
pool.every(5.seconds) { autosave() }
pool.close()                     // its threads keep the program alive until then

Threads take turns, so a pool overlaps waiting, not computing: tasks are the first choice for plain waiting, and parallel sections and workers for computing.

class ThreadPool : AutoCloseable#

Threads that run the jobs given to them, submit { } giving a Job of what a job returns. Close it when it is done with: until then its threads may keep the program alive, unless it is made with daemon = true.

var made#

How many pools have been made, JobQueues among them: the number in the name pool-N.

fun <T> submit(job : suspend () -> T throws Exception) : Job<T>#

Runs the job on one of the pool's threads: a Job of what it returns.

fun <T> after(delay : Duration, job : suspend () -> T throws Exception) : Job<T>#

Runs the job once, delay from now.

fun every(period : Duration, job : suspend () -> unit throws Exception) : Job<unit>#

Runs the job every period, the first time a period from now, until the Job it gives is cancelled or the pool is closed. An error the job throws stops it, failing the Job.

override fun close()#

Takes no more jobs, and waits for those it has to finish; timed jobs not yet due are dropped.

fun closeNow()#

Takes no more jobs, cancels those not started, and interrupts the threads running the others.

val isClosed : bool#

Whether close() or closeNow() has been called: it takes no more jobs.

class JobQueue(name : string? = null, daemon : bool = false) : ThreadPool(1, name, daemon)#

One thread running its jobs one at a time, in the order they were given.

Countdown

Opens once countDown() has been called count times; await() waits for that.

val done = Countdown(3)
repeat(3) { thread { work(); done.countDown() } }
done.await(timeout = 10.seconds)
class Countdown(private var remaining : int)#

Opens once countDown() has been called remaining times, starting at 0 or more: IllegalArgumentException if remaining is negative.

val count : int#

How many countDown() calls are still to come.

fun countDown()#

Counts one down, waking those that await() once it reaches 0; at 0 it does nothing.

suspend fun await(timeout : Duration? = null) : bool#

Waits until it reaches 0: a thread blocks, a task on the main thread is set aside. With a time limit, false if it ran out first.

Atomic values

A value threads and the cores of a parallel section change in one step each, with the processor's own operations underneath:

val hits = Atomic(0)
items.parallelStream().forEach { if (matches(it)) { hits.increment() } }

In a parallel section only values are stored in one: numbers, bools, small values and frozen data, never an object a core has just made. update { } may run its lambda more than once when cores contend, so the lambda only works out the new value from the old. For totals over all items, count { }, sumOf { } and gather share nothing and are faster still.

class Atomic<T>(initial : T)#

A value threads and the cores of a parallel section change in one step each, initial to begin with. In a parallel section only values are stored in one: storing an object, there, that a core may still change is an IllegalStateException.

fun get() : T#

The value it holds now.

fun set(value : T)#

Sets the value it holds.

fun exchange(value : T) : T#

Sets it, giving the value it held.

fun compareAndSet(expected : T, value : T) : bool#

Sets it to value if it holds expected (a number or a bool equal to it, or the very same object): whether it did.

fun update(change : (T) -> T) : T#

Works out the new value from the old and sets it, in one step: giving the new value.

override fun toString() : string#

The value it holds now, as text.

fun Atomic<int>.increment() : int#

On an Atomic<int>, each giving the new value.

fun Atomic<int>.decrement() : int#

Takes 1 off, giving the new value.

fun Atomic<int>.add(amount : int) : int#

Adds amount, giving the new value: an ArithmeticException if the total does not fit in an int, and nothing is changed.

time.ww

Durations

A length of time, kept as a whole number of nanoseconds in a value of its own, free to make:

delay(16.milliseconds)
job.get(timeout = 2.seconds)
val frame = 1.seconds / 60          // 16.666666ms

Every call that waits or times takes one: delay, Thread.sleep, join, Job.get, a channel's receive, Countdown.await, waitUntil, EventLoop.after and every, a pool's after and every.

value Duration(internal val seconds : int, internal val nanos : int)#

A length of time, positive, zero or negative; usually made as 5.seconds, 16.milliseconds and the like. Kept as Java's Duration keeps it, whole seconds (rounded down) and the nanoseconds past them, so it reaches as far as an int of seconds, about 292 billion years either way; a length past that is an ArithmeticException. Duration(seconds, nanoseconds) makes one from the two, the nanoseconds 0 to 999999999; Duration(n) one n nanoseconds long.

op +(other : Duration) : Duration#

The two lengths added together.

op -(other : Duration) : Duration#

This length with the other taken off.

op -() : Duration#

The same length the other way: -(2.seconds) is -2s.

op *(times : int) : Duration#

This length times over.

op *(times : float) : Duration#

This length times over, rounded to the nearest nanosecond.

op /(by : int) : Duration#

This length in by parts, what is less than a nanosecond left out: 1.seconds / 3 is 333.333333ms. By 0 is an ArithmeticException.

op /(by : float) : Duration#

This length in by parts, rounded to the nearest nanosecond. By 0.0 is an ArithmeticException.

op /(other : Duration) : float#

How many times the other fits in this one.

op <(other : Duration) : bool#

Whether this length is shorter than the other.

op <=(other : Duration) : bool#

Whether this length is shorter than the other or as long.

op >(other : Duration) : bool#

Whether this length is longer than the other.

op >=(other : Duration) : bool#

Whether this length is longer than the other or as long.

fun compareTo(other : Duration) : int#

-1, 0 or 1 as this length is shorter than the other, as long, or longer.

val inNanoseconds : int#

The length in nanoseconds: an ArithmeticException past what an int holds, about 292 years either way.

val inMicroseconds : float#

The length in each unit, with what is less than a whole one as a fraction.

val inMilliseconds : float#

The length in milliseconds, with what is less than a whole one as a fraction.

val inSeconds : float#

The length in seconds, with what is less than a whole one as a fraction.

val inMinutes : float#

The length in minutes, with what is less than a whole one as a fraction: 90.seconds is 1.5.

val inHours : float#

The length in hours, with what is less than a whole one as a fraction.

val inDays : float#

The length in days, with what is less than a whole one as a fraction.

val inWholeMilliseconds : int#

In whole milliseconds, what is less than one left out.

val isNegative : bool#

Whether it is less than no time.

val isZero : bool#

Whether it is no time at all.

fun toString() : string#

As Kotlin writes it: 1.7s, 16ms, 250us, 1m 30s, 2h 5m, 3d.

val zero#

No time at all: 0s.

val int.nanoseconds : Duration#

This many nanoseconds, as a Duration.

val int.microseconds : Duration#

This many microseconds, as a Duration.

val int.milliseconds : Duration#

This many milliseconds, as a Duration.

val int.seconds : Duration#

This many seconds, as a Duration.

val int.minutes : Duration#

This many minutes, as a Duration.

val int.hours : Duration#

This many hours, as a Duration.

val int.days : Duration#

This many days, as a Duration.

val float.nanoseconds : Duration#

This many nanoseconds, rounded to the nearest one, as a Duration: 2.6.nanoseconds is 3ns.

val float.microseconds : Duration#

This many microseconds, rounded to the nearest nanosecond, as a Duration.

val float.milliseconds : Duration#

This many milliseconds, rounded to the nearest nanosecond, as a Duration: 1.5.milliseconds.

val float.seconds : Duration#

This many seconds, rounded to the nearest nanosecond, as a Duration: 0.25.seconds is 250ms.

val float.minutes : Duration#

This many minutes, rounded to the nearest nanosecond, as a Duration.

val float.hours : Duration#

This many hours, rounded to the nearest nanosecond, as a Duration.

val float.days : Duration#

This many days, rounded to the nearest nanosecond, as a Duration.

Moments in time

A moment, kept as Java's Instant keeps it: the whole seconds since the start of 1970 in UTC (rounded down; leap seconds left out, as Java and Kotlin count them) and the nanoseconds past that second, in a value of its own:

val started = Instant.now()
val took : Duration = Instant.now() - started
print(path.lastModified())                  // 2026-11-02T14:05:31.250Z

It reaches as Java's does, from the year -1000000000 to 1000000000; past that is an ArithmeticException. Dates in a calendar and time zones are not part of it.

value Instant(val epochSeconds : int, val nanosecondOfSecond : int)#

The moment epochSeconds seconds and nanosecondOfSecond nanoseconds (0 to 999999999) after the start of 1970, UTC (before it where negative); Instant(n) is the moment n nanoseconds after.

op +(time : Duration) : Instant#

The moment a Duration later, or earlier for a negative one.

op -(time : Duration) : Instant#

The moment a Duration earlier.

op -(other : Instant) : Duration#

How long after other this is: negative where it is before.

op <(other : Instant) : bool#

Whether this is before other.

op <=(other : Instant) : bool#

Whether this is before other or the same moment.

op >(other : Instant) : bool#

Whether this is after other.

op >=(other : Instant) : bool#

Whether this is after other or the same moment.

fun compareTo(other : Instant) : int#

Negative, zero or positive as this is before, at or after other.

val epochNanoseconds : int#

The nanoseconds since the start of 1970: an ArithmeticException past what an int holds, before 1677 or after 2262.

val epochMilliseconds : int#

The whole milliseconds since the start of 1970, rounded down, as Java's toEpochMilli (an ArithmeticException past what an int holds, 292 million years either way).

fun toString() : string#

The moment in ISO 8601, in UTC: 2026-11-02T14:05:31Z, with the fraction of a second where there is one, in 3, 6 or 9 digits as it needs, as Java writes it (2026-11-02T14:05:31.250Z). A year past 9999 has a + in front (+10000-01-01T00:00:00Z), one before 1 a - (-0001 is the year before 0000).

fun now() : Instant#

The moment it is now, by the system's clock (which may be set back or forward; to time something, clock() and Durations go only forward).

fun fromEpochMilliseconds(milliseconds : int) : Instant#

The moment milliseconds after the start of 1970, as Java's ofEpochMilli.

fun fromEpochSeconds(seconds : int, nanoseconds : int = 0) : Instant#

The moment seconds and nanoseconds after the start of 1970 (any number of nanoseconds, before or after, as Java's ofEpochSecond).

fun parse(text : string) : Instant#

The moment ISO 8601 text gives, as toString writes it: a date, a time to the second with up to nine digits of fraction, and Z or an offset from UTC (+01:00). The year is four digits, or more after a + (+10000), or four or more after a - (-0001), as Java reads it. An IllegalArgumentException for anything else.

values.ww

A map's values

What map.values gives: the map's values, in the order of its keys, as the map holds them now. It is a live view: it keeps nothing of its own, and reading it reads the map, so it shows every change made to the map since. Nothing is changed through it, and its values have no positions: values[i] is values.toList()[i], a list of them as they are. A value that two keys share is there twice.

val prices = {"tea": 3, "cake": 4}
val all = prices.values           // [3, 4]
prices["jam"] = 2                 // all is [3, 4, 2] now
all.sum()   all.sortedBy { -it }   for price in prices.values { ... }
val Values.size : int#

The number of values, as the map's size.

val Values.itemType : Class#

The class of value the map was made to hold: int# for a Map<string, int>'s.

fun <T> Values<T>.isEmpty() : bool#

True if the map has no values.

fun <T> Values<T>.isNotEmpty() : bool#

True if the map has any values.

fun <T> Values<T>.contains(item : T) : bool#

True if item is one of the values (==); each is looked at in turn.

fun <T> Values<T>.toList() : open List<T>#

The values, in order, as a new list, which does not change with the map.

fun <T> Values<T>.first() : T#

The first value; a NoSuchElementException when the map is empty.

fun <T> Values<T>.last() : T#

The last value; a NoSuchElementException when the map is empty.

fun <T> Values<T>.toOpenList() : open List<T>#

The values, in order, as a new list; the same as toList().

fun <T> Values<T>.toSet() : open Set<T>#

The values, each once, as a new set.

fun <T> Values<T>.firstOrNull() : T?#

The first value, or null when the map is empty.

fun <T> Values<T>.lastOrNull() : T?#

The last value, or null when the map is empty.

inline fun <T> Values<T>.firstOrNull(predicate : (T) -> bool) : T?#

The first value for which predicate is true, or null when there is none.

inline fun <T> Values<T>.first(predicate : (T) -> bool) : T#

The first value for which predicate is true; a NoSuchElementException when there is none.

inline fun <T> Values<T>.find(predicate : (T) -> bool) : T?#

The first value for which predicate is true, or null when there is none; as firstOrNull.

inline fun <T> Values<T>.forEach(action : (T) -> unit)#

Calls action with each value, in order.

inline fun <T, R> Values<T>.map(transform : (T) -> R) : open List<R>#

What transform gives for each value, in order, as a new list.

inline fun <T, R> Values<T>.mapNotNull(transform : (T) -> R?) : open List<R>#

What transform gives for each value where that is not null, in order, as a new list.

inline fun <T, R> Values<T>.flatMap(transform : (T) -> List<R>) : open List<R>#

The items of the lists transform gives for each value, one list after another, as a new list.

inline fun <T> Values<T>.filter(predicate : (T) -> bool) : open List<T>#

The values for which predicate is true, in their order, as a new list.

inline fun <T> Values<T>.filterNot(predicate : (T) -> bool) : open List<T>#

The values for which predicate is false, in their order, as a new list.

fun <T> Values<T>.any() : bool#

True if the map has any values.

inline fun <T> Values<T>.any(predicate : (T) -> bool) : bool#

True if predicate is true for any value; it stops at the first for which it is.

inline fun <T> Values<T>.all(predicate : (T) -> bool) : bool#

True if predicate is true for every value, stopping at the first for which it is not; true for an empty map.

fun <T> Values<T>.none() : bool#

True if the map is empty.

inline fun <T> Values<T>.none(predicate : (T) -> bool) : bool#

True if predicate is true for no value; true for an empty map.

fun <T> Values<T>.count() : int#

The number of values, as size.

inline fun <T> Values<T>.count(predicate : (T) -> bool) : int#

The number of values for which predicate is true.

inline fun <T, R> Values<T>.fold(initial : R, operation : (R, T) -> R) : R#

initial combined with each value in turn by operation(soFar, value); initial for an empty map.

fun <T> Values<T>.sum() : any?#

The sum of the values, which are numbers: an int for ints, a float for floats; 0 for an empty map.

inline fun <T, R : Number> Values<T>.sumOf(selector : (T) -> R) : R#

The sum of what selector gives for each value; 0 (or 0.0) for an empty map.

fun <T> Values<T>.average() : float#

The mean of the values, which are numbers; NaN for an empty map.

fun <T> Values<T>.max() : T#

The largest value; a NoSuchElementException when the map is empty.

fun <T> Values<T>.min() : T#

The smallest value; a NoSuchElementException when the map is empty.

fun <T> Values<T>.maxOrNull() : T?#

The largest value, or null when the map is empty.

The smallest value, or null when the map is empty.

inline fun <T, R> Values<T>.maxOf(selector : (T) -> R) : R#

inline fun <T> Values<T>.minByOrNull(selector : (T) -> any?) : T?#

The value for which selector gives the least, the first of those that tie; null when the map is empty.

The values in order, smallest first, as a new list.

The values in order, largest first, as a new list.

The values ordered by what selector gives for each, smallest first, as a new list; those that tie keep their order.

fun <T> Values<T>.sortedByDescending(selector : (T) -> any?) : open List<T>#

The values ordered by what selector gives for each, largest first, as a new list; those that tie keep their order.
