The values ordered by comparator (negative: the first comes first), as a new list.

fun <T> Values<T>.distinct() : open List<T>#

The values, each once, in the order first seen, as a new list.

inline fun <T, K> Values<T>.groupBy(keySelector : (T) -> K) : open Map<K, open List<T>>#

The values grouped by what keySelector gives for each: a map from each key to a list of the values that gave it, in order.

inline fun <T> Values<T>.partition(predicate : (T) -> bool) : Pair<List<T>, List<T>>#

The values for which predicate is true, and those for which it is false, as two lists.

fun <T> Values<T>.join(separator : string = ", ", prefix : string = "", postfix : string = "", limit : int = -1, truncated : string = "...", transform : ((T) -> any?)? = null) : string#

The values as text, each made text with transform (or as it is), between separators.

fun <T> Values<T>.single() : T#

The only value: a NoSuchElementException when the map is empty, an IllegalArgumentException when it has more than one.

fun <T> Values<T>.singleOrNull() : T?#

The only value, or null when the map is empty or has more than one.

webp.ww

WebP

worker.ww

Workers

class Worker#

A worker is a second copy of the runtime, on a thread of its own, running a script of its own. Unlike a thread it runs at the same time as the program that started it, on another core: that is what it is for. The price is that the two share nothing, not a variable and not an object. They exchange messages, and a message is copied.

In the program:

val worker = Worker("primes.ww")         // the script, next to this file
worker.onMessage = { result -> print("the worker found", result) }
worker.post({"upTo": 100000})

In primes.ww, where Worker.parent is the program that started it:

Worker.parent.onMessage = { job -> Worker.parent.post(countPrimes(job["upTo"])) }

A message can hold numbers, strings, booleans, null, bytes, and lists and maps of those. Frozen data in one is shared rather than copied, and bytes can be handed over. Messages arrive on the event loop of the side they are sent to, one at a time.

val parent#

In a worker: the program that started it. Null in a program that is not a worker.

fun sharedHoldsForTest(data : any?) : int#

For tests: how many hold the block frozen data is shared as, this program and the messages carrying it among them; 0 for data never sent.

var onMessage : ((any?) -> unit)?#

Called with each message the worker sends, on this program's event loop.

var onError : ((any?) -> unit)?#

Called with a description of an error that ended the worker. Without one, it is reported as an uncaught error is.

val path : string#

The script it runs, as the call named it.

fun post(message : any?, handOver : bool = false) : bool#

Sends a copy of the message: false if the worker has ended. Frozen data (frozenList and the rest, frozen bytes) is not copied but shared, read in place by every worker it is sent to. With handOver, bytes are moved to the worker with no copy, leaving these empty.

fun terminate()#

Stops the worker, whatever it is doing: at its next call or loop round.

val isAlive : bool#

True until this program's event loop has heard that the worker ended, whether it finished, failed or was terminated; so it can stay true for a moment after the end.

class WorkerParent#

What a worker sees of the program that started it.

var onMessage : ((any?) -> unit)?#

Called with each message the program sends, on the worker's event loop.

fun post(message : any?, handOver : bool = false)#

Sends a copy of the message to the program, as Worker.post does.

fun close()#

Says that the worker is finished: it ends once what it is doing is done.

xml.ww

XML

object Xml#

XML read into a tree with nothing left out (M9/M10): SVG pictures, layout overrides, and any other file of tags.

val svg = Xml.parse(FilePath("logo.svg").readText()).root
for (shape in svg.elements("rect")) { print(shape.attr("width")) }

It reads XML 1.0 strictly: a document that is not well-formed is an XmlException giving the line and column. Everything in it is kept: the XML declaration, the DOCTYPE, comments, processing instructions and CDATA sections are each read into the tree, in their places. The predefined entities (&lt; &gt; &amp; &apos; &quot;) and numeric ones (&#169; &#x1F600;) are read, and so are the entities the DOCTYPE declares, as long as they hold text only and all of them together expand to no more than a million characters; an entity the DOCTYPE says is in another file is never read, and using one is an error. Names are kept as written, prefixes and all ("svg:rect", "xlink:href"): namespaces are not worked out.

fun parse(text : string) : XmlDocument throws XmlException#

XML text read as a document, with everything in it. An XmlException where the text is not well-formed XML.

fun scan(text : string, startIndex : int = 0) : List<int>#

Where the parts of an element are, in one pass in C++: the element whose start tag is at startIndex and all inside it, to its end tag. What Xml.parse builds its tree from, for a reader of one's own. Records one after another, each its kind and then its numbers, places counted in characters, an end left out:

1 a start tag:   1, where, name start, name end, name number, 1 if it ends with />, the
                 number of attributes, then for each: name start, name end, name number,
                 value start, value end, and 1 + 2 where the value holds & (1) or a line
                 end or tab (2)
2 an end tag:    2, where
3 text:          3, start, end, 1 where it holds &
4 a comment:     4, where, start, end (of what is between <!-- and -->)
5 an instruction: 5, where, target start, target end, data start, data end
6 CDATA:         6, where, start, end (of what is between <![CDATA[ and ]]>)
0 the end:       0, where it stopped

The scan takes only well-formed XML whose names are ASCII; it stops at the end of the element, or at anything else (something malformed, a name past ASCII, ]]> in text, an end tag of another element), which a reader then reads a part at a time. Entities are left as written; values and text keep their line ends. A name number is the same for each name spelt the same, element's or attribute's, 0 for the first met, 1 for the next new one and so on, so that a reader may make each name's string once.

Xml.scan("<a b='1'>hi</a>")   // [1, 0, 1, 2, 0, 0, 1, 3, 4, 1, 6, 7, 0, 3, 9, 11, 0, 2, 11, 0, 15]
fun attributes(text : string, records : List<int>, index : int, names : open List<string>) : Map<string, string>?#

The attributes of the start tag whose record is at index in records (from Xml.scan of text), as a map made in C++ in one call, a line end or tab in a value read as a space. names is a reader's list of the names by number, empty at first: the names not in it yet are added, the element's own included, so that each name's string is made once. Null where a value holds &, whose entities are left to the reader.

class XmlException(val description : string, val line : int, val column : int)#

What is wrong with XML that Xml.parse was given, and where: the line and the column in it, each counted from 1, the column in characters.

class XmlDocument#

A whole XML document: its declaration, and the DOCTYPE, comments and processing instructions around its root element.

val doc = Xml.parse(text)
print(doc.version, doc.encoding, doc.root.name)
val version : string?#

The version the XML declaration gives, "1.0"; null where there is no declaration.

val encoding : string?#

The encoding the XML declaration names, as written; null where it names none.

val standalone : bool?#

The XML declaration's standalone="yes" (true) or "no" (false); null where it says neither.

val doctype : XmlDoctype?#

The DOCTYPE; null where there is none.

val root : XmlElement#

The root element, with everything inside it.

val children : List<XmlNode>#

What is outside the XML declaration, in order: comments, processing instructions, the DOCTYPE and the root element.

override fun toString() : string#

The document written as XML: the declaration where it had one, then each of its children on a line of its own.

interface XmlNode#

A part of an XML document: an element (XmlElement), a run of text (XmlText, and XmlCData within it), a comment (XmlComment), a processing instruction (XmlInstruction) or the DOCTYPE (XmlDoctype).

val parent : XmlElement?#

The element it is in; null for the root element and what is outside it.

val text : string#

Its text: a run of text's own, a comment's, an instruction's data, or all the text inside an element (its CDATA sections included, its comments and instructions not); none for the DOCTYPE.

class XmlText : XmlNode#

A run of text in an element, its entities read: "a < b" for a &lt; b.

override val text : string#

The text.

override val parent : XmlElement?#

The element it is in.

override fun toString() : string#

The text as it is.

class XmlCData : XmlText#

A CDATA section: a run of text written as <![CDATA[ ... ]]>, where nothing is markup.

override fun toString() : string#

The section as written: <![CDATA[a < b]]>.

class XmlComment : XmlNode#

A comment: its text is what is between <!-- and -->.

override val text : string#

The comment's text, its spaces kept: " note " for <!-- note -->.

override val parent : XmlElement?#

The element it is in; null outside the root element.

override fun toString() : string#

The comment as written: <!-- note -->.

class XmlInstruction : XmlNode#

A processing instruction: <?xml-stylesheet href="style.css"?> is the target "xml-stylesheet" and the data "href=\"style.css\"".

val target : string#

The name it starts with.

val data : string#

What follows the target and its space, up to the ?>; empty where there is nothing.

override val parent : XmlElement?#

The element it is in; null outside the root element.

override val text : string#

The data.

override fun toString() : string#

The instruction as written: <?target data?>.

class XmlDoctype : XmlNode#

The DOCTYPE: the root element's name it gives, the DTD it names, and the declarations written inside it. A DTD in another file is never read.

val name : string#

The name of the root element it gives.

val publicId : string?#

The public identifier after PUBLIC; null where there is none.

val systemId : string?#

The address after SYSTEM, or after PUBLIC and its identifier; null where there is none.

val internalSubset : string?#

The declarations between [ and ] as written, their comments included; null where there is no [ ].

override val parent : XmlElement?#

Null: the DOCTYPE is outside the root element.

override val text : string#

Empty: the DOCTYPE holds no text.

override fun toString() : string#

The DOCTYPE as XML: <!DOCTYPE svg PUBLIC "id" "address" [declarations]>.

class XmlElement : XmlNode#

An element: its name, its attributes, and what it holds.

val rect = svg.elements("rect").first()
val fill = rect.attr("fill") ?: "black"
val name : string#

Its name as written, a prefix included: "rect", "svg:rect".

val attributes : Map<string, string>#

Its attributes by name, in the order they were written, their entities read.

override val parent : XmlElement?#

The element it is in; null for the root.

val children : List<XmlNode>#

What it holds, in order: its elements, runs of text, CDATA sections, comments and processing instructions.

fun attr(name : string) : string?#

The attribute of that name, or null where it has none.

fun elements(name : string? = null) : List<XmlElement>#

The elements it holds (not those inside them), only those named name where it is given.

override val text : string#

All the text inside it, its elements' and CDATA sections' included, in order; not its comments or processing instructions.

override fun toString() : string#

The element written as XML: <rect width="4"/>, with & < > (and " in an attribute) written as entities, and its CDATA sections, comments and processing instructions as they were.
